\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{K_{0}}_{{\rm ratio},\alpha,n}(\alpha;s_{0}):=\log\left(\widehat{p}^{K_{0}}_{1,n}(s_{0})/\widehat{p}^{K_{0}}_{0,n}(s_{0})\right)\pm c^{K_{0}}_{{\rm ratio},\alpha,n}(s_{0}),

(34)

where
c^{K_{0}}_{{\rm ratio},\alpha,n}(s_{0})
is the
1-\alpha
quantile of
\left|\hat{\alpha}^{K_{0}}_{0}/\sqrt{n(\Delta\tau^{K_{0}}_{0,n})}{\mathbb{L}}_{0k}^{(0)}-\hat{\alpha}^{K_{0}}_{1}/\sqrt{n(\Delta\tau^{K_{0}}_{1,n})}{\mathbb{L}}_{1k}^{(0)}\right|
, and
\hat{\alpha}^{K_{0}}_{a}:=\sqrt{\hat{\chi}^{K_{0}}_{\theta_{a}}}/\widehat{p}^{K_{0}}_{a,n}(s_{0})
, for
a\in\{0,1\}
.

To estimate
\chi_{\theta_{a}}
using sample splitting, we propose the estimator
\widehat{\chi}^{K_{0}}_{\theta_{a},n}
defined as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{1}{2h_{n}K_{0}}\sum_{k=1}^{K_{0}}\left[{\mathbb{P}}^{k}_{n}\left\{D_{a,{\widehat{\theta}_{a,-k}}}(s_{0}+h_{n})-D_{a,{\widehat{\theta}_{a,-k}}}(s_{0})\right\}^{2}\right.\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.+{\mathbb{P}}^{k}_{n}\left\{D_{a,{\widehat{\theta}_{a,-k}}}(s_{0}-h_{n})-D_{a,{\widehat{\theta}_{a,-k}}}(s_{0})\right\}^{2}\right],\end{split}

(35)

where
{\mathbb{P}}_{n}^{k}
is the empirical distribution of the data in the
k
-th fold
\mathcal{V}_{n,k}
, and
D_{a,{\widehat{\theta}_{a,-k}}}
is the estimated influence function for
\widehat{\theta}_{a,-k}=(\widehat{\phi}_{a,-k},\widehat{\pi}_{a,-k})
obtained from
\mathcal{T}_{n,k}
.
We again suggest
h_{n}=n^{-1/10}
as a tuning parameter. As in Lemma
4
, consistency of
\widehat{\chi}^{K_{0}}_{\theta_{a},n}
holds under the same conditions as Theorem
5
as long as
h_{n}^{-1}=O(\sqrt{n})
. Hence, this estimator does not require conditions controlling the complexity of the nuisance estimators.

The sample splitting estimator can be used to construct a uniform confidence band using the approach discussed in Section
5.3
.

Appendix B
Data analysis

We use our method to analyze the
lalonde
dataset from the R package
cobalt

(
Greifer, 2023
)
.
The data was used by
Dehejia and Wahba (1999)
to study the effectiveness of a job training program on earnings
(
LaLonde, 1986
)
.
The treatment
A
is an indicator of participation in the National Supported Work Demonstration job training program.
The data contains 185 people with
A=1
and a comparison sample of 429 people with
A=0
from the Population Survey of Income Dynamics.
The outcome
Y
is the real earnings in US dollars measured in 1978, several years after completion of the program. We scaled the outcome by
10^{4}
.
The covariates include demographic variables (age, race, education, and marital status), and two previous earnings levels measured in 1974 and 1975, which we also scaled by
10^{4}
.

(a)

95% CI’s

(b)

95% CI’s via sample splitting

(c)

95% CI’s via projection method

(d)

95% diference CI’s

(e)

95% difference CI’s via sample splitting

(f)

95% log-ratio CI’s

(g)

95% log-ratio CI’s via sample splitting

Figure 2:
(a–c): Density estimates and corresponding 95% pointwise CI’s for the Lalonde data. P1 and P0 stand for the counterfactual density estimates for each treatment and control group, respectively. (a) Our log-concave estimator. (b) Our estimator with five-fold sample splitting. (c) Basis expansion estimator
(
Kennedy et al., 2023
)
.
(d–e): 95% pointwise difference (P1
-
P0) CI’s for the Lalonde data. (d) Our log-concave estimator. (e) Our estimator with five-fold sample splitting.
(f–g): 95% pointwise log-ratio (
\log({\rm P1}/{\rm P0})
) CI’s for the Lalonde data. (f) Our log-concave estimator. (g) Our estimator with five-fold sample splitting.

We estimated the density of real earnings in 1978 for treatment and control using our proposed method adjusting for the covariates listed above.
We estimated the conditional distribution function using the location-scale estimator with a gamma distribution as in Lemma
6
.
We estimated the propensity score and the conditional mean of the outcome using random forests via the R package
ranger

(
Wright and Ziegler, 2017
)
.
For the conditional mean, we included interactions between the treatment and covariates as additional predictors in the random forest.
To estimate the conditional variance, we used another random forest with these same predictors and outcome
(Y_{i}-\hat{\mu}_{i})^{2}
, where
\hat{\mu}_{i}
is the fitted value of the conditional mean for the
i
th observation.
We considered other several other conditional distribution estimators, including location-scale estimators with exponential and uniform CDFs, as well as quantile regression random forest
(
Meinshausen and Ridgeway, 2006
;
Elie-Dit-Cosaque and Maume-Deschamps, 2022
)
. We did not find significant differences between the results, so we only present the results from the gamma conditional distribution function.
We also computed the log-concave estimator with sample splitting with
K_{0}=5
folds and the same nuisance estimators as above (see Section
A
).
We set the tuning parameter for estimating
\chi_{\theta_{a}}
to be
n^{-1/10}
.
For comparison, we estimated the counterfactual density and corresponding 95% CI’s using the basis expansion method
(
Kennedy et al., 2023
)
using the
cdensity
function in the R package
npcausal

(
Kennedy, 2023
)
.
We again used 5 fold sample splitting and random forests for the nuisance estimators.

Figure
2
displays the estimated densities.
The densities under treatment and control are similar in shape, but perhaps surprisingly there are regions where the CI’s in the upper and lower tails do not overlap.
By contrast, a 95% CI for the ATE (using the
ate
function in the

npcausal
package with default settings) was
[-0.10,0.56]
,
indicating there was not a statistically significant difference in the
counterfactual means. Our proposed log-concave estimator does not have the
same boundary issue that the kernel density estimator and/or the projection
method encounter on this dataset.

Figures
2(d)
–
2(e)
display the pointwise difference CI’s derived from our estimator, both with and without the application of sample splitting.
Figures
2(f)
–
2(g)
illustrate the pointwise density log-ratio CI’s derived from both of our estimators.
Both proposed methods reveal significant differences, specifically at the upper and lower extremes.
Therefore, if we have accounted for all sources of confounding and the true densities are log-concave, then there is evidence of an effect of treatment on the upper and lower tails of the distribution of income.

Appendix C
Empirical processes tools

Given classes
\mathcal{F}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{F}_{k}
of functions
\mathcal{F}_{i}:\mathcal{X}\mapsto\mathcal{R}
and a function
\varphi:\mathbb{R}^{k}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
, let
\varphi(\mathcal{F}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{F}_{k})
be the class of functions
x\mapsto\varphi(f_{1}(x),\dots,f_{k}(x))
, where
f_{i}\in\mathcal{F}_{i}
, for
i=1,\dots,k
. The following proposition is Theorem 3 in
van der Vaart and Wellner (2000)
.

Proposition 1
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{F}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{F}_{k}
are
P
-Glivenko-Cantelli classes of functions, and that
\varphi:\mathbb{R}^{k}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
is continuous. Then
\mathcal{H}:=\varphi(\mathcal{F}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{F}_{k})
is a
P
-Glivenko-Cantelli class given that it has an integrable envelope.

The following lemma that controls empirical process terms under sample splitting scheme and its proof were provided in Lemma C.3 of
Kim et al. (2018)
.

Lemma 5
.

(Sample-splitting). Let
{\mathbb{P}}_{n}
denote the empirical measure over a set
D_{1,n}=(Z_{1},\cdots,Z_{n})
, which is i.i.d. from
{\mathbb{P}}
. Let
\hat{f}
be a sample operator (e.g., estimator) constructed in a separate, independent sample set
D_{2,m}
with m observations. Then we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\Big(({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-{\mathbb{P}})\hat{f}\Big)^{2}\leq\frac{1}{n}{\mathbb{P}}\hat{f}^{2}

We state the following proposition that controls empirical processes indexed by classes of functions that change with
n
. It is Theorem 2.11.24 in
van der Vaart and Wellner (2023)
. For each
n
, suppose that a class of measurable functions
\mathcal{F}_{n}=\{f_{n,t}:t\in T\}
indexed by a totally bounded semimetric space
(T,\rho)
admits an envelope function (sequence)
F_{n}
. And, further suppose that the classes
\mathcal{F}_{n,\delta}=\{f_{n,s}-f_{n,t}:\rho(s,t)<\delta\}
and
\mathcal{F}^{2}_{n,\delta}
are measurable under the probability measure
P
.

Proposition 2
.

Suppose that the following holds,

\begin{split}&PF_{n}^{2}=O(1),\\
&PF_{n}^{2}I(F_{n}>\eta\sqrt{n})\rightarrow 0,\qquad{\rm for~every~}\eta>0,\\
\sup_{\rho(s,t)<\delta_{n}}&P(f_{n,s}-f_{n,t})^{2}\rightarrow 0,\qquad{\rm for~every~}\delta_{n}\downarrow 0,\\
\end{split}

(36)

and,

\displaystyle\sup_{Q}\int_{0}^{\delta_{n}}\sqrt{\log N\left(\epsilon\|F_{n}\|_{Q,2},\mathcal{F}_{n},L_{2}(Q)\right)}d\epsilon\rightarrow 0,\qquad{\rm for~every~}\delta_{n}\downarrow 0.

(37)

Then the sequence
\{\sqrt{n}({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P)f_{n,t}:t\in T\}
is asymptotically tight in
\ell^{\infty}(T)
. Moreover, given that the sequence of covariance functions
Pf_{n,s}f_{n,t}-Pf_{n,s}Pf_{n,t}
converges pointwise on
T\times T
, the sequence
\{\sqrt{n}({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P)f_{n,t}:t\in T\}
converges in distribution to a Gaussian process.

Here we state the following propositions that give bounds for each
L_{p}
-norm (resp.
L_{1}
-norm) of
{\mathbb{G}}_{n}=\sqrt{n}({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P)
for function classes
\mathcal{F}
that admit a finite uniform entropy (resp. bracketing) integral.

Proposition 3
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a
P
-measurable class of measurable functions with measurable envelope function
F
. Then,

\displaystyle\left\|\|{\mathbb{G}}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{F}}\right\|_{P,p}\lesssim J(1,\mathcal{F})\|F\|_{P,2\vee p},\qquad 1\leq p,

where the entropy integral
J(1,\mathcal{F})
is given by

\displaystyle J(1,\mathcal{F})=\sup_{Q}\int_{0}^{\delta}\sqrt{1+\log N\left(\epsilon\|F\|_{Q,2},\mathcal{F},L_{2}(Q)\right)}d\epsilon,

where the supremum is taken over all discrete probability measures
Q
with
\|F\|_{Q,2}>0
.

Proposition 4
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a
P
-measurable class of measurable functions with measurable envelope function
F
. Then,

\displaystyle\left\|\|{\mathbb{G}}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{F}}\right\|_{P,1}\lesssim J_{[]}(1,\mathcal{F},L_{2}(P))\|F\|_{P,2},

where the entropy integral
J_{[]}(\delta,\mathcal{F},L_{2}(P))
(for
\delta>0
) is given by

\displaystyle J_{[]}(\delta,\mathcal{F},L_{2}(P))=\int_{0}^{\delta}\sqrt{1+\log N_{[]}\left(\epsilon\|F\|_{P,2},\mathcal{F},L_{2}(P)\right)}d\epsilon.

Appendix D
Proofs of theorems, lemmas

D.1
Proof of Lemma
1

Fix
\varepsilon,\delta>0
. For any fixed
M>0
, we have

\displaystyle d_{1}\left(\widehat{\phi}_{a}(\cdot|\mathbf{X}),{\phi}_{a,\infty}(\cdot|\mathbf{X})\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\left|\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})-{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right|\,ds

\displaystyle=\int_{-M}^{M}\left|\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})-{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right|\,ds

\displaystyle\indent+\int_{(-\infty,-M]\cup[M,\infty)}\left|\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})-{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right|\,ds

\displaystyle\leq\int_{-M}^{M}\left|\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})-{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right|\,ds

\displaystyle\indent+\int_{-\infty}^{-M}\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})ds+\int_{-\infty}^{-M}\phi_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\,ds

\displaystyle\indent+\int_{M}^{\infty}\left[1-\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})\right]\,ds+\int_{M}^{\infty}\left[1-\phi_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right]\,ds.

Thus,

\displaystyle P_{*}\left\{d_{1}\left(\widehat{\phi}_{a}(\cdot|\mathbf{X}),{\phi}_{a,\infty}(\cdot|\mathbf{X})\right)\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq P_{*}\left\{\int_{-M}^{M}\left|\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})-{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right|ds\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle\indent+P_{*}\left\{\int_{-\infty}^{-M}\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})ds\right\}^{2}+P_{*}\left\{\int_{M}^{\infty}\left[1-\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})\right]\,ds\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle\indent+P_{*}\left\{\int_{-\infty}^{-M}\phi_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\,ds\right\}^{2}+P_{*}\left\{\int_{M}^{\infty}\left[1-\phi_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right]ds\right\}^{2}

Define

\displaystyle D_{n,M}^{(1)}

\displaystyle:=P_{*}\left\{\int_{-M}^{M}\left|\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})-{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right|ds\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle D_{n,M}^{(2)}

\displaystyle:=P_{*}\left\{\int_{-\infty}^{-M}\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})ds\right\}^{2}+P_{*}\left\{\int_{M}^{\infty}\left[1-\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})\right]\,ds\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle d_{M}^{(3)}

\displaystyle:=P_{*}\left\{\int_{-\infty}^{-M}\phi_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\,ds\right\}^{2}+P_{*}\left\{\int_{M}^{\infty}\left[1-\phi_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right]ds\right\}^{2}.

Then, our goal is to show that for all
\varepsilon,\delta>0
, there exists
N=N_{\varepsilon,\delta}
and
M=M_{\varepsilon,\delta}
such that for all
n\geq N
,

\displaystyle P_{*}\left(D_{n,M}^{(1)}>\delta\right)<\varepsilon,\quad P_{*}\left(D_{n,M}^{(2)}>\delta\right)<\varepsilon,\quad d_{M}^{(3)}<\delta.

(38)

Since
\int_{\mathbb{R}}|s|\,d\phi_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})<T(\mathbf{X})
and
T\in L_{2}(P_{*})
,
\int_{-\infty}^{-M}\phi_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\,ds
and
\int_{M}^{\infty}\left[1-\phi_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right]\,ds
both decrease to 0 as
M\to\infty
for
P_{*}
-a.e.
\mathbf{X}
. Therefore, by the monotone convergence theorem,

\displaystyle\lim_{M\rightarrow\infty}P_{*}\left\{\int_{-\infty}^{-M}\phi_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\,ds\right\}^{2}=0,{~\rm and~}\lim_{M\rightarrow\infty}P_{*}\left\{\int_{M}^{\infty}\left[1-\phi_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right]\,ds\right\}^{2}=0,

Hence, we have that for all
\delta>0
, there exists
M_{3}(\delta)
such that for all
M\geq M_{3}(\delta)
,
d_{M}^{(3)}<\delta
.

\displaystyle P_{*}\left\{\int_{-\infty}^{-M}\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})ds\right\}^{2}+P_{*}\left\{\int_{M}^{\infty}\left[1-\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})\right]ds\right\}^{2}\leq\mathcal{U}_{n}(M;\mathbf{Z}),

M_{2}(\varepsilon,\delta)
and
N_{2}(\varepsilon,\delta)
such that for all
M\geq M_{2}(\varepsilon,\delta)
and
n\geq N_{2}(\varepsilon,\delta)
,
P_{*}\left(\mathcal{U}_{n}(M;\mathbf{Z})>\delta\right)<\epsilon
.

Finally, by the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality,

\displaystyle P_{*}\left\{\int_{-M}^{M}\left|\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})-{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right|\,ds\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq P_{*}\left\{2M\int_{-M}^{M}\left|\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})-{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right|^{2}\,ds\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 2M\int_{-M}^{M}P_{*}\left|\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})-{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right|^{2}\,ds

\displaystyle\leq 4M^{2}\sup_{s\in[-M,M]}P_{*}\left|\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})-{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right|^{2}.

Since
\sup_{s\in[-M,M]}P_{*}\left|\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})-{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right|^{2}=o_{p}(1)
for any
M>0
by assumption, for all
\varepsilon,\delta,M>0
, there exists
N_{1}(\varepsilon,\delta,M)
such that for all
n\geq N_{1}(\varepsilon,\delta,M)
,
P_{*}\left(D_{n,M}^{(1)}>\delta\right)<\varepsilon
.

For any
\varepsilon,\delta>0
, we let
M_{\epsilon,\delta}:=\max\{M_{2}(\varepsilon,\delta),M_{3}(\delta)\}
and
N_{\epsilon,\delta}:=\max\{N_{1}(\varepsilon,\delta,M_{\epsilon,\delta}),N_{2}(\varepsilon,\delta)\}
. This implies that, for any
n\geq N_{\epsilon,\delta}
, (
38
) holds.
This completes the proof.

D.2
A semi-parametric class of functions
\mathcal{F}_{H}

Recall the class of functions
\mathcal{F}_{H}
defined in (
10
).
We provide a lemma under which
\mathcal{F}_{H}
satisfies conditions
(E1)
,
(E3)
,
(EC1)
, and
(EC2)
.

Lemma 6
.

Suppose
\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{x})=H((s-\hat{\mu}_{a}(\mathbf{x}))/\hat{\sigma}_{a}(\mathbf{x}))
for a fixed CDF
H
with mean 0 and variance 1 and estimators
\hat{\mu}_{a}
and
\hat{\sigma}_{a}
such that
\hat{\mu}_{a}\in\mathcal{F}_{1}
and
\hat{\sigma}_{a}\in\mathcal{F}_{2}
, where
\mathcal{F}_{1}
and
\mathcal{F}_{2}
are classes of measurable functions uniformly bounded by
K>0
, and
\mathcal{F}_{2}
is also uniformly bounded away from
0
by
1/K
, the function
L:\mathbb{R}\mapsto[0,\infty)
defined as

\displaystyle L(s):=\sup_{K(s-K)<s_{1}\neq s_{2}\leq K(s+K)}\frac{|H(s_{2})-H(s_{1})|}{|s_{2}-s_{1}|},

(39)

satisfies
\int_{\mathbb{R}}(|s|+1)L(s)ds<\infty
, and
\|\hat{\mu}_{a}-\mu_{a,\infty}\|\rightarrow_{p}0
and
\|\hat{\sigma}_{a}-\sigma_{a,\infty}\|\rightarrow_{p}0
.
Then conditions
(E1)
and
(E3)
are satisfied.

Assume that
\mathcal{F}_{1}
and
\mathcal{F}_{2}
satisfy the aforementioned conditions.
Suppose that
\widehat{\phi}_{a}\in\mathcal{F}_{H}
and
\mathcal{F}_{1}\times\mathcal{F}_{2}
is endowed with
L_{1}(P_{*})
distance, where

\displaystyle L_{1}((f_{1},g_{1}),(f_{2},g_{2}))(P_{*})=P_{*}\left[\left|f_{1}-f_{2}\right|+\left|g_{1}-g_{2}\right|\right].

Without loss of generality, assume that the support of
Y^{a}

(a\in\{0,1\})
is
\mathbb{R}
.
If the
L_{1}(P_{*})
bracketing number satisfies
N_{[]}(\varepsilon,\mathcal{F}_{1}\times\mathcal{F}_{2},L_{1}(P_{*}))<\infty
for every
\varepsilon>0
and
H^{-1}
is Lipschitz continuous on any compact interval contained in
(0,1)
, then condition
(EC1)
is satisfied. If in addition
J_{1}(t):=\int_{-\infty}^{t}H(s)ds
and
J_{2}(t)=\int_{t}^{\infty}(1-H(s))ds
satisfy that
J_{1}^{-1}
and
J_{2}^{-1}
are Lipschitz continuous on every compact interval
[t,J_{1}(0)]
and
[t,J_{2}(0)]
, respectively, for every
0<t
, then condition
(EC2)
is satisfied.

Proof.

First, we show
(E1)
, since
(E3)
is trivial.
Under the conditions in Lemma
6
, we have

\displaystyle P_{*}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|H\left(\frac{s-\hat{\mu}_{a}(\mathbf{X})}{\hat{\sigma}_{a}(\mathbf{X})}\right)-H\left(\frac{s-\mu_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}{\sigma_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\right)\right|ds

\displaystyle\indent\leq P_{*}\int_{\mathbb{R}}L(s)\left|\frac{s-\hat{\mu}_{a}(\mathbf{X})}{\hat{\sigma}_{a}(\mathbf{X})}-\frac{s-\mu_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}{\sigma_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\right|ds

\displaystyle\indent\leq P_{*}\int_{\mathbb{R}}L(s)\left|\frac{s(\hat{\sigma}_{a}(\mathbf{X})-\sigma_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X}))}{\hat{\sigma}_{a}(\mathbf{X})\sigma_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}+\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{a}(\mathbf{X})(\mu_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})-\hat{\mu}_{a}(\mathbf{X}))+\hat{\mu}_{a}(\mathbf{X})(\hat{\sigma}_{a}(\mathbf{X})-\sigma_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X}))}{\hat{\sigma}_{a}(\mathbf{X})\sigma_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\right|ds

\displaystyle\indent\leq P_{*}\int_{\mathbb{R}}L(s)\left[(K^{2}|s|+K^{3})|\hat{\sigma}_{a}(\mathbf{X})-\sigma_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})|+K|\hat{\mu}_{a}(\mathbf{X})-\mu_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})|\right]ds\rightarrow 0.

Now, we show
(EC1)
and
(EC2)
.
For each
\epsilon>0
, there exists
0<\epsilon_{0}<1
which satisfies
H(s)<\epsilon
when
s\leq K(H^{-1}(\epsilon_{0})+K)
, and
1-H(s)<\epsilon
when
s\geq K(H^{-1}(1-\epsilon_{0})-K)
.
Define
L:=\max_{s\in\mathbb{R}}L(s)
and
L_{\epsilon_{0}}
as a Lipschitz constant for
H^{-1}
on the interval
[\epsilon_{0},1-\epsilon_{0}]
.
We further let
K_{H,\epsilon_{0}}
as
\max\{|H^{-1}(\epsilon_{0})|,|H^{-1}(1-\epsilon_{0})|\}
.
By the bracketing number condition on the class
\mathcal{F}_{1}\times\mathcal{F}_{2}
, we have finite number of brackets
(f_{l,i},g_{l,i}),\,(f_{u,i},g_{u,i})
where
L_{1}((f_{l,i},g_{l,i}),(f_{u,i},g_{u,i}))(P_{*})\leq\epsilon/(2L(K^{3}+K_{H,\epsilon_{0}}))
for
1\leq i\leq N_{\epsilon}
where
N_{\epsilon}=N_{\epsilon,L,K,K_{H,\epsilon_{0}}}=N_{[]}(\epsilon/(2L(K^{3}+K_{H,\epsilon_{0}})),\mathcal{F}_{1}\times\mathcal{F}_{2},L_{1}(P_{*}))
.
Now we construct the brackets for the class
\mathcal{F}_{H}
at
H((s-f)/g)
by
H((H^{-1}(\epsilon_{j})-f_{u,i})/g_{u,i})
,
H((H^{-1}(\epsilon_{j+i})-f_{l,i})/g_{l,i})
, where
(f,g)
has lower and upper brackets
(f_{l,i},g_{l,i}),(f_{u,i},g_{u,i})
and
H^{-1}(\epsilon_{j})\leq s<H^{-1}(\epsilon_{j+1})
, and
0\leq(\epsilon_{j+1}-\epsilon_{j})\leq\epsilon/(2LL_{\epsilon_{0}}K)
for
1\leq i\leq N^{\prime}_{\epsilon}-1
,
\epsilon_{1}=\epsilon_{0},\,\epsilon_{N^{\prime}_{\epsilon}}=1-\epsilon_{0}
.
As in Section
D.2
, assuming that
K>1
without loss of generality, for
\epsilon_{0}\leq s\leq 1-\epsilon_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle P_{*}\left|H\left(\frac{H^{-1}(\epsilon_{j})-f_{u,i}}{g_{u,i}}\right)-H\left(\frac{H^{-1}(\epsilon_{j+1})-f_{l,i}}{g_{i,i}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\indent\leq L\left[P_{*}\left|\frac{g_{u,i}(f_{u,i}-f_{l,i})+f_{u,i}(g_{u,i}-g_{l,i})}{g_{u,i}g_{i,i}}\right|\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\left.+P_{*}\left|\frac{g_{l,i}(H^{-1}(\epsilon_{j})-H^{-1}(\epsilon_{j+1}))+H^{-1}(\epsilon_{j+1})(g_{l,i}-g_{u,i})}{g_{u,i}g_{i,i}}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\indent\leq L(K^{3}+K_{H,\epsilon_{0}})L_{1}((f_{l,i},g_{l,i}),(f_{u,i},g_{u,i}))(P_{*})+LL_{\epsilon_{0}}K(\epsilon_{j+i}-\epsilon_{j})

\displaystyle\indent\leq\epsilon.

In addition, since
H(s)<\epsilon
on
s\in(-\infty,K(H^{-1}(\epsilon_{0})+K)]
,
0
and
H(K(H^{-1}(\epsilon_{0})+K)
can serve as lower and upper bracket for any
H((s-f)/g)\in\mathcal{F}_{H}
with
s\in(-\infty,K(H^{-1}(\epsilon_{0})+K)]
.
Analogous brackets
1
and
H(K(H^{-1}(1-\epsilon_{0})-K)
work for the case
s\geq K(H^{-1}(1-\epsilon_{0})-K)
.
Thus, we have
N_{[]}(\epsilon,\mathcal{F}_{H},L_{1}(P_{*}))\leq N_{\epsilon}N^{\prime}_{\epsilon}+2<\infty
, and this implies the condition
(EC1)
.

To show
(EC2)
, first we notice that, the primitive
\int_{-\infty}^{t}H((s-f)/g)ds
is
gJ_{1}((t-f)/g)
in
t\in(-\infty,0]
, similarly one can see the primitive
\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(1-H((s-f)/g)\right)ds
is
gJ_{2}((t-f)/g)
in
t\in[0,\infty)
.

J_{1}
and
J_{2}
are uniformly bounded on
(-\infty,0],\,[0,\infty)
by sufficiently large
K_{J}>0
, respectively, since
H
has finite first moment, which implies
\int_{-\infty}^{0}H(s)ds+\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(1-H(s)\right)ds<\infty
.
Recalling that
\mathcal{F}_{2}
is uniformly bounded by
K
, as in the derivation steps to show
(EC1)
, there exists
M_{J_{1}}<0
such that
J(s)<\epsilon
when
s<K(J_{1}^{-1}(M_{J_{1}})+K)
, for each
\epsilon>0
.
Now, for general bracket pairs, we have,

\displaystyle P_{*}\left|g_{l,i}J_{1}\left(\frac{J_{1}^{-1}(\epsilon_{j})-f_{u,i}}{g_{u,i}}\right)-g_{u,i}J_{1}\left(\frac{J_{1}^{-1}(\epsilon_{j+1})-f_{l,i}}{g_{i,i}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq P_{*}\left|\left(g_{l,i}-g_{u,i}\right)J_{1}\left(\frac{J_{1}^{-1}(\epsilon_{j})-f_{u,i}}{g_{u,i}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\indent\qquad+P_{*}\left|g_{u,i}\left[J_{1}\left(\frac{J_{1}^{-1}(\epsilon_{j})-f_{u,i}}{g_{u,i}}\right)-J_{1}\left(\frac{J_{1}^{-1}(\epsilon_{j+1})-f_{l,i}}{g_{i,i}}\right)\right]\right|

\displaystyle\indent\leq K_{J}P_{*}\left|g_{u,i}-g_{l,i}\right|+L_{J_{1}}KP_{*}\left|\frac{g_{u,i}(f_{u,i}-f_{l,i})+f_{u,i}(g_{u,i}-g_{l,i})}{g_{u,i}g_{i,i}}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+L_{J_{1}}KP_{*}\left|\frac{g_{l,i}(J_{1}^{-1}(\epsilon_{j})-J_{1}^{-1}(\epsilon_{j+1}))+J_{1}^{-1}(\epsilon_{j+1})(g_{l,i}-g_{u,i})}{g_{u,i}g_{i,i}}\right|,

where
L_{J_{1}}
is a Lipschitz constant of
J_{1}^{-1}
on
[M_{J_{1}},J_{1}(0)]
.
Analogous derivation to steps for proving
(EC1)
can be directly applied to control the last term above, since
J_{1}
has global Lipschitz constant
1
because of condition
(E3)
.
In addition, similar reasoning can be applied to
J_{2}
on
t\geq 0
. So we omit the proof.
∎

Many conditional distributions can satisfy the conditions of Lemma
6
, such as log-concave CDFs.
Moreover, any union of classes of
\mathcal{F}_{H}
over finitely many
H

satisfies the conditions as well.

We next provide a follow-up to Lemma
6
under which
(E7)
and
(EC3)
hold for the special case
\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})=H((s-\hat{\mu}_{a}(\mathbf{X}))/\hat{\sigma}_{a}(\mathbf{X}))\in\mathcal{F}_{H}
.

Lemma 7
.

Suppose the setup and conditions of Lemma
6
hold. Define the Lipschitz constant of
H^{-1}
on
[\epsilon,1-\epsilon]
as

\displaystyle L_{\varepsilon}:=\sup_{\epsilon\leq s_{1}\neq s_{2}\leq 1-\epsilon}\frac{|H^{-1}(s_{2})-H^{-1}(s_{1})|}{|s_{2}-s_{1}|}.

(40)

If
\log L_{\varepsilon}\lesssim(1/\varepsilon)^{V}
,
\max\{|H^{-1}(\varepsilon)|,\,|H^{-1}(1-\varepsilon)|\}\lesssim\varepsilon^{1-V/V_{0}}
, and
\log N_{[]}(\varepsilon,\mathcal{F}_{1}\times\mathcal{F}_{2},L_{1}(P_{*}))\lesssim(1/\varepsilon)^{V_{0}}
for
0\leq V_{0}<V<2
, then
(E7)
and the second statement of
(EC3)
hold.

A wide range of conditional distributions satisfy this condition, such as normals, exponentials, and gammas.
In addition, we note that the convergence rate of
\mathcal{G}(s,t;\mathbf{Z};S)
is controlled by

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(s,t;\mathbf{Z};S)\lesssim|t-s|\left(\|\widehat{\mu}_{a}-\mu_{a,\infty}\|+\|\widehat{\sigma}_{a}-\sigma_{a,\infty}\|\right).

Hence, when
\widehat{\phi}_{a}\in\mathcal{F}_{H}
,
(E6)
is satisfied under sufficient rates of convergence of
\|\widehat{\pi}_{a}-\pi_{a,\infty}\|
and
\|\widehat{\mu}_{a}-\mu_{a,\infty}\|+\|\widehat{\sigma}_{a}-\sigma_{a,\infty}\|
.

Proof.

(E7)
is trivial.
We showed that
N_{[]}(\epsilon,\mathcal{F}_{H},L_{1}(P_{*}))
is controlled by
N_{\epsilon}N^{\prime}_{\epsilon}
in Section
D.2
. If
H(K(H^{-1}(\epsilon_{0})+K))=\epsilon
, then one can approximate
\epsilon_{0}
with
H\left(\frac{H^{-1}(\epsilon)-K}{K}\right)
. Similarly, if
1-H(s)=H(K(H^{-1}(1-\epsilon_{0})-K))
, then one can approximate
1-\epsilon_{0}
with
H\left(\frac{H^{-1}(1-\epsilon)+K}{K}\right)
.
Since we assumed
K>>1
, one can approxiamtely control
H^{-1}(\epsilon)\leq(H^{-1}(\epsilon)-K)/K
and
H^{-1}(1-\epsilon)\geq(H^{-1}(1-\epsilon)+K)/K
.
This implies that one can approximately control
[\epsilon_{0},1-\epsilon_{0}]
by
[\epsilon,1-\epsilon]
.
Thus, we have
N^{\prime}_{\epsilon}\lesssim\frac{L_{\epsilon}}{\epsilon}
approximately.
Hence, one could control
\log N^{\prime}_{\epsilon}
approximately by
\log(L_{\epsilon}/\epsilon)\lesssim(1/\epsilon)^{V}-\log(\epsilon)
.
Furthermore,
K_{H,\epsilon_{0}}=\max\{|H^{-1}(\epsilon_{0})|,\,|H^{-1}(1-\epsilon_{0})|\}
can be further approximately bounded by
K_{H,\epsilon}:=\max\{|H^{-1}(\epsilon)|,|H^{-1}(1-\epsilon)|\}
.
This yields

\displaystyle\log N_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\lesssim\log N_{[]}(\epsilon/K_{H,\epsilon},\mathcal{F}_{1}\times\mathcal{F}_{2},L_{1}(P_{*}))

\displaystyle\leq(K_{H,\epsilon}/\epsilon)^{V_{0}}\lesssim(1/\epsilon)^{V},

since
K_{H,\epsilon}^{V_{0}}\lesssim(1/\epsilon)^{V-V_{0}}
.
Thus,
\log N_{\epsilon}+\log N^{\prime}_{\epsilon}\lesssim(1/\epsilon)^{V}-\log(\epsilon)
, and this implies condition
(EC3)
.
∎

D.3
Proof of Theorem
1

Recall that
d_{1}
denotes the Wasserstein distance; see (
9
). In the proof of Theorem
1
(see Section
D.3.3
below), we will check that
d_{1}(\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n},F_{a})\rightarrow_{p}0
implies the claimed convergence, and
d_{1}(\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n},F_{a})\rightarrow_{p}0
if and only if
\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s)\rightarrow_{p}F_{a}(s)
for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
and
\int_{\mathbb{R}}|s|d\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s)\rightarrow_{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|s|\,dF_{a}(s)
.
Lemmas
2
and
3
provide conditions on
\widehat{F}_{a,n}\,\widehat{F}_{a,n}^{c_{0}}
that are used for establishing
\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s)\rightarrow_{p}F_{a}(s)
for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
and
\int_{\mathbb{R}}|s|d\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s)\rightarrow_{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|s|\,dF_{a}(s)
.
We first provide proofs of Lemmas
2
and
3
.

D.3.1
Proof of Lemma
2

Proof.

For any
x\in\mathbb{R}
,
|\widehat{F}^{c_{0}}_{a,n}(x)-F_{a}(x)|\leq|\widehat{F}_{a,n}(x)-F_{a}(x)|
. Moreover,

\displaystyle|\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(x)-F_{a}(x)|

\displaystyle=I(x<L_{n})F_{a}(x)+I(x\geq U_{n})[1-F_{a}(x)]

\displaystyle\qquad+I(L_{n}\leq x<U_{n})|\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(x)-F_{a}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq F_{a}(L_{n})+[1-F_{a}(U_{n})]+I(L_{n}\leq x<U_{n})|\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(x)-F_{a}(x)|

\displaystyle=o_{p}(1)+I(L_{n}\leq x<U_{n})|\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(x)-F_{a}(x)|.

If
L_{n}\leq x<U_{n}
, then there exists
k\in\{1,\dotsc,m_{n}-1\}
such that
x\in[s_{k},s_{k+1})
. Then,

\displaystyle|\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(x)-F_{a}(x)|

\displaystyle=|\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s_{k})-F_{a}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq|\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s_{k})-F_{a}(s_{k})|+|F_{a}(s_{k})-F_{a}(x)|,

(41)

and
|F_{a}(s_{k})-F_{a}(x)|=o_{p}(1)
because
F_{a}
is uniformly continuous and
|s_{k}-x|\leq\delta_{n}\to_{p}0
. We also have

\displaystyle\max_{k}|\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s_{k})-F_{a}(s_{k})|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{k}|\widehat{F}^{c_{0}}_{a,n}(s_{k})-F_{a}(s_{k})|\leq\max_{k}|\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s_{k})-F_{a}(s_{k})|\rightarrow_{p}0.

where the first inequality is from Theorem 1(i) of
Westling et al. (2020)
.
∎

D.3.2
Proof of Lemma
3

Proof.

We can write

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}|s|\,d\left\{\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s)-F_{a}(s)\right\}

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{L_{n}}|s|\,d\left\{\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s)-F_{a}(s)\right\}+\int_{L_{n}}^{U_{n}}|s|\,d\left\{\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s)-F_{a}(s)\right\}

\displaystyle\indent+\int_{U_{n}}^{\infty}|s|\,d\left\{\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s)-F_{a}(s)\right\}.

Since
\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s)=0
for
s\leq L_{n}
and
\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s)=1
for
s\geq U_{n}
,

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{L_{n}}|s|\,d\left\{\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s)-F_{a}(s)\right\}

\displaystyle=-\int_{-\infty}^{L_{n}}|s|\,dF_{a}(s),\text{ and}

\displaystyle\int_{U_{n}}^{\infty}|s|\,d\left\{\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s)-F_{a}(s)\right\}

\displaystyle=-\int_{U_{n}}^{\infty}|s|\,dF_{a}(s)

Since
\int_{\mathbb{R}}|s|\,dF_{a}(s)<\infty
,
F_{a}(L_{n})\rightarrow_{p}0
, and
F_{a}(U_{n})\rightarrow_{p}1
by assumption,
\int_{-\infty}^{L_{n}}|s|\,dF_{a}(s)=o_{p}(1)
and
\int_{U_{n}}^{\infty}|s|dF_{a}(s)=o_{p}(1)
.

Now, it suffices to study the limiting behavior of
\int_{L_{n}}^{U_{n}}|s|\,d\left\{\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s)-F_{a}(s)\right\}
.
First, we have

\displaystyle\int_{L_{n}}^{U_{n}}|s|d\left\{\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s)-F_{a}(s)\right\}

\displaystyle=\int_{L_{n}\wedge 0}^{U_{n}\wedge 0}|s|\,d\left\{\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s)-F_{a}(s)\right\}+\int_{L_{n}\vee 0}^{U_{n}\vee 0}|s|\,d\left\{\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s)-F_{a}(s)\right\}

\displaystyle=-\int_{L_{n}\wedge 0}^{U_{n}\wedge 0}sd\left\{\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s)-F_{a}(s)\right\}+\int_{L_{n}\vee 0}^{U_{n}\vee 0}s\,d\left\{\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s)-F_{a}(s)\right\}.

Furthermore, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{L_{n}\wedge 0}^{U_{n}\wedge 0}s\,d\left\{\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s)-F_{a}(s)\right\}

\displaystyle=(U_{n}\wedge 0)\left\{\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(U_{n}\wedge 0)-F_{a}(U_{n}\wedge 0)\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad-(L_{n}\wedge 0)\left\{\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(L_{n}\wedge 0)-F_{a}(L_{n}\wedge 0)\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad-\int_{L_{n}\wedge 0}^{U_{n}\wedge 0}\left\{\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s)-F_{a}(s)\right\}\,ds.

Since
\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(U_{n})=1
,

\displaystyle\left|(U_{n}\wedge 0)\left\{\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(U_{n}\wedge 0)-F_{a}(U_{n}\wedge 0)\right\}\right|\leq|U_{n}|[1-F_{a}(U_{n})],

which is
o_{p}(1)
by assumption. Similarly, since
\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(L_{n})=0
,

\displaystyle\left|(L_{n}\wedge 0)\left\{\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(L_{n}\wedge 0)-F_{a}(L_{n}\wedge 0)\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq|L_{n}|F_{a}(L_{n}),

which is
o_{p}(1)
by assumption. By a similar derivation for
\int_{L_{n}\vee 0}^{U_{n}\vee 0}s\,d\left\{\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s)-F_{a}(s)\right\}
, we then have

\displaystyle\int_{L_{n}}^{U_{n}}|s|\,d\left\{\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s)-F_{a}(s)\right\}

\displaystyle=-\int_{L_{n}\wedge 0}^{U_{n}\wedge 0}\left\{\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s)-F_{a}(s)\right\}\,ds

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{L_{n}\vee 0}^{U_{n}\vee 0}\left\{\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s)-F_{a}(s)\right\}\,ds+o_{p}(1).

Let
J_{1}:=\{k:1\leq k\leq m_{n}-1,L_{n}\vee 0\leq s_{k}<U_{n}\vee 0\}
. Then,

\displaystyle\int_{L_{n}\vee 0}^{U_{n}\vee 0}\left\{\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s)-F_{a}(s)\right\}\,ds

\displaystyle=-\int_{L_{n}\vee 0}^{U_{n}\vee 0}\left\{\left[1-\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s)\right]-\left[1-F_{a}(s)\right]\right\}\,ds

\displaystyle=-\delta_{n}\sum_{k\in J_{1}}\left\{\left[1-\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s_{k})\right]-\left[1-F_{a}(s_{k})\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad-\delta_{n}\sum_{k\in J_{1}}\left[1-F_{a}(s_{k})\right]+\int_{L_{n}\vee 0}^{U_{n}\vee 0}\left[1-F_{a}(s)\right]\,ds

\displaystyle=\delta_{n}\sum_{k\in J_{1}}\left\{\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s_{k})-F_{a}(s_{k})\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad-\delta_{n}\sum_{k\in J_{1}}\left[1-F_{a}(s_{k})\right]+\int_{L_{n}\vee 0}^{U_{n}\vee 0}\left[1-F_{a}(s)\right]\,ds.

We then have

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in J_{1}}\left\{\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s_{k})-F_{a}(s_{k})\right\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq k,\,s_{k}<U_{n}\vee 0}\left\{\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s_{k})-F_{a}(s_{k})\right\}-\sum_{1\leq k,\,s_{k}<L_{n}\vee 0}\left\{\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s_{k})-F_{a}(s_{k})\right\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{2\leq k,\,s_{k}<U_{n}\vee 0}\left\{\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s_{k})-F_{a}(s_{k})\right\}-\sum_{2\leq k,\,s_{k}<L_{n}\vee 0}\left\{\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s_{k})-F_{a}(s_{k})\right\}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\left|\delta_{n}\sum_{k\in J_{1}}\left\{\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s_{k})-F_{a}(s_{k})\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq 2\delta_{n}\max_{2\leq k\leq m_{n}-1}\left|\sum_{j=2}^{k}\left\{\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s_{j})-F_{a}(s_{j})\right\}\right|.

Now, by Marshall’s inequality (see, e.g., ExerCI’se 3.1-c in
Groeneboom and Jongbloed, 2014
), we have

\displaystyle 2\delta_{n}\max_{2\leq k\leq m_{n}-1}\left|\sum_{j=2}^{k}\left\{\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s_{j})-F_{a}(s_{j})\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq 2\delta_{n}\max_{2\leq k\leq m_{n}-1}\left|\sum_{j=2}^{k}\left\{\widehat{F}^{c_{0}}_{a,n}(s_{j})-F_{a}(s_{j})\right\}\right|,

which is
o_{p}(1)
by assumption. Next, since
1-F_{a}
is non-increasing, we have

\displaystyle\delta_{n}\sum_{k\in J_{1}}\left[1-F_{a}(s_{k+1})\right]\leq\int_{L_{n}\vee 0}^{U_{n}\vee 0}\left[1-F_{a}(s)\right]\,ds\leq\delta_{n}\sum_{k\in J_{1}}\left[1-F_{a}(s_{k})\right].

Hence,

\displaystyle\left|\delta_{n}\sum_{k\in J_{1}}\left[1-F_{a}(s_{k})\right]-\int_{L_{n}\vee 0}^{U_{n}\vee 0}\left[1-F_{a}(s)\right]\,ds\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\delta_{n}\left|\sum_{k\in J_{1}}\left[1-F_{a}(s_{k})\right]-\sum_{k\in J_{1}}\left[1-F_{a}(s_{k+1})\right]\right|

\displaystyle\qquad=\delta_{n}\left|\left[1-F_{a}(s_{k_{2}+1})\right]-\left[1-F_{a}(s_{k_{1}})\right]\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 2\delta_{n}=o_{p}(1)

where
k_{1}
and
k_{2}
are the minimal and maximal elements of
J_{1}
, respectively. Therefore,

\int_{L_{n}\vee 0}^{U_{n}\vee 0}\left\{\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s)-F_{a}(s)\right\}\,ds=o_{p}(1).

An analogous argument shows that

\displaystyle\int_{L_{n}\wedge 0}^{U_{n}\wedge 0}\left\{\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s)-F_{a}(s)\right\}\,ds=o_{p}(1),

which completes the proof.
∎

D.3.3
Proof of the main consistency result

For two cumulative distribution functions
F,G:{\mathbb{R}}\mapsto[0,1]
, the Lévy distance between
F
and
G
is defined as

\displaystyle{\displaystyle L(F,G):=\inf\{\varepsilon>0:F(x-\varepsilon)-\varepsilon\leq G(x)\leq F(x+\varepsilon)+\varepsilon,\;\forall x\in\mathbb{R}\}.}

(42)

We state and prove the following lemma which relates the Levy distance and the Wasserstein distance.

Lemma 8
.

For any CDFs
F
and
G
on
\mathbb{R}
,
L^{2}(F,G)\leq d_{1}(F,G)
.

Proof.

If
L(F,G)=0
then the result is trivial. If
L(F,G)>0
, then for any
h\in\mathbb{R}
such that
0<h<L(F,G)
, by definition of the Levy distance, either
F(x-h)-h>G(x)
or
G(x)>F(x+h)+h
for some
x\in\mathbb{R}
. If
G(x)>F(x+h)+h
, then by monotonicity of
F
and
G
,
G(y)>F(y)+h
for all
y\in(x,x+h)
. Hence,

\displaystyle d_{1}(F,G)\geq\int_{x}^{x+h}\left|F(y)-G(y)\right|dy\geq h^{2}.

If
F(x-h)-h>G(x)
, then an identical calculation yields the same result. Hence,
d_{1}(F,G)\geq h^{2}
for all
0<h<L(F,G)
, and taking the limit as
h\uparrow L(F,G)
yields the result.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1

Now, we give the main proof of Theorem
1
.

Proof.

We first show that the result follows if
d_{1}(\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n},F_{a})\rightarrow_{p}0
.
If
d_{1}(\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n},F_{a})\rightarrow_{p}0
,
then any subsequence
\{n_{k}\}
of
\mathbb{N}
has a further subsequence
\{n_{k_{l}}\}
for which
d_{1}(\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n_{k_{l}}},F_{a})\rightarrow_{a.s.}0
. Proposition 2-(c) of
Cule and Samworth (2010)
, this implies that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\alpha)
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{\varepsilon|s|}|\widehat{p}_{a,n_{k_{l}}}(s)-p_{a}(s)|\,ds\rightarrow_{a.s.}0,

(43)

since
p_{a}(s)\leq e^{-\alpha|s|+\beta}
by assumption. Hence, for any subsequence
\{n_{k}\}
of
\mathbb{N}
, there exists a further subsequence
\{n_{k_{l}}\}
such that
\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{\varepsilon|s|}|\widehat{p}_{a,n_{k_{l}}}(s)-p_{a}(s)|\,ds\rightarrow_{a.s.}0
holds, which implies that
\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{\varepsilon|s|}|\widehat{p}_{a,n_{k_{l}}}(s)-p_{a}(s)|\,ds\rightarrow_{p}0
. Since this implies the total variation distance converges in probability to zero, and convergence in total variation implies convergence in distribution, by a similar subsequence argument and the second statement of Proposition 2-(c) of
Cule and Samworth (2010)
, continuity of
p_{a}
further implies that
\sup_{s\in\mathbb{R}}e^{\varepsilon|s|}|\widehat{p}_{a,n}(s)-p_{a}(s)|\rightarrow_{p}0
. Therefore, the result follows if
d_{1}(\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n},F_{a})\rightarrow_{p}0
. Furthermore,
d_{1}(\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n},F_{a})\rightarrow_{p}0
if and only if
\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s)\rightarrow_{p}F_{a}(s)
for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
and
\int_{\mathbb{R}}|s|d\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s)\rightarrow_{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|s|\,dF_{a}(s)
(see, e.g., page 407 of
Pnanteros et al., 2019
), so it suffices to show these two statements.

We start by showing that
\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s)\rightarrow_{p}F_{a}(s)
for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
. If
\sup_{s\in\mathbb{R}}|\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s)-F_{a}(s)|\to_{p}0
, then since the other conditions of Lemma
2
hold by assumption, it follows that
\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s)\rightarrow_{p}F_{a}(s)
for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
. Hence, it is sufficient to show that
\sup_{s\in\mathbb{R}}|\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s)-F_{a}(s)|\to_{p}0
.

For each
s\in\mathbb{R}
, by Assumptions
(I1)
,
(I2)
, and the tower property, we have

\displaystyle P_{*}D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s)

\displaystyle=P_{*}\left[{I(A=a)\over\pi_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\left\{I(Y\leq s)-\phi_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right\}+\phi_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right]

\displaystyle=P_{*}\left[\frac{\pi_{a,*}(\mathbf{X})}{\pi_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\left\{\phi_{a,*}(s|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right\}+\phi_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right].

Furthermore, by
(E4)
,

\displaystyle P_{*}\left[\frac{\pi_{a,*}(\mathbf{X})}{\pi_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\left\{\phi_{a,*}(s|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right\}+\phi_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad=P_{*}\left[I(\mathbf{X}\in\mathcal{S}_{1})\frac{\pi_{a,*}(\mathbf{X})}{\pi_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\left\{\phi_{a,*}(s|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right\}+I(\mathbf{X}\in\mathcal{S}_{1})\phi_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+P_{*}\left[I(\mathbf{X}\in\mathcal{S}_{2})\frac{\pi_{a,*}(\mathbf{X})}{\pi_{a,\infty}\mathbf{X})}\left\{\phi_{a,*}(s|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right\}+I(\mathbf{X}\in\mathcal{S}_{2})\phi_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad=P_{*}\left[I(\mathbf{X}\in\mathcal{S}_{1})\frac{\pi_{a,*}(\mathbf{X})}{\pi_{a,*}(\mathbf{X})}\left\{\phi_{a,*}(s|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right\}+I(\mathbf{X}\in\mathcal{S}_{1})\phi_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+P_{*}\left[I(\mathbf{X}\in\mathcal{S}_{2})\frac{\pi_{a,*}(\mathbf{X})}{\pi_{a,\infty}\mathbf{X})}\left\{\phi_{a,*}(s|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{a,*}(s|\mathbf{X})\right\}+I(\mathbf{X}\in\mathcal{S}_{2})\phi_{a,*}(s|\mathbf{X})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad=P_{*}\left[I(\mathbf{X}\in\mathcal{S}_{1})\phi_{a,*}(s|\mathbf{X})\right]+P_{*}\left[I(\mathbf{X}\in\mathcal{S}_{2})\phi_{a,*}(s|\mathbf{X})\right]

\displaystyle=P_{*}\left[\phi_{a,*}(s|\mathbf{X})\right]=F_{a}(s).

Hence,
P_{*}D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(s)=F_{a}(s)
.

Now, since
\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s)=\mathbb{P}_{n}D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(s)
and
P_{*}D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(s)=F_{a}(s)
, by adding and subtracting terms we can write

\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s)-F_{a}(s)=(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{*})D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(s)+P_{*}\left[D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(s)-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s)\right].

(44)

For the first term of (
44
), we note that by conditions
(E2)
and
(EC1)
, for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
,

\begin{split}D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(s)&:(Y,A,\mathbf{X})\mapsto\frac{I(A=a)}{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})}\left[I(Y\leq s)-\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})\right]+\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})\\
&\in\mathcal{F}^{a}:=\left\{g(f_{1},\pi,f_{2},\phi_{a}):f_{1}\in\mathcal{F}_{1},\pi\in\mathcal{F}_{\pi},f_{2}\in\mathcal{F}_{2},\phi_{a}\in\mathcal{F}_{\phi}\right\},\end{split}

(45)

where
g:\{0,1\}\times[1,K]\times[0,1]\times[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}
is given by
g(a,b,c,d)=\frac{a}{b}(c-d)+d
,
\mathcal{F}_{1}
is the singleton class containing the function
A\mapsto I(A=a)
, and
\mathcal{F}_{2}:=\{Y\mapsto I(Y\leq s):s\in\mathbb{R}\}
. Here,
g
is a continuous function, and
\mathcal{F}_{1}
,
\mathcal{F}_{\pi}
,
\mathcal{F}_{2}
, and
\mathcal{F}_{\phi}
are
P_{*}
-Glivenko-Cantelli by
(EC1)
and Example 2.6.1 of
van der Vaart and Wellner (2023)
. Thus, by Theorem 3 of
van der Vaart and Wellner (2000)
,
\mathcal{F}^{a}
is
P_{*}
-Glivenko-Cantelli as well. Hence,

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in\mathbb{R}}\left|(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{*})D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(s)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}^{a}}\left|(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{*})f\right|\to_{a.s.}0.

Using total expectation, we can write the second summand in (
44
) as

\displaystyle P_{*}

\displaystyle\left[D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(s)-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s)\right]

\displaystyle=P_{*}\left[I(A=a)\left\{I(Y\leq s)-\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})\right\}\left\{\frac{1}{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})}-\frac{1}{{\pi}_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\right\}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\qquad\qquad+\left\{1-\frac{I(A=a)}{{\pi}_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\right\}\left\{\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right\}\right]

\displaystyle=P_{*}\left[\frac{\pi_{a,*}(\mathbf{X})}{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})\pi_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\left\{\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right\}\left\{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})-\pi_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})\right\}\right.

(46)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{\pi_{a,*}(\mathbf{X})}{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})\pi_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\left\{{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{a,*}(s|\mathbf{X})\right\}\left\{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})-\pi_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})\right\}

(47)

\displaystyle\left.\qquad\qquad+\left\{1-\frac{\pi_{a,*}(\mathbf{X})}{{\pi}_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\right\}\left\{\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right\}\right],

(48)

Hence, by
(E2)
and the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in\mathbb{R}}\left|P_{*}\left[D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(s)-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s)\right]\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim K^{2}\left\{P_{*}\left[\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})-{\pi}_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})\right]^{2}\right\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\qquad+K\left\{\sup_{s\in\mathbb{R}}P_{*}\left[\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})-{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right]^{2}\right\}^{1/2},

up to a constant, since
\widehat{\theta}_{a},\theta_{a,\infty},\theta_{a,*}
are less than a universal upper bound 1.
By
(E1)
, the first term is
o_{P}(1)
. To show that
(E1)
also implies the second term is
o_{P}(1)
, we introduce the Lévy metric, which is associated with the Wasserstein distance (see Lemma
8
).
We define

\hat{L}(\mathbf{X}):=L\left(\phi_{a,\infty}(\cdot|\mathbf{X}),\widehat{\phi}_{a}(\cdot|\mathbf{X})\right)

for all
\mathbf{X}\in\mathcal{X}
. Then, by the definition of the Lévy metric, for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
and
\mathbf{X}\in\mathcal{X}
we have

\displaystyle{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s-\hat{L}(\mathbf{X})|\mathbf{X})-\hat{L}(\mathbf{X})\leq\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})\leq{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s+\hat{L}(\mathbf{X})|\mathbf{X})+\hat{L}(\mathbf{X}).

Hence,

\displaystyle|\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})-{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})|

\displaystyle\quad=\max\left\{\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})-{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X}),{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})-\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})\right\}

\displaystyle\quad=\max\left\{\left[\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})-{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s+\hat{L}(\mathbf{X})|\mathbf{X})\right]+\left[{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s+\hat{L}(\mathbf{X})|\mathbf{X})-{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right],\right.

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad\left.\left[{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})-{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s-\hat{L}(\mathbf{X})|\mathbf{X})\right]+\left[{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s-\hat{L}(\mathbf{X})|\mathbf{X})-\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\max\left\{\hat{L}(\mathbf{X})+\left[{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s+\hat{L}(\mathbf{X})|\mathbf{X})-{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right],\right.

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad\left.\left[{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})-{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s-\hat{L}(\mathbf{X})|\mathbf{X})\right]+\hat{L}(\mathbf{X})\right\}

\displaystyle\quad=w\left(s,\hat{L}(\mathbf{X})|\mathbf{X}\right)+\hat{L}(\mathbf{X}),

where we define

\displaystyle w(s,k|\mathbf{X}):=\max\{{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s+k|\mathbf{X})-{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X}),{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})-{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s-k|\mathbf{X})\}

for all
k,s\in\mathbb{R}
and
\mathbf{X}\in\mathcal{X}
. Hence,

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in\mathbb{R}}P_{*}\left\{\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})-{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sup_{s\in\mathbb{R}}P_{*}w(s,\hat{L}(\mathbf{X})|\mathbf{X})^{2}+2P_{*}\hat{L}(\mathbf{X})^{2}.

(49)

By Lemma
8
(and Assumption
(E3)
), we have

P_{*}\hat{L}(\mathbf{X})^{2}\leq P_{*}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})-{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X}))|\,ds,

so by
(E1)
, we have
P_{*}\hat{L}(\mathbf{X})^{2}=o_{p}(1)
. By Jensen’s inequality, the fact that
|w(\cdot,\cdot|\cdot)|\leq 1
, Assumption
(E5)
, and the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in\mathbb{R}}P_{*}w(s,\hat{L}(\mathbf{X})|\mathbf{X})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{s\in\mathbb{R}}P_{*}\left|w(s,\hat{L}(\mathbf{X})|\mathbf{X})\right|

\displaystyle\leq P_{*}\sup_{s\in\mathbb{R}}\left|w(s,\hat{L}(\mathbf{X})|\mathbf{X})\right|

\displaystyle\leq P_{*}\left\{\hat{L}(\mathbf{X})R(\mathbf{X})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\left[P_{*}\left\{|\hat{L}(\mathbf{X})|^{2}\right\}\right]^{1/2}\left[P_{*}\left\{|R(\mathbf{X})|^{2}\right\}\right]^{1/2},

which is
o_{p}(1)
.

We have now shown that both summands of (
44
) are
o_{p}(1)
uniformly on
s\in\mathbb{R}
, so we conclude that
\sup_{s\in\mathbb{R}}|\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s)-F_{a}(s)|=o_{p}(1)
, and hence
\widehat{F}_{a,n}^{c}(s)\to_{p}F_{a}(s)
for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
.

We now show that
\int_{\mathbb{R}}|s|\,d\widehat{F}_{a,n}^{c}(s)\to_{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|s|\,dF_{a}(s)
. If we can show that

\displaystyle\delta_{n}\max_{2\leq k\leq m_{n}-1}\left|\sum_{j=2}^{k}\widehat{F}^{c_{0}}_{a,n}(s_{j})-\sum_{j=2}^{k}F_{a}(s_{j})\right|\rightarrow_{p}0,

(50)

then the result follows by Lemma
3
, since the other conditions of Lemma
3
hold by assumption and by the derivation above. We have

\displaystyle\delta_{n}\max_{2\leq k\leq m_{n}-1}\left|\sum_{j=2}^{k}\widehat{F}^{c_{0}}_{a,n}(s_{j})-\sum_{j=2}^{k}F_{a}(s_{j})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{n}\max_{2\leq k\leq m_{n}-1}\left\{\left|\sum_{j=2}^{k}\left[\widehat{F}_{a,n}^{c_{0}}(s_{j})-\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s_{j})\right]\right|+\left|\sum_{j=2}^{k}\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s_{j})-\sum_{j=2}^{k}F_{a}(s_{j})\right|\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{n}\max_{2\leq k\leq m_{n}-1}\left|\sum_{j=2}^{k}I\left\{\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s_{j})<0\right\}\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s_{j})\right|

(51)

\displaystyle\qquad+\delta_{n}\max_{2\leq k\leq m_{n}-1}\left|\sum_{k=2}^{j}I\left\{\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s_{j})>1\right\}\left[1-\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s_{j})\right]\right|

(52)

\displaystyle\qquad+\delta_{n}\max_{2\leq k\leq m_{n}-1}\left|\sum_{j=2}^{k}\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s_{j})-\sum_{j=2}^{k}F_{a}(s_{j})\right|.

(53)

We show that each of these three summands is
o_{p}(1)
in turn. First,
we study (
51
). We define
A_{n}
as
\max\{s_{k}:\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s_{k})<0,1\leq k\leq m_{n}-1\}
. We note that only the case where
A_{n}>L_{n}
matters, since if
A_{n}=L_{n}
then the expression is zero. We then have

\displaystyle\delta_{n}\max_{2\leq k\leq m_{n}-1}\left|\sum_{j=2}^{k}I\left\{\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s_{j})<0\right\}\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s_{j})\right|

\displaystyle\qquad=-\delta_{n}\sum_{k=2}^{m_{n}-1}I\left\{\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s_{k})<0\right\}\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s_{k})

\displaystyle\qquad\leq-\delta_{n}\sum_{k=1,s_{k}\leq A_{n}}I\left\{\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s_{k})<0\right\}\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s_{k})

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\delta_{n}\sum_{k=1,s_{k}\leq A_{n}}\left|\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s_{k})\right|

\displaystyle\qquad=\left[\delta_{n}\sum_{k=1,s_{k}\leq A_{n}}\left|\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s_{k})\right|-\int_{L_{n}}^{A_{n}}\left|\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s)\right|\,ds\right]+\int_{L_{n}}^{A_{n}}\left|\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s)\right|\,ds.

We can write
\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s)=\widehat{F}_{a,n,1}(s)-\widehat{F}_{a,n,2}(s)
for

\displaystyle\widehat{F}_{a,n,1}(s)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\frac{I(A_{i}=a)}{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X}_{i})}I(Y_{i}\leq s)+\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X}_{i})\right]

\displaystyle\widehat{F}_{a,n,2}(s)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{I(A_{i}=a)}{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X}_{i})}\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X}_{i}).

By Assumptions
(E2)
and
(E3)
,
\widehat{F}_{a,n,1}(s)
and
\widehat{F}_{a,n,2}(s)
are uniformly bounded monotone functions. Therefore, there exists a constant
C<\infty
, independent of
n
, such that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{L_{n}}^{A_{n}}\left|\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s)\right|\,ds-\delta_{n}\sum_{k=1,s_{k}\leq A_{n}}\left|\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s_{k})\right|\right|\leq C\delta_{n},

which is
o_{p}(1)
. To show that
\int_{L_{n}}^{A_{n}}\left|\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s)\right|\,ds\to_{p}0
, we define
\varepsilon_{n}:=\sup_{s\in\mathbb{R}}|\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s)-F_{a}(s)|
.
Since
\widehat{F}_{a,n}(A_{n})<0
if
A_{n}>L_{n}
, we have
F_{a}(A_{n})\leq\widehat{F}_{a,n}(A_{n})+\varepsilon_{n}<\varepsilon_{n}
, so
A_{n}\leq F_{a}^{-1}(\varepsilon_{n})
. If
u_{a}>-\infty
, then since
s_{2}>u_{a}
by assumption, we have
A_{n}\geq L_{n}=s_{2}-\delta_{n}>u_{a}-\delta_{n}
. Then, since
|\widehat{F}_{a,n}|
is uniformly bounded,
\int_{L_{n}}^{A_{n}}\left|\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s)\right|\,ds
is bounded up to a constant by
A_{n}-L_{n}\leq F_{a}^{-1}(\varepsilon_{n})-u_{a}+\delta_{n}
. Since
\varepsilon_{n}\to_{p}0
,
F_{a}^{-1}(\varepsilon_{n})\to_{p}u_{a}
, so this is
o_{p}(1)
. If
u_{a}=-\infty
, then
A_{n}\leq F_{a}^{-1}(\varepsilon_{n})\rightarrow_{p}-\infty
by uniform continuity of
F_{a}
. Hence,
\int_{L_{n}}^{A_{n}}\left|\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s)\right|ds\leq\int_{-\infty}^{A_{n}}\left|\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s)\right|ds\rightarrow_{p}0
,
as long as
\int_{-\infty}^{0}\left|\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s)\right|ds<\infty
, which we now show. By definition and Assumption
(E2)
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\left|\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s)\right|\,ds

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\left|{\mathbb{P}}_{n}D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(s)\right|\,ds\

(54)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left\{{\mathbb{P}}_{n}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\left|D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(s)\right|ds\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{I(A_{i}=a)}{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X}_{i})}I(Y_{i}\leq 0)(-Y_{i})\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\left[1-\frac{I(A_{i}=a)}{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X}_{i})}\right]\int_{-\infty}^{0}\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X}_{i})\,ds\right|\right\}

\displaystyle\leq K\mathbb{E}\left|Y^{a}\right|+(1+K)\mathbb{E}\left\{\int_{-\infty}^{0}\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})\,ds\right\},

(55)

which is finite by log-concavity of the distriution of
Y^{a}
and Assumption
(E3)
. Hence,
\int_{-\infty}^{0}\left|\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s)\right|\,ds<\infty
almost surely. Therefore, (
51
) is
o_{p}(1)
. Similar reasoning can be applied to show that (
52
) is
o_{p}(1)
, so we omit the details.

Finally, we prove that

\displaystyle\delta_{n}\max_{2\leq k\leq m_{n}-1}\left|\sum_{j=2}^{k}\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s_{j})-\sum_{j=2}^{k}F_{a}(s_{j})\right|=o_{p}(1),

(56)

from which it will follow that
\int_{\mathbb{R}}|s|\,d\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s)\to_{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|s|\,dF_{a}(s)
. Then, where
\tilde{F}_{a,n}(x):=\widehat{F}_{a,n}(x)I(x\leq 0)+[\widehat{F}_{a,n}(x)-1]I(x>0)
and
\tilde{F}_{a}(x):=F_{a}(x)I(x\leq 0)+[F_{a}(x)-1]I(x>0)
, we have

\displaystyle\delta_{n}\left|\sum_{j=2}^{k}\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s_{j})-\sum_{j=2}^{k}F_{a}(s_{j})\right|

\displaystyle\qquad=\delta_{n}\left|\sum_{j=2}^{k}\tilde{F}_{a,n}(s_{j})-\sum_{j=2}^{k}\tilde{F}_{a}(s_{j})\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\left|\int_{L_{n}}^{s_{k}}\left[\tilde{F}_{a,n}(s)-\tilde{F}_{a}(s)\right]\,ds\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\left|\int_{L_{n}}^{s_{k}}\tilde{F}_{a,n}(s)\,ds-\delta_{n}\sum_{j=2}^{k}\tilde{F}_{a,n}(s_{j})\right|+\left|\int_{L_{n}}^{s_{k}}\tilde{F}_{a}(s)\,ds-\delta_{n}\sum_{j=2}^{k}\tilde{F}_{a}(s_{j})\right|.

As above, we can write
\widehat{F}_{a,n}
as the difference of two monotone functions, and
F_{a}
is monotone by definition, so that

\displaystyle\max_{2\leq k\leq m_{n}-1}\left|\int_{L_{n}}^{s_{k}}\tilde{F}_{a,n}(s)\,ds-\delta_{n}\sum_{j=2}^{k}\tilde{F}_{a,n}(s_{j})\right|

\displaystyle=o_{p}(1)\text{ and}

\displaystyle\max_{2\leq k\leq m_{n}-1}\left|\int_{L_{n}}^{s_{k}}\tilde{F}_{a}(s)\,ds-\delta_{n}\sum_{j=2}^{k}\tilde{F}_{a}(s_{j})\right|

\displaystyle=o_{p}(1).

Furthermore,

\displaystyle\max_{2\leq k\leq m_{n}-1}\left|\int_{L_{n}}^{s_{k}}\left[\tilde{F}_{a,n}-\tilde{F}_{a}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\sup_{L_{n}<t\leq U_{n}}\left|\int_{L_{n}}^{s_{k}}\left[\tilde{F}_{a,n}-\tilde{F}_{a}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\qquad=\sup_{L_{n}<t\leq U_{n}}\left|\int_{-\infty}^{t}\left[\tilde{F}_{a,n}-\tilde{F}_{a}\right]-\int_{-\infty}^{L_{n}}\left[\tilde{F}_{a,n}-\tilde{F}_{a}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\left|\int_{-\infty}^{L_{n}}\left[\tilde{F}_{a,n}-\tilde{F}_{a}\right]\right|+\sup_{L_{n}<t\leq U_{n}}\left|\int_{-\infty}^{t}\left[\tilde{F}_{a,n}-\tilde{F}_{a}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 2\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\int_{-\infty}^{t}\left[\tilde{F}_{a,n}-\tilde{F}_{a}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 2\sup_{t\leq 0}\left|\int_{-\infty}^{t}\left[\tilde{F}_{a,n}-\tilde{F}_{a}\right]\right|+2\sup_{0<t}\left|\int_{-\infty}^{t}\left[\tilde{F}_{a,n}-\tilde{F}_{a}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 2\sup_{t\leq 0}\left|\int_{-\infty}^{t}\left[\widehat{F}_{a,n}-F_{a}\right]\right|+2\left|\int_{-\infty}^{0}\left[\widehat{F}_{a,n}-F_{a}\right]\right|+2\sup_{0<t}\left|\int_{0}^{t}\left[\left\{1-\widehat{F}_{a,n}\right\}-\left\{1-F_{a}\right\}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 4\sup_{t\leq 0}\left|\int_{-\infty}^{t}\left[\widehat{F}_{a,n}-F_{a}\right]\right|+2\sup_{0<t}\left|\int_{0}^{t}\left[\left\{1-\widehat{F}_{a,n}\right\}-\left\{1-F_{a}\right\}\right]\right|,

where the fifth inequality holds due to
\int_{-\infty}^{t}=\int_{-\infty}^{0}+\int_{0}^{t}
. We also have

\displaystyle\sup_{0<t}\left|\int_{0}^{t}\left[\left\{1-\widehat{F}_{a,n}\right\}-\left\{1-F_{a}\right\}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\sup_{0<t}\left|\int_{t}^{\infty}\left[\left\{1-\widehat{F}_{a,n}\right\}-\left\{1-F_{a}\right\}\right]\right|+\left|\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[\left\{1-\widehat{F}_{a,n}\right\}-\left\{1-F_{a}\right\}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 2\sup_{0\leq t}\left|\int_{t}^{\infty}\left[\left\{1-\widehat{F}_{a,n}\right\}-\left\{1-F_{a}\right\}\right]\right|.

Thus, (
56
) follows if

\displaystyle\sup_{t\leq 0}\left|\int_{-\infty}^{t}\left[\widehat{F}_{a,n}-F_{a}\right]\right|=o_{p}(1)\text{ and}

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t}\left|\int_{t}^{\infty}\left[\left\{1-\widehat{F}_{a,n}\right\}-\left\{1-F_{a}\right\}\right]\right|=o_{p}(1).

We only check the first statement in the preceding display, since similar reasoning can be applied to the second statement.
We use the decomposition established in (
44
):

\displaystyle\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s)-F_{a}(s)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{P}}_{n}D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(s)-P_{*}D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s)

\displaystyle=(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{*})D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(s)+P_{*}\left[D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(s)-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s)\right].

(57)

For the second term in (
57
), as in (
46
)–(
48
), we can write

\int_{-\infty}^{t}P_{*}\left[D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(s)-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s)\right]\,ds=P_{*}\left[Q_{1}(t,\mathbf{Z})+Q_{2}(t,\mathbf{Z})+Q_{3}(t,\mathbf{Z})\right]

for

\displaystyle Q_{1}(t,\mathbf{Z})

\displaystyle=\frac{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})-{\pi}_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})}\int_{-\infty}^{t}\left\{\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})-{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right\}\,ds,

(58)

\displaystyle Q_{2}(t,\mathbf{Z})

\displaystyle=\frac{{\pi}_{a,*}(\mathbf{X})\left\{{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})}-{\pi}_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})\right\}}{{\pi}_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X}){\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})}}\int_{-\infty}^{t}\left\{{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})-{\phi}_{a,*}(s|\mathbf{X})\right\}\,ds,

(59)

\displaystyle Q_{3}(t,\mathbf{Z})

\displaystyle=\frac{{\pi}_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})-{\pi}_{a,*}(\mathbf{X})}{\pi_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\int_{-\infty}^{t}\left\{\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})-{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right\}\,ds.

(60)

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\leq 0}\left|P_{*}Q_{1}(t,\mathbf{Z})\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq K^{2}\sup_{t\leq 0}P_{*}\left[\int_{-\infty}^{t}\left\{\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})-{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right\}\,ds\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times P_{*}\left[\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})-{\pi}_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\sup_{t\leq 0}\left|P_{*}Q_{2}(t,\mathbf{Z})\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq K^{4}C_{1}P_{*}\left[\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})-{\pi}_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\sup_{t\leq 0}\left|P_{*}Q_{3}(t,\mathbf{Z})\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq K^{2}\sup_{t\leq 0}P_{*}\left[\int_{-\infty}^{t}\left\{\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})-{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right\}\,ds\right]^{2}

where
C_{1}=2P_{*}\left[\int_{-\infty}^{0}{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})ds\right]^{2}+2P_{*}\left[\int_{-\infty}^{0}{\phi}_{a,*}(s|\mathbf{X})ds\right]^{2}
.
And,
P_{*}\left[\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})-{\pi}_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})\right]^{2}=o_{p}(1)
holds by assumption
(E1)
.
We also have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\leq 0}P_{*}\left[\int_{-\infty}^{t}\left\{\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})-{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right\}\,ds\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq P_{*}\left[\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\left|\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})-{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right|\,ds\right]^{2},

which is also
o_{p}(1)
by
(E1)
.
This further implies that
\int_{-\infty}^{0}{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\,ds\in L_{2}(P_{*})
by Assumption
(E3)
. Additionally, by
(I1)
and
(I2)
, Jensen’s inequality, and the tower property,

\displaystyle P_{*}\left[\int_{-\infty}^{0}{\phi}_{a,*}(s|\mathbf{X})\,ds\,\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq P_{*}\left[\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|s|\,d{\phi}_{a,*}(s|\mathbf{X})\right]^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}(|Y^{a}||\mathbf{X})\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\mathbb{E}\left[(Y^{a})^{2}|\mathbf{X}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[(Y^{a})^{2}\right],

which is finite by
(I4)
since log-concave distributions have finite moments. Thus,
C_{1}<\infty
. We conclude that
\sup_{t\leq 0}\left|P_{*}Q_{j}(t,\mathbf{X})\right|=o_{p}(1)
for
j\in\{1,2,3\}
, which implies that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in(-\infty,0]}\left|\int_{-\infty}^{t}P_{*}\left[D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(s)-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s)\right]\,ds\right|\rightarrow_{p}0.

Finally, we show that the first term in (
57
) is
o_{p}(1)
. We have

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{t}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{*})D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(s)\,ds

\displaystyle\quad=(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{*})\left[\frac{I(A=a)}{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})}\left\{I(Y\leq t)(t-Y)-\int_{-\infty}^{t}\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})\,ds\right\}+\int_{-\infty}^{t}\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})\,ds\right].

(61)

Hence, similar to (
45
), we can write

\displaystyle\sup_{t\leq 0}\left|\int_{-\infty}^{t}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{*})D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(s)\,ds\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{f\in\mathcal{\mathcal{}}F^{a}_{\rm int}}\left|(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{*})f\right|,

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{a}_{\rm int}:=\left\{g(f_{1},\pi,f_{3},f_{4}):f_{1}\in\mathcal{F}_{1},\pi\in\mathcal{F}_{\pi},f_{3}\in\mathcal{F}_{3},f_{4}\in\mathcal{F}_{4}\right\}

for
g
and
\mathcal{F}_{1}
defined following (
45
),
\mathcal{F}_{3}:=\left\{Y\mapsto I(Y\leq t)(t-Y):t\in(-\infty,0]\right\}
, and

\mathcal{F}_{4}:=\left\{\mathbf{X}\mapsto\int_{-\infty}^{t}\phi_{a}(s|\mathbf{X})\,ds:\phi_{a}:\in\mathcal{F}_{\phi},\,t\in(-\infty,0]\right\}.

The classes
\mathcal{F}_{1}
and
\mathcal{F}_{\pi}
are
P_{*}
-Glivenko Cantelli as noted above.
For
\mathcal{F}_{3}
, we note that
\sup_{t\leq 0}|({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})(Y-t)|=|({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})Y|\to_{a.s.}0
because
P_{*}|Y|<\infty
, so the class
\{Y\mapsto Y-t:t\leq 0\}
is
P_{*}
-Glivenko Cantelli.
Hence, since
\{Y\mapsto I(Y\leq t):t\leq 0\}
is also
P_{*}
-Glivenko Cantelli and
Y\mapsto|Y|
is an envelope for
\mathcal{F}_{3}
,
\mathcal{F}_{3}
is
P_{*}
-Glivenko Cantelli by Proposition
1
.
Finally,
\mathcal{F}_{4}
is
P_{*}
-Glivenko-Cantelli by Assumption
(EC2)
.
Also by
(E3)
, an envelope function for
\mathcal{F}^{a}_{\rm int}
is given by

KI(A=a)\left\{|Y|+h(\mathbf{X})\right\}+h(\mathbf{X})

Since
{\mathbb{E}}[I(A=a)|Y|]\leq{\mathbb{E}}|Y^{a}|<\infty
, and
h(\mathbf{X})\in L_{2}(P_{*})
by assumption
(E3)
, this envelope function is integrable.
Thus, by Theorem 3 in
van der Vaart and Wellner (2000)
(see Proposition
1
),
\mathcal{F}^{a}_{\rm int}
is
P_{*}
-Glivenko-Cantelli.
This implies (
61
) is
o_{p}(1)
, so that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\leq 0}\left|\int_{-\infty}^{t}\left[\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s)-F_{a}(s)\right]\,ds\right|=o_{p}(1).

This further yields that (
53
) is
o_{p}(1)
, which concludes the proof.
∎

D.4
Proof of Theorem
2

D.4.1
Lemmas needed for proof Theorem
2

We define three basic processes,

\displaystyle\widehat{G}_{a,n}(x)=\int_{-\infty}^{x}\widehat{p}_{a,n}(t)dt=\int_{s_{1}}^{x}{\rm exp}(\widehat{\varphi}_{a,n})(t)dt,

\displaystyle\widehat{H}_{a,n}(x)=\int_{s_{1}}^{x}\widehat{G}_{a,n}(t)dt,

\displaystyle\widehat{H}^{c}_{a,n}(x)=\int_{s_{1}}^{x}\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(t)dt.

The following lemma is a slight modification of Lemma A.1 in
Balabdaoui and Wellner (2007)
, which is itself an extension of the pioneering work of
Kim and Pollard (1990)
.

Lemma 9
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a collection of functions defined on
[s_{0}-\delta,s_{0}+\delta]^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}
with small
\delta>0
and arbitrary positive integer
m
. Suppose that for a fixed
s_{1}\in[s_{0}-\delta,s_{0}+\delta]
and
R>0
, such that
s_{0}-\delta\leq s_{1}\leq s_{2}\leq s_{1}+R\leq s_{0}+\delta
, the collection

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{s_{0},R}=\{f_{s_{1},s_{2}}:f_{s_{1},s_{2}}(\mathbf{x})=f(s_{1},s_{2},\mathbf{x})\in\mathcal{F},s_{0}-\delta\leq s_{1}\leq s_{2}\leq s_{1}+R\leq s_{0}+\delta\}

admits an envelope
F_{s_{0},,R}
, such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}F^{2}_{s_{0},R}(\mathbf{X})\leq K_{0}R^{2t-1},~~R\leq R_{0}

for some
t\geq 1/2
and
K_{0}>0
, depending only on
s_{0}
and
\delta
. Moreover, suppose that

\displaystyle\sup_{Q}\int_{0}^{1}\sqrt{{\rm log}N(\eta\|F_{s_{1},R}\|_{Q,2},\mathcal{F}_{s_{0},R},L_{2}(Q))}d\eta<\infty.

Then, for each
\epsilon>0
, there exist random variables
M_{n}
of order
O_{P}(1)
which does not depend on
s_{1},s_{2}
and
R_{0}>0
, such that

\displaystyle|({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-{\mathbb{P}})f_{s_{1},s_{2}}|\leq\epsilon|s_{2}-s_{1}|^{l+t}+n^{-(l+t)/(2l+1)}M_{n}~~~~{\rm for}~|s_{2}-s_{1}|\leq R_{0}

for
f\in\mathcal{F}_{s_{0},R}
and
l>0
.

Proof.

The proof is identical to the proof of Lemma A.1 of
Balabdaoui and Wellner (2007)
where one can prove the same result for
\mathcal{F}_{s_{0},R}
in a similar fashion to
\mathcal{F}_{x,R}
in their proof.
∎

The following lemma about analytical properties of
p_{a},\varphi_{a}
is identical to Lemma 4.2 in
Balabdaoui et al. (2009)
.

Lemma 10
.

If
(R1)
and
(R2)
hold, then, for
a\in\{0,1\}
, we have

\displaystyle p_{a}^{(j)}(s_{0})=[\varphi^{\prime}_{a}(s_{0})]^{j}p_{a}(s_{0})~~~~{\rm for}~j=1,\dots,k-1

(62)

and, for
j=k
,

\displaystyle p_{a}^{(k)}(s_{0})=(\varphi_{a}^{(k)}(s_{0})+[\varphi^{\prime}_{a}(s_{0})]^{k})p_{a}(s_{0}).

(63)

The following lemma shows that the distance between two adjacent knots around the target point
s_{0}
follows the same rate of convergence with the non-causal log-concave MLE
(
Balabdaoui et al., 2009
)
. Recall that
\tau_{n}^{+},\tau_{n}^{-}
are defined in (
16
) and
\delta_{n}
denotes the regular grid length for the isotonic regression grid (see (
5
) and the preceding texts).

Lemma 11
.

If
(I1)
–
(I4)
,
(G1)
,
(E1)
–
(E8)
,

(EC1)
–
(EC3)
, and

(R1)
–
(R2)
hold, and if
\delta_{n}=O_{p}(n^{-(k+1)/(2k+1)})
, then, for
a\in\{0,1\}
,

\displaystyle\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-}=O_{p}(n^{-1/(2k+1)}).

Proof.

The proof is aligned with the proof of Theorem 4.3 and Lemma 4.4 from
Balabdaoui et al. (2009)
. We similarly define

\displaystyle R_{1n}

\displaystyle:=\int\Delta(y)(\widehat{p}_{a,n}-p_{a})(y)dy,

\displaystyle R_{2n}

\displaystyle:=\int\Delta(y)d(\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}-F_{a})(y),

where

\displaystyle\Delta(y)=(y-\tau_{n}^{-})I_{[\tau_{n}^{-},\bar{\tau}]}(y)+(\tau_{n}^{+}-y)I_{[\bar{\tau},\tau_{n}^{+}]}(y)-\frac{1}{4}(\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})I_{[\tau_{n}^{-},\tau_{n}^{+}]}(y),

and
\bar{\tau}=\frac{\tau_{n}^{-}+\tau_{n}^{+}}{2}
. Following the same steps in
Balabdaoui et al. (2009)
for the log-concave MLE
\widehat{p}_{a,n}
, one can easily verify that

\displaystyle-R_{1n}\leq\frac{\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-}}{2n}-R_{2n}

(64)

\displaystyle R_{1n}=M_{k}p_{a}(s_{0})\varphi_{a}^{(k)}(s_{0})(\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})^{k+2}+o_{p}((\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})^{k+2}),

(65)

where
M_{k}>0
depends only on
k
. Furthermore, since uniform convergence of
\widehat{F}_{a,n}
to
F_{a}
(see proof of Theorem
1
) holds with
F_{a}(s_{0})>0
, and consistency from Theorem
1
implies
\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-}=o_{p}(1)
, we can identify
\widehat{F}_{a,n}
with
\widehat{F}^{c_{0}}_{a,n}
locally for the term
R_{2n}
.
Thus, we identify
\widehat{F}_{a,n}
with
\widehat{F}^{c_{0}}_{a,n}
throughout the following proof.
We further decompose
R_{2n}
as follows:

\displaystyle\int\Delta(y)d(\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}-F_{a})(y)=\int\Delta(y)d(\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}-\widehat{F}_{a,n})(y)+\int\Delta(y)d(\widehat{F}_{a,n}-F_{a})(y).

(66)

We start with analyzing the second term in (
66
). With integration by parts, one can check

\displaystyle\int\Delta(y)d(\widehat{F}_{a,n}-F_{a})(y)

\displaystyle=\int_{\bar{\tau}}^{\tau_{n}^{+}}\Big[(\widehat{F}_{a,n}-F_{a})(y)-(\widehat{F}_{a,n}-F_{a})(2\bar{\tau}-y)\Big]dy

(67)

\displaystyle\indent-\frac{\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-}}{4}\Big[(\widehat{F}_{a,n}-F_{a})(\tau_{n}^{+})-(\widehat{F}_{a,n}-F_{a})(\tau_{n}^{-})\Big].

(68)

To examine terms (
67
) and (
68
), we exploit the decomposition
\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s)-F_{a}(s)={\mathbb{P}}_{n}D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(s)-P_{*}D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s)=({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(s)+P_{*}(D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(s)-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s))
. Then (
68
) can be expressed as

\displaystyle\frac{\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-}}{4}\Big[({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})(D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(\tau_{n}^{+})

\displaystyle-D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(\tau_{n}^{-}))

\displaystyle+P_{*}\Big((D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}})(\tau_{n}^{+})-(D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}})(\tau_{n}^{-})\Big)\Big].

For
[s_{1},s_{2}]\subseteq[s_{0}-\delta,s_{0}+\delta]
,

\begin{split}({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})(D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(s_{2})-D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(s_{1}))&=({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})\Big[\frac{I(A=a)}{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})}I(s_{1}<Y\leq s_{2})\\
&\indent+(\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s_{2}|\mathbf{X})-\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s_{1}|\mathbf{X}))\Big(1-\frac{I(A=a)}{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})}\Big)\Big].\end{split}

(69)

To apply Lemma
9
, we line up with the conditions for two terms in (
69
). Firstly,
\{(a,b]:a<b\}
has VC dimension of 2 (see Example 2.6.1 in
van der Vaart and Wellner, 2023
). Thus, since the function class
\mathcal{F}_{1}=\{I_{(s_{1},s_{2}](\cdot)}:[s_{1},s_{2}]\subseteq I_{s_{0},\omega},s_{1}\leq s_{2}\leq s_{1}+R\}
allows an envelope function of constant 1,

\displaystyle N(\epsilon,\mathcal{F}_{1},L_{2}(Q))\leq C_{3}\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)^{2}

for any probability measure
Q
and a constant
C_{3}>0
(see Theorem 2.6.7 in
van der Vaart and Wellner, 2023
). Similar reasoning can be applied to the indicator function on a single point. In addition, with Assumption
(E2)
,
(EC3)
, and Lemma 5.1 in
van der Vaart and van der Laan (2006)
, a function class
\mathcal{F}_{2}:=\Big\{\frac{I_{\{a\}}(x_{1})}{\pi_{a}(\mathbf{x_{2}})}:\pi_{a}\in\mathcal{F}_{\pi},x_{1}\in\{0,1\},\mathbf{x_{2}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\Big\}
satisfies, for arbitrary probability measure
Q
,

\displaystyle\sup_{Q}N(\epsilon K,\mathcal{F}_{2},L_{2}(Q))\leq\sup_{Q}N(\epsilon,\mathcal{F}_{\pi},L_{2}(Q))

by Assumption
(E2)
and knowing that the function class
\mathcal{F}_{\pi}
allows the same envelope as
\mathcal{F}_{1}
.
Thus, the whole function class
\mathcal{F}_{t1}:=\mathcal{F}_{1}\cdot\mathcal{F}_{2}
for the first term in (
69
) has finite uniform entropy integral, by Assumption
(EC3)
and Lemma 5.1 from
van der Vaart and van der Laan (2006)
,

\displaystyle\sup_{Q}{\rm log}N(\epsilon,\mathcal{F}_{t1},L_{2}(Q))\lesssim\epsilon^{-V}-{\rm log}(\epsilon)

up to a constant, with
V\in[0,2)
. Furthermore, the class allows an envelope
KI(s_{0}-\omega<Y\leq s_{0}+\omega)I(A=a)
which satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big(KI(s_{0}-\omega<Y\leq s_{0}+\omega)I(A=a)\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle=K^{2}P_{*}(s_{0}-\omega<Y^{a}\leq s_{0}+\omega)

\displaystyle\leq C_{4}\omega K^{2}

for some constant
C_{4}>0
. Hence, the first term in (
69
) satisfies the assumptions of Lemma
9
with
t=1,l=k
, and therefore, there exist a random variable
B_{1}
which has order of
O_{p}(n^{-(k+1)/(2k+1)})
and is independent of
s_{1},s_{2}
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{[s_{1},s_{2}]\subset I_{s_{0},\omega},s_{1}\leq s_{2}\leq s_{1}+R}\Big|({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})\Big[\frac{I(A=a)}{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})}I(s_{1}<Y\leq s_{2})\Big|\leq\epsilon|s_{2}-s_{1}|^{k+1}+B_{1},

(70)

for arbitrary
\epsilon>0
.
Next, we set up a similar reasoning for the second term in (
69
). With Assumption
(E7)
and Lemma 5.1 from
van der Vaart and van der Laan (2006)
, a function class
\mathcal{F}_{3}:=\{\phi_{a}(s_{2}|\cdot)-\phi_{a}(s_{1}|\cdot):[s_{1},s_{2}]\subseteq[s_{0}-\delta,s_{0}+\delta],s_{1}\leq s_{2}\leq s_{1}+R,\phi_{a}\in\mathcal{F}_{\phi}\}
satisfies

\displaystyle\sup_{Q}{\rm log}N(\epsilon,\mathcal{F}_{3},L_{2}(Q))\lesssim\epsilon^{-V}

up to a constant with
V\in[0,2)
, and supremum taken over any probability measure
Q
. Thus, similar to the reasoning above combined with Assumption
(E2)
and Lemma 5.1 in
van der Vaart and van der Laan (2006)
, one can easily show that a function class
\mathcal{F}_{t2}:=\mathcal{F}_{3}\cdot(1-\mathcal{F}_{2})
for the second term satisfies

\displaystyle\sup_{Q}{\rm log}N(\epsilon,\mathcal{F}_{3},L_{2}(Q))\lesssim\epsilon^{-V}.

This yields the finite uniform entropy integral of this function class. From Assumption
(E7)
, this class has an envelope
(1+K)R_{1}(\mathbf{X})\omega^{\alpha}
, and, the envelope satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big((1+K)R_{1}(\mathbf{X})R^{\alpha}\Big)^{2}=\omega^{2\alpha}(1+K)^{2}\mathbb{E}R_{1}^{2}(\mathbf{X}).

Hence, this further implies that the second term satisfies the conditions in Lemma
9
with some
t>1
and
l=k
. In other words, for each
\epsilon>0
, there exist a random variable
B_{2}
which has order of
O_{p}(n^{-(k+t)/(2k+1)})
and is independent of
s_{1},s_{2}
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{[s_{1},s_{2}]\subset I_{s_{0},\omega},s_{1}\leq s_{2}\leq s_{1}+R}\Big|(\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s_{2}|\mathbf{X})-\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s_{1}|\mathbf{X}))\Big(1-\frac{I(A=a)}{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})}\Big)\Big|\leq\epsilon|s_{2}-s_{1}|^{k+t}+B_{2}.

(71)

Combining the two empirical process terms (
70
) and (
71
), for the empirical process part of (
68
), we obtain

\begin{split}\Bigg|({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})\Big\{\frac{\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-}}{4}&\Big[(\widehat{F}_{a,n}-F_{a})(\tau_{n}^{+})-(\widehat{F}_{a,n}-F_{a})(\tau_{n}^{-})\Big]\Big\}\Bigg|\\
&\leq 2\epsilon(\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})^{k+2}+(\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})O_{p}\Big(n^{-(k+1)/(2k+1)}\Big),\end{split}

(72)

for each
\epsilon>0
.
We now check the empirical process term of (
67
). Since, in (
70
) and (
71
),
B_{1}
and
B_{2}
do not depend on
s_{1},s_{2}
, we have

\begin{split}\Big|\int_{\bar{\tau}}^{\tau_{n}^{+}}&({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})(D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(y)-D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(2\bar{\tau}-y))dy\Big|\\
&\leq\int_{\bar{\tau}}^{\tau_{n}^{+}}\Big|({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})(D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(y)-D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(2\bar{\tau}-y))\Big|dy\\
&\leq 2\epsilon(\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})^{k+2}+(\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})O_{p}\Big(n^{-(k+1)/(2k+1)}\Big),\end{split}

(73)

due to
2y-2{\bar{\tau}}\leq(\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})
in
y\in[{\bar{\tau}},\tau_{n}^{+}]
.

On the other hand, for the remainder term analyses for (
67
) and (
68
), we exploit a decomposition as follows

\displaystyle P_{*}

\displaystyle\Big[(D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}})(\tau_{n}^{+})-(D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}})(\tau_{n}^{-})\Big]

\displaystyle=P_{*}\Big[\frac{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})-\pi_{\infty}(\mathbf{X})}{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})}\Big((\widehat{\phi}_{a}-\phi_{a,\infty})(\tau_{n}^{+}|\mathbf{X})-(\widehat{\phi}_{a}-\phi_{a,\infty})(\tau_{n}^{-}|\mathbf{X})\Big)

(74)

\displaystyle\indent+\frac{\pi_{a,*}(\mathbf{X})(\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})-\pi_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X}))}{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})\pi_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\Big((\phi_{a,\infty}-\phi_{a,*})(\tau_{n}^{+}|\mathbf{X})-(\phi_{a,\infty}-\phi_{a,*})(\tau_{n}^{-}|\mathbf{X})\Big)

(75)

\displaystyle\indent+\frac{\pi_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})-\pi_{a,*}(\mathbf{X})}{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})}\Big((\widehat{\phi}_{a}-\phi_{a,\infty})(\tau_{n}^{+}|\mathbf{X})-(\widehat{\phi}_{a}-\phi_{a,\infty})(\tau_{n}^{-}|\mathbf{X})\Big)\Big].

(76)

Then, the absolute values of three terms in (
74
), (
75
), (
76
) are order of
(\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})o_{p}(n^{-k/(2k+1)})
from Assumption
(E2)
,
(E5)
, and
(E6)
, and since true
p_{a}
is unimodal and
P_{*}[\phi_{a,*}(\tau_{n}^{+}|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{a,*}(\tau_{n}^{-}|\mathbf{X})]=F_{a}(\tau_{n}^{+})-F_{a}(\tau_{n}^{-})
which is
(\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})O_{p}(1)
. Thus, we have the following result for the remainder term of (
68
).

\begin{split}(\tau_{n}^{+}&-\tau_{n}^{-})P_{*}\Big((D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}})(\tau_{n}^{+})-(D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}})(\tau_{n}^{-})\Big)\\
&\asymp(\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})^{2}o_{p}(n^{-k/(2k+1)}),\end{split}

(77)

up to a constant. Since, similarly to (
73
), the random variables
M_{i}
s (
i=1,2,3)
in Assumption
(E6)
does not depend on
s_{1},\,s_{2}
, the following holds for the remainder term of (
68
).

\begin{split}P_{*}&\Big\{\int_{\bar{\tau}}^{\tau_{n}^{+}}\Big[(D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}})(y)-(D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}})(2\bar{\tau}-y)\Big]\Big\}\\
&\asymp(\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})^{2}o_{p}(n^{-k/(2k+1)}),\end{split}

(78)

up to a constant.
Hence, we obtain a result for one step estimator
\widehat{F}_{a,n}
as follows.

\displaystyle\Big|\int\Delta(y)d(\widehat{F}_{a,n}-F_{a})(y)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq 4\epsilon(\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})^{k+2}+(\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})O_{p}(n^{-(k+1)/(2k+1)})

\displaystyle\indent+(\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})^{2}o_{p}(n^{-k/(2k+1)}).

(79)

Now it remains to study the remaining term from (
66
),

\displaystyle\Big|\int\Delta(y)d(\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}-\widehat{F}_{a,n})(y)\Big|.

Analogously to the former expansion for
\widehat{F}_{a,n}-F_{a}
(see (
67
) and (
68
)), we have

\displaystyle\int\Delta(y)d(\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}-\widehat{F}_{a,n})(y)

\displaystyle=\int_{\bar{\tau}}^{\tau_{n}^{+}}\Big((\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}-\widehat{F}_{a,n})(y)-(\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}-\widehat{F}_{a,n})(2\bar{\tau}-y)\Big)dy

\displaystyle\indent-\frac{\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-}}{4}\Big[(\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}-\widehat{F}_{a,n})(\tau_{n}^{+})-(\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}-\widehat{F}_{a,n})(\tau_{n}^{-})\Big].

By a straightforward application of Lemma
12
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-}}{4}\Big[(\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}-\widehat{F}_{a,n})(\tau_{n}^{+})-(\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}-\widehat{F}_{a,n})(\tau_{n}^{-})\Big]=(\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})O_{p}(n^{-(k+1)/(2k+1)}).

(80)

Similarly, one can easily check

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\bar{\tau}}^{\tau_{n}^{+}}\Big((\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}-\widehat{F}_{a,n})(y)-(\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}-\widehat{F}_{a,n})(2\bar{\tau}-y)\Big)dy\Big|=(\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})O_{p}(n^{-(k+1)/(2k+1)}).

(81)

Hence, combining the above results (
79
), (
80
) and (
81
) with (
65
), (
66
), and plugging them altogether into (
64
), this yields

\displaystyle M_{k}p_{a}(s_{0})

\displaystyle|\varphi_{a}^{(k)}(s_{0})|(\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})^{k+2}+o_{p}((\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})^{k+2})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3(\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})}{2n}+4\epsilon(\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})^{k+2}+(\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})O_{p}(n^{-(k+1)/(2k+1)})

\displaystyle\indent+(\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})^{2}o_{p}(n^{-k/(2k+1)}).

Similarly to the proof of Lemma 4.4 in
Balabdaoui et al. (2009)
, since
\epsilon>0
is arbitrary and uniform consistency from Theorem
1
with
\varphi_{a}^{(k)}(s_{0})<0
implies
(\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})=o_{p}(1)
, this proves the claim.
∎

The following lemma is a straightforward extension of Theorem 2 in
Westling et al. (2020)
.

Lemma 12
.

Under the same assumptions for Lemma
11
, if an interval
[l_{n},u_{n}]
that contains
s_{0}
satisfies
|u_{n}-l_{n}|=o_{p}(1)
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[l_{n},u_{n}]}|\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s)-\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s)|=O_{p}(n^{-(k+1)/(2k+1)})

for
a\in\{0,1\}
.

Proof.

The proof follows the steps in Lemma 1, 2, and 3 of

Westling et al. (2020)
.
We give a sketch here.
First, Assumption
(I3)
and
(E8)
line up with the condition (i) and (ii) in Section 4.1 (see page 16) of
Westling et al. (2020)
which correspond to condition (B) and (C) therein (see page 8). Since
\delta_{n}=O_{p}(n^{-(k+1)/(2k+1)})
and we verified that

\displaystyle|(\widehat{F}_{a,n}(t)-\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s))-(F_{a}(t)-F_{a}(s))|

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon|t-s|^{k+1}+O_{p}(n^{-(k+1)/(2k+1)})

\displaystyle\indent+|t-s|o_{p}(n^{-k/(2k+1)}),

for arbitrary small
\epsilon>0
in the proof of Lemma
11
, this yields

\displaystyle\sup_{|t-s|<cw_{n}}w_{n}^{-1}|(\widehat{F}_{a.n}(t)-\widehat{F}_{a.n}(s))-(F_{a}(t)-F_{a}(s))|=O_{p}(1),

which meets the condition (A) in
Westling et al. (2020)
(see page 8), where
w_{n}=n^{-(k+1)/(2k+1)}
.
When we define
\kappa_{n}:=\sup\{|t-s|:t,s\in[L_{n},U_{n}],s\leq t,\widehat{F}_{a,n}(t)\leq\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s)\}
, then by the same procedure in Lemma 2 of
Westling et al. (2020)
, we obtain
\kappa_{n}=O(n^{-(k+1)/(2k+1)})
. This yields the conclusion with Lemma 3 in
Westling et al. (2020)
.
∎

The following lemma is analogous to Lemma 4.5 in
Balabdaoui et al. (2009)
.

Lemma 13
.

For any
T>0
, under the same assumptions as in Lemma
11
, for
a\in\{0,1\}
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{|t|\leq T}|\widehat{\varphi}^{\prime}_{a,n}(s_{0}+v_{n}t)-\varphi^{\prime}_{a}(s_{0})|=O_{p}(n^{-(k-1)/(2k+1)})

(82)

\displaystyle\sup_{|t|\leq T}|\widehat{\varphi}_{a,n}(s_{0}+v_{n}t)-\varphi_{a}(s_{0})-v_{n}t\varphi^{\prime}_{a}(s_{0})|=O_{p}(n^{-k/(2k+1)}),

(83)

where
v_{n}=n^{-1/(2k+1)}
. This implies that for
