\widehat{\varpi}_{a,n,1}(u):=\sum_{j=k+1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{j!}(\widehat{\varphi}_{a,n}(u)-\varphi_{a}(s_{0}))^{j}
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\varpi}_{a,n,1}(u)=o_{p}(n^{-k/(2k+1)}),

(84)

uniformly in
u\in[s_{0}-tn^{-1/(2k+1)},s_{0}+tn^{-1/(2k+1)}]
, where
|t|\leq T
.
Furthermore, if we define, for any
u\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\widehat{e}_{a,n}(u)=\widehat{p}_{a,n}-\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{p_{a}^{(j)}(s_{0})}{j!}(u-s_{0})^{j}-p_{a}(s_{0})\frac{[\varphi^{\prime}_{a}(s_{0})]^{k}}{k!}(u-s_{0})^{k},

then

\displaystyle\sup_{|t|\leq T}

\displaystyle|\widehat{e}_{a,n}(s_{0}+v_{n}t)-p_{a}(s_{0})(\widehat{\varphi}_{a,n}(s_{0}+v_{n}t)-\varphi_{a}(s_{0})-v_{n}t\varphi^{\prime}_{a}(s_{0}))|

(85)

\displaystyle=o_{p}(n^{-k/(2k+1)}).

(86)

Proof.

The proof for (
82
), (
83
) is straightforward extension from the proof for Lemma 4.3, and Lemma 4.4 in
Groeneboom et al. (2001b)
with (our) Lemma
11
. Then, the proof for (
84
), (
85
) is identical to the proof for Lemma 4.5 in
Balabdaoui et al. (2009)
, relying on (
82
) and (
83
).
∎

We construct local processes

\displaystyle\widehat{G}^{\rm loc}_{a,n}(t)

\displaystyle=r_{n}\int_{s_{0}}^{s_{n}(t)}\Big(\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(v)-\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s_{0})-\int_{s_{0}}^{v}\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{p_{a}^{(j)}(s_{0})}{j!}(u-s_{0})^{j}du\Big)dv,

and

\displaystyle\widehat{H}^{\rm loc}_{a,n}(t)

\displaystyle=r_{n}\int_{s_{0}}^{s_{n}(t)}\int_{s_{0}}^{v}\Big(\widehat{p}_{a,n}(u)-\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{p_{a}^{(j)}(s_{0})}{j!}(u-s_{0})^{j}\Big)dudv+\widehat{A}_{a,n}t+\widehat{B}_{a,n},

where

\displaystyle\widehat{A}_{a,n}

\displaystyle=r_{n}v_{n}(\widehat{G}_{a,n}(s_{0})-\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s_{0})),

\displaystyle\widehat{B}_{a,n}

\displaystyle=r_{n}(\widehat{H}_{a,n}(s_{0})-\widehat{H}^{c}_{a,n}(s_{0})),

where
r_{n}=n^{(k+2)/(2k+1)},v_{n}=n^{-1/(2k+1)},s_{n}(t)=s_{0}+v_{n}t
.
We further define modified processes of each
\widehat{G}^{\rm loc}_{a,n}
and
\widehat{H}^{\rm loc}_{a,n}
as follows.

\displaystyle\widehat{G}^{\rm locmod}_{a,n}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{r_{n}}{p_{a}(s_{0})}\int_{s_{0}}^{s_{n}(t)}\Big(\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(v)-\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s_{0})-\int_{s_{0}}^{v}\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{p_{a}^{(j)}(s_{0})}{j!}(u-s_{0})^{j}du\Big)dv

\displaystyle\indent-r_{n}\int_{s_{0}}^{s_{n}(t)}\int_{s_{0}}^{v}\widehat{\varpi}_{a,n}(u)dudv

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p_{a}(s_{0})}\widehat{G}^{\rm loc}_{a,n}(t)-r_{n}\int_{s_{0}}^{s_{n}(t)}\int_{s_{0}}^{v}\widehat{\varpi}_{a,n}(u)dudv,

and

\displaystyle\widehat{H}^{\rm locmod}_{a,n}(t)

\displaystyle=r_{n}\int_{s_{0}}^{s_{n}(t)}\int_{s_{0}}^{v}(\widehat{\varphi}_{a,n}(u)-\varphi_{a}(s_{0})-(u-s_{0})\varphi^{\prime}_{a}(s_{0}))dudv+\frac{\widehat{A}_{a,n}t+\widehat{B}_{a,n}}{p_{a}(s_{0})}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p_{a}(s_{0})}\widehat{H}^{\rm loc}_{a,n}(t)-r_{n}\int_{s_{0}}^{s_{n}(t)}\int_{s_{0}}^{v}\widehat{\varpi}_{a,n}(u)dudv,

and

\displaystyle\widehat{\varpi}_{a,n}(u)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{j!}[\widehat{\varphi}_{a,n}(u)-\varphi_{a}(s_{0})]^{j}-\sum_{j=2}^{k-1}\frac{[\varphi^{\prime}_{a}(s_{0})]^{j}}{j!}(u-s_{0})^{j}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\varpi}_{a,n,1}+\sum_{j=2}^{k}\frac{1}{j!}[\widehat{\varphi}_{a,n}(u)-\varphi_{a}(s_{0})]^{j}-\sum_{j=2}^{k-1}\frac{[\varphi^{\prime}_{a}(s_{0})]^{j}}{j!}(u-s_{0})^{j}.

We let
W
denote a standard two-sided Brownian motion starting at
0
. For each
t\in\mathbb{R},k\in\mathbb{N}
, we defined the integrated Gaussian process with drift
Y_{k}
as

\displaystyle Y_{k}(t):=\begin{cases}\int_{0}^{t}W(s)ds-t^{k+2},&{\rm if~}t\geq 0,\\
\int_{t}^{0}W(s)ds-t^{k+2},&{\rm if~}t<0.\end{cases}

(87)

The invelope process
H_{k}
of
Y_{k}
is then the unique process satisfying:

\begin{gathered}H_{k}(t)\leq Y_{k}(t)\text{ for all }t\in\mathbb{R},\\
\int\left[H_{k}(t)-Y_{k}(t)\right]\,dH^{(3)}_{k}(t)=0,\text{ and}\\
H_{k}^{(2)}\text{ is concave}.\end{gathered}

(88)

We study the asymptotic behavior of localized processes (at the ‘log density level’) in Lemma
14
.

Lemma 14
.

Let
T>0
. Under the same assumptions as in Lemma
11
, the following holds for
a\in\{0,1\}
.

(a)

\widehat{G}^{\rm locmod}_{a,n}
converges weakly in
C[-T,T]
to the driving process
G_{\alpha_{a},k,\sigma_{a}}
, where

\displaystyle G_{\alpha_{a},k,\sigma_{a}}(t):=\alpha_{a}\int_{0}^{t}W(s)ds-\sigma_{a}t^{k+2},

(89)

for
\alpha_{a}=\frac{1}{p_{a}(s_{0})}\sqrt{{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\frac{\pi_{a,*}(\mathbf{X})}{\pi^{2}_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\eta_{a,*}(s_{0}|\mathbf{X})\Big]}
,
\sigma_{a}=|\varphi_{a}^{(k)}(s_{0})|/(k+2)!
.

(b)

The following inequality holds.

\displaystyle\widehat{G}^{\rm locmod}_{a,n}(t)-\widehat{H}^{\rm locmod}_{a,n}(t)\geq 0,

for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
. And, equality holds for all
t
such that
s_{n}(t)\in\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{a}
.

(c)

Both
\widehat{A}_{a,n},\widehat{B}_{a,n}
are tight.

(d)

The vector of processes

\displaystyle(\widehat{H}^{\rm locmod}_{a,n},(\widehat{H}^{\rm locmod}_{a,n})^{(1)},(\widehat{H}^{\rm locmod}_{a,n})^{(2)},\widehat{G}^{\rm locmod}_{a,n},(\widehat{H}^{\rm locmod}_{a,n})^{(3)},(\widehat{G}^{\rm locmod}_{a,n})^{(1)})

converges weakly in
(C[-T,T])^{4}\times(D[-T,T])^{2}
, endowed with the product topology induced by the uniform topology on the spaces
C[-T,T]
and the
M_{1}
Skorohod topology on the spaces
D[-T,T]
, to the process

\displaystyle(H_{\alpha_{a},k,\sigma_{a}},(H_{\alpha_{a},k,\sigma_{a}})^{(1)},H_{\alpha_{a},k,\sigma_{a}}^{(2)},Y_{\alpha_{a},k,\sigma_{a}},(H_{\alpha_{a},k,\sigma_{a}})^{(3)},(Y_{\alpha_{a},k,\sigma_{a}})^{(1)}),

where
H_{\alpha_{a},k,\sigma_{a}}
is the unique process on
\mathbb{R}
that satisfies

\displaystyle\begin{cases}H_{\alpha_{a},k,\sigma_{a}}(t)\leq Y_{\alpha_{a},k,\sigma_{a}}(t)&for~all~t\in\mathbb{R},\\
\int(H_{\alpha_{a},k,\sigma_{a}}(t)-Y_{\alpha_{a},k,\sigma_{a}}(t))dH^{(3)}_{\alpha_{a},k,\sigma_{a}}(t)=0,&\\
H^{(2)}_{\alpha_{a},k,\sigma_{a}}&is~concave.\end{cases}

(90)

To allow multiple jumps to approximate a single jump in
(\widehat{H}^{\rm locmod}_{a,n})^{(3)}
and
(\widehat{G}^{\rm locmod}_{a,n})^{(1)}
, we use the
M_{1}
topology on the space
D[-T,T]
instead of
J_{1}
topology (see Chapter 12 of
Billingsley, 2013
for the definition of the
J_{1}
topology) which was used in Theorem 4.6 of
Balabdaoui et al. (2009)
. The
M_{1}
topology is defined in Section 12.3 of
Whitt (2002)
, and its separability and completeness are thoroughly proved in Lemma 8.22 and Proposition 8.23 of
Doss and Wellner (2019c)
.

Proof.

For the first part
(a)
, by a straightforward application of the proof of Theorem 4.6 in
Balabdaoui et al. (2009)
in combination with the results from Lemma
13
, we have

\displaystyle r_{n}\int_{s_{0}}^{s_{n}(t)}\int_{s_{0}}^{v}\widehat{\varpi}_{a,n}(u)dudv=\frac{[\varphi_{a}(s_{0})]^{k}}{(k+2)!}t^{k+2}+o_{p}(1).

Then, by Lemma
13
, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{G}^{\rm locmod}_{a,n}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{r_{n}}{p_{a}(s_{0})}\int_{s_{0}}^{s_{n}(t)}\Big(\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(v)-\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s_{0})-\int_{s_{0}}^{v}\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{p_{a}^{(j)}(s_{0})}{j!}(u-s_{0})^{j}du\Big)dv

\displaystyle\indent-\frac{[\varphi_{a}(s_{0})]^{k}}{(k+2)!}t^{k+2}+o_{p}(1).

In addition, the display above further yields

\begin{split}\widehat{G}^{\rm locmod}_{a,n}(t)&=\frac{r_{n}}{p_{a}(s_{0})}\int_{s_{0}}^{s_{n}(t)}\Big(\widehat{F}_{a,n}(v)-\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s_{0})-\int_{s_{0}}^{v}\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{p_{a}^{(j)}(s_{0})}{j!}(u-s_{0})^{j}du\Big)dv\\
&\indent+\frac{r_{n}}{p_{a}(s_{0})}\int_{s_{0}}^{s_{n}(t)}\Big((\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(v)-\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s_{0}))-(\widehat{F}_{a,n}(v)-\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s_{0}))\Big)dv\\
&\indent-\frac{[\varphi_{a}(s_{0})]^{k}}{(k+2)!}t^{k+2}+o_{p}(1)\\
&=\frac{r_{n}}{p_{a}(s_{0})}\int_{s_{0}}^{s_{n}(t)}\Big((\widehat{F}_{a,n}(v)-\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s_{0}))-(F_{a}(v)-F_{a}(s_{0}))\Big)dv\\
&\indent+\frac{p_{a}^{(k)}(s_{0})}{(k+2)!p_{a}(s_{0})}t^{k+2}-\frac{[\varphi_{a}(s_{0})]^{k}}{(k+2)!}t^{k+2}+o_{p}(1),\end{split}

(91)

where the last equality arises from Lemma
17
. Moreover, by Lemma
10
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}^{(k)}(s_{0})}{(k+2)!p_{a}(s_{0})}t^{k+2}-\frac{[\varphi_{a}(s_{0})]^{k}}{(k+2)!}t^{k+2}=\frac{\varphi_{a}^{(k)}(s_{0})}{(k+2)!}t^{k+2}.

With the decomposition
\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s)-F_{a}(s)={\mathbb{P}}_{n}D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(s)-P_{*}D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(s)
, the preceding displays yield

\displaystyle\widehat{G}^{\rm locmod}_{a,n}(t)

\displaystyle=({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})\Big[\frac{r_{n}}{p_{a}(s_{0})}\int_{s_{0}}^{s_{n}(t)}(D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(v)-D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(s_{0}))dv\Big]+\frac{\varphi_{a}^{(k)}(s_{0})}{(k+2)!}t^{k+2}+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle\indent+P_{*}\Big[\frac{r_{n}}{p_{a}(s_{0})}\int_{s_{0}}^{s_{n}(t)}\Big((D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}})(v)-(D_{a,\hat{\theta}_{a}}-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}})(s_{0})\Big)dv\Big].

In addition, we verified

\displaystyle P_{*}\Big[\frac{r_{n}}{p_{a}(s_{0})}\int_{s_{0}}^{s_{n}(t)}\Big((D_{a,\hat{\theta}_{a}}-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}})(v)-(D_{a,\hat{\theta}_{a}}-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}})(s_{0})\Big)dv\Big]=o_{p}(1),

in the proof of Lemma
11
(see (
77
), (
78
)). Thus, we finally obtain

\displaystyle\widehat{G}^{\rm locmod}_{a,n}(t)

\displaystyle=({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})\Big[\frac{r_{n}}{p_{a}(s_{0})}\int_{s_{0}}^{s_{n}(t)}(D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(v)-D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(s_{0}))dv\Big]+\frac{\varphi_{a}^{(k)}(s_{0})}{(k+2)!}t^{k+2}+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle\indent+P_{*}\Big[\frac{r_{n}}{p_{a}(s_{0})}\int_{s_{0}}^{s_{n}(t)}\Big((D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}})(v)-(D_{a,\hat{\theta}_{a}}-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}})(s_{0})\Big)dv\Big]

which is equal to

\begin{split}&({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})\Big[\frac{r_{n}}{p_{a}(s_{0})}\int_{s_{0}}^{s_{n}(t)}(D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(v)-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0}))dv\Big]+\frac{\varphi_{a}^{(k)}(s_{0})}{(k+2)!}t^{k+2}+o_{p}(1)\\
&\indent+({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})\Big[\frac{r_{n}}{p_{a}(s_{0})}\int_{s_{0}}^{s_{n}(t)}(D_{a,\hat{\theta}_{a}}(v)-D_{a,\hat{\theta}_{a}}(s_{0}))-(D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(v)-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0}))dv\Big]\\
&=({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})\Big[\frac{r_{n}}{p_{a}(s_{0})}\int_{s_{0}}^{s_{n}(t)}(D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(v)-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0}))dv\Big]+\frac{\varphi_{a}^{(k)}(s_{0})}{(k+2)!}t^{k+2}+o_{p}(1),\end{split}

where we used Lemma
18
in the last equality.

Now, we study the convergence of the term

\displaystyle({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})\Big[\frac{r_{n}}{p_{a}(s_{0})}\int_{s_{0}}^{s_{n}(t)}(D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(v)-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0}))dv\Big],

by Theorem 2.11.24 in
van der Vaart and Wellner (2023)
(see Proposition
2
). To line up with the assumption that Proposition
2
requires, assuming
t\geq 0
without loss of generality, we first define the function class

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{n}:=\Big\{\sqrt{v_{n}^{-1}}\Big(D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0}+v_{n}t)-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0})\Big):t\in[-T,T]\Big\}.

Since we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{v_{n}^{-1}}\Big(D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0}+v_{n}t)-D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(s_{0})\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{I(A=a)}{\pi_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\left(I(Y\leq s_{0}+v_{n}t)-I(Y\leq s_{0})\right)

\displaystyle\indent+\Big(1-\frac{I(A=a)}{\pi_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\Big)(\phi_{a,\infty}(s_{0}+v_{n}t|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{a,\infty}(s_{0}|\mathbf{X})),

the class
\mathcal{F}_{n}
has an envelope which is given by

\displaystyle F_{\rm env,n}:

\displaystyle=\sqrt{v_{n}^{-1}}\Big(KI(A=a)I(s_{0}-v_{n}T<Y\leq s_{0}+v_{n}T)

\displaystyle\indent+(K+1)(\phi_{a,\infty}(s_{0}+v_{n}T|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{a,\infty}(s_{0}-v_{n}T|\mathbf{X}))\Big),

and so

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}F_{\rm env,n}^{2}=O(1),

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}F_{\rm env,n}^{2}I(F_{\rm env}\geq\eta\sqrt{n})\rightarrow 0,

for arbitrary
\eta>0
. In addition, we have

\displaystyle\Big(D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0}+v_{n}t_{1})-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0})\Big)-\Big(D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0}+v_{n}t_{2})-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0})\Big)

\displaystyle=D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0}+v_{n}t_{1})-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0}+v_{n}t_{2})

\displaystyle=\Big(1-\frac{I(A=a)}{\pi_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\Big)(\phi_{a,\infty}(u_{1}|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{a,\infty}(u_{2}|\mathbf{X}))

\displaystyle\indent+\frac{I(A=a)}{\pi_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}I(u_{2}<Y\leq u_{1}),

where
u_{i}=s_{0}+v_{n}t_{i}
for
i\in\{1,2\}
. Then, for some
C_{2}>0
,

\displaystyle P_{*}\Big[\Big(D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0}+v_{n}t_{1})-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0})\Big)-\Big(D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0}+v_{n}t_{2})-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0})\Big)\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2P_{*}\Big[\frac{I(A=a)}{\pi^{2}_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}I(u_{2}<Y\leq u_{1})\Big]

\displaystyle\indent+2P_{*}\Big[\Big(1-\frac{I(A=a)}{\pi_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\Big)^{2}(\phi_{a,\infty}(u_{1}|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{a,\infty}(u_{2}|\mathbf{X}))^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}K^{2}v_{n}(t_{1}-t_{2})+(K+1)^{2}(t_{2}-t_{1})^{2}v_{n}^{2},

where a similar reasoning to the proof of Theorem
1
is applied with Assumption
(E5)
. Thus, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{|t_{2}-t_{1}|<\delta_{n}}v_{n}^{-1}

\displaystyle P_{*}\Big[\Big(D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0}+v_{n}t_{1})-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0})\Big)-\Big(D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0}+v_{n}t_{2})-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0})\Big)\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle\rightarrow 0,

as
\delta_{n}\rightarrow 0
. Furthermore, following the steps in the proof of Lemma
11
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{Q}\int_{0}^{\delta_{n}}\sqrt{{\rm log}N(\epsilon\|F_{\rm env,n}\|_{Q,2},\mathcal{F}_{n},L_{2}(Q))d\epsilon}\rightarrow 0,

for every
\delta_{n}\rightarrow 0
. The last step to apply Proposition
2
is studying the limiting covariance structure. Indeed, for
t_{1},t_{2}>s_{0}
defined with
u_{1},u_{2}
as above,

\displaystyle v_{n}^{-1}P_{*}(D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(u_{1})-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0}))(D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(u_{2})-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0}))

\displaystyle=v_{n}^{-1}P_{*}\Big[\frac{I(A=a)}{\pi^{2}_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}I(s_{0}<Y\leq u_{m})

(92)

\displaystyle\indent+\frac{I(A=a)}{\pi_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\Big(1-\frac{I(A=a)}{\pi_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\Big)I(s_{0}<Y\leq u_{1})(\phi_{a,\infty}(u_{2}|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{a,\infty}(s_{0}|\mathbf{X}))

(93)

\displaystyle\indent+\frac{I(A=a)}{\pi_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\Big(1-\frac{I(A=a)}{\pi_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\Big)I(s_{0}<Y\leq u_{2})(\phi_{a,\infty}(u_{1}|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{a,\infty}(s_{0}|\mathbf{X}))

(94)

\displaystyle\indent+\Big(1-\frac{I(A=a)}{\pi_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\Big)^{2}(\phi_{a,\infty}(u_{1}|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{a,\infty}(s_{0}|\mathbf{X}))(\phi_{a,\infty}(u_{2}|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{a,\infty}(s_{0}|\mathbf{X}))\Big],

(95)

where
u_{m}=\min\{u_{1},u_{2}\}
. Let
t_{m}=\min\{t_{1},t_{2}\}
. For the first term (
92
), we have

\displaystyle v_{n}^{-1}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\frac{I(A=a)}{\pi_{a,\infty}^{2}(\mathbf{X})}

\displaystyle I(s_{0}<Y\leq u_{m})\Big]

\displaystyle=v_{n}^{-1}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\frac{I(A=a)}{\pi^{2}_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}I(s_{0}<Y\leq u_{m})\Big|\mathbf{X},A\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle=v_{n}^{-1}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{\pi^{2}_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}{\mathbb{E}}\Big(I(A=a)I(s_{0}<Y^{a}\leq s_{0}+v_{n}t_{m})\Big|\mathbf{X},A\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\rightarrow t_{m}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\frac{\pi_{a,*}(\mathbf{X})}{\pi^{2}_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\eta_{a,*}(s_{0}|\mathbf{X})\Big],

due to the Assumptions
(I1)
,
(I2)
,
(E2)
, and the Lebesgue Dominated convergence Theorem. Next, the second term (
93
) converges to
0
, since

\displaystyle v_{n}^{-1}{\mathbb{E}}

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{I(A=a)}{\pi_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\Big(1-\frac{I(A=a)}{\pi_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\Big)I(s_{0}<Y\leq u_{1})(\phi_{a,\infty}(u_{2}|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{a,\infty}(s_{0}|\mathbf{X}))\Big|

\displaystyle\leq v_{n}^{-1}K(K+1){\mathbb{E}}\Big|I(s_{0}<Y^{a}\leq s_{0}+v_{n}t_{1})(\phi_{a,\infty}(u_{2}|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{a,\infty}(s_{0}|\mathbf{X}))\Big|

\displaystyle\leq v_{n}^{-1}K(K+1)\sqrt{{\mathbb{E}}I(s_{0}<Y^{a}\leq s_{0}+v_{n}t_{1})}\sqrt{{\mathbb{E}}R^{2}(\mathbf{X})}|v_{n}t_{2}|

\displaystyle\leq K(K+1)|t_{2}|\sqrt{{\mathbb{E}}R^{2}(\mathbf{X})}\sqrt{{\mathbb{E}}I(s_{0}<Y^{a}\leq s_{0}+v_{n}t_{1})}\rightarrow 0,

by a similar reasoning as above with the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and Assumption
(E5)
. Identical procedure yields that the third term (
94
) is also vanishing. Finally, again by Assumption
(E5)
, we have

\displaystyle v_{n}^{-1}{\mathbb{E}}

\displaystyle\Big[\Big(1-\frac{I(A=a)}{\pi_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\Big)^{2}(\phi_{a,\infty}(u_{1}|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{a,\infty}(s_{0}|\mathbf{X}))(\phi_{a,\infty}(u_{2}|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{a,\infty}(s_{0}|\mathbf{X}))\Big]

\displaystyle\leq v_{n}|t_{1}t_{2}|(1+K)^{2}{\mathbb{E}}R^{2}(\mathbf{X})\rightarrow 0,

for the fourth term (
95
). On the other hand, when
t_{1}>s_{0}>t_{2}
, the first term (
92
) is now

\displaystyle-v_{n}^{-1}P_{*}\left[\frac{I(A=a)}{\pi_{a,\infty}^{2}(\mathbf{X})}I(s_{0}<Y\leq u_{1})I(u_{2}<Y\leq s_{0})\right]=0.

Obviously, the other terms (
93
), (
94
), (
95
) are still vanishing.
Similarly, since

\displaystyle v_{n}^{-1/2}P_{*}(D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0}+v_{n}t)-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0}))\rightarrow 0

(96)

holds for each
t\in[-T,T]
,
it is straightforward that

\displaystyle v_{n}^{-1}P_{*}(D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(u_{2})-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0}))P_{*}(D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(u_{1})-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0}))\rightarrow 0.

Thus, for each
T
, by Proposition
2
,

\displaystyle({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})\Big[\frac{r_{n}}{p_{a}(s_{0})}\int_{s_{0}}^{s_{n}(t)}(D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(v)-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0}))dv\Big]\rightarrow_{d}\alpha_{a}\int_{0}^{t}W(v)dv,

in
C[-T,T]
with
\alpha_{a}=\frac{1}{p_{a}(s_{0})}\sqrt{{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\frac{\pi_{a,*}(\mathbf{X})}{\pi^{2}_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\eta_{a,*}(s_{0}|\mathbf{X})\Big]}
.

The part
(b)
can be proved identically to the proof for Lemma 4.6-(ii) in
Balabdaoui et al. (2009)
. Furthermore, part
(c)
can easily be check with a slight modification of the proof Lemma 4.6-(iii) in
Balabdaoui et al. (2009)
. Their
\widehat{F}_{n},{\mathbb{F}}_{n},x_{0}
are identified with our
\widehat{G}_{a,n},\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n},s_{0}
, respectively. The terms
\widehat{A}_{n1},\widehat{A}_{n2}
in
Balabdaoui et al. (2009)
are technically the same, but our
\widehat{A}_{n3}
is

\displaystyle\widehat{A}_{n3}:=r_{n}s_{n}\Big|\int_{\tau}^{s_{0}}d(\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}-F_{a})\Big|.

But, indeed, the perturbation of
\Delta(x)=I_{[\tau,s_{0}]}(x)
also yields the same result as theirs with a similar reasoning to Lemma
11
with
9
.

Finally, the proof of the last part
(d)
follows identical steps to that of Theorem 6.2 in
Groeneboom et al. (2001b)
regarding the least squares estimator (or Lemma 8.24–Lemma 8.27 of
Doss and Wellner (2019c)
).
∎

We state and prove the joint convergence result of the vectors of processes defined in
(d)
of Lemma
14
for
a=0,1
that serves as an intermediate result to show that asymptotic independence between
(\widehat{p}_{1,n}(s_{0})-p_{1}(s_{0}))
and
(\widehat{p}_{0,n}(s_{0})-p_{0}(s_{0}))
.

Lemma 15
.

Let
T>0
and

\displaystyle V_{a,n}:=(\widehat{H}^{\rm locmod}_{a,n},(\widehat{H}^{\rm locmod}_{a,n})^{(1)},(\widehat{H}^{\rm locmod}_{a,n})^{(2)},\widehat{G}^{\rm locmod}_{a,n},(\widehat{H}^{\rm locmod}_{a,n})^{(3)},(\widehat{G}^{\rm locmod}_{a,n})^{(1)}),

(97)

\displaystyle V_{a}:=(H_{\alpha_{a},k,\sigma_{a}},(H_{\alpha_{a},k,\sigma_{a}})^{(1)},H_{\alpha_{a},k,\sigma_{a}}^{(2)},Y_{\alpha_{a},k,\sigma_{a}},(H_{\alpha_{a},k,\sigma_{a}})^{(3)},(Y_{\alpha_{a},k,\sigma_{a}})^{(1)}),

(98)

where
H_{\alpha_{a},k,\sigma_{a}}
is the unique process defined in (
90
), for
a\in\{0,1\}
.
Then the vector of processes
(V_{1,n},V_{0,n})
converges weakly in
(C[-T,T])^{4}\times(D[-T,T])^{2}\times(C[-T,T])^{4}\times(D[-T,T])^{2}
to the process
(V_{1},V_{0})
, where
V_{1}
and
V_{0}
are independent vectors of processes.

Proof.

Stacking the vectors of processes
V_{1,n}
and
V_{0,n}
and following the same derivation in Lemma
14
, one can easily check the joint convergence holds.
Now it suffices to check the independence of two limit vectors of processes,
V_{1}
and
V_{0}
.
Recall that
r_{n}=n^{(k+2)/(2k+1)}
.
In the proof of Lemma
14
, we verified

\displaystyle\widehat{G}^{\rm locmod}_{a,n}(t)

\displaystyle=({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})\Big[\frac{r_{n}}{p_{a}(s_{0})}\int_{s_{0}}^{s_{n}(t)}(D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(v)-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0}))dv\Big]+\frac{\varphi_{a}^{(k)}(s_{0})}{(k+2)!}t^{k+2}+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle\rightarrow_{d}\alpha_{a}\int_{0}^{t}W_{a}(v)dv+\frac{\varphi_{a}^{(k)}(s_{0})}{(k+2)!}t^{k+2},

for
a=0,\,1
.
To prove the asymptotic independence of
\widehat{G}^{\rm locmod}_{1,n}
and
\widehat{G}^{\rm locmod}_{0,n}
, it suffices to show that the limiting covariance of
\widehat{G}^{\rm locmod}_{1,n}(t_{1})
and
\widehat{G}^{\rm locmod}_{0,n}(t_{0})
is zero for any
(t_{1},\,t_{0})\in[-T,T]^{2}
.
Recall that
v_{n}=n^{-1/(2k+1)}
. We now check, for any
(t_{1},\,t_{0})\in[-T,T]^{2}
,

\displaystyle v_{n}^{-1}P_{*}(D_{1,{\theta_{1,\infty}}}(u_{1})-D_{1,{\theta_{1,\infty}}}(s_{0}))(D_{0,{\theta_{0,\infty}}}(u_{0})-D_{0,{\theta_{0,\infty}}}(s_{0}))\rightarrow 0,

(99)

\displaystyle v_{n}^{-1/2}P_{*}(D_{1,{\theta_{1,\infty}}}(u_{1})-D_{1,{\theta_{1,\infty}}}(s_{0}))\rightarrow 0,

(100)

\displaystyle v_{n}^{-1/2}P_{*}(D_{0,{\theta_{1,\infty}}}(u_{0})-D_{0,{\theta_{0,\infty}}}(s_{0}))\rightarrow 0,

(101)

where
u_{i}=s_{0}+v_{n}t_{i}
, for
i=0,1
.
We already verified (
100
) and (
101
) hold in the proof of Lemma
14
(see (
96
)).
Now we prove (
99
).
Without loss of generality we assume
t_{1},\,t_{0}\geq 0
. We have

\begin{split}&v_{n}^{-1}P_{*}(D_{1,{\theta_{1,\infty}}}(u_{1})-D_{1,{\theta_{1,\infty}}}(s_{0}))(D_{0,{\theta_{0,\infty}}}(u_{0})-D_{0,{\theta_{0,\infty}}}(s_{0}))\\
&=v_{n}^{-1}P_{*}\Big[\frac{I(A=1)}{\pi_{1,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\Big(1-\frac{I(A=0)}{\pi_{0,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\Big)I(s_{0}<Y\leq u_{1})(\phi_{0,\infty}(u_{0}|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{0,\infty}(s_{0}|\mathbf{X}))\\
&\indent+\frac{I(A=0)}{\pi_{0,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\Big(1-\frac{I(A=1)}{\pi_{1,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\Big)I(s_{0}<Y\leq u_{0})(\phi_{1,\infty}(u_{1}|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{a,\infty}(s_{0}|\mathbf{X}))\\
&\indent+\Big(1-\frac{I(A=1)}{\pi_{1,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\Big)\Big(1-\frac{I(A=0)}{\pi_{0,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\Big)(\phi_{1,\infty}(u_{1}|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{1,\infty}(s_{0}|\mathbf{X}))(\phi_{0,\infty}(u_{0}|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{0,\infty}(s_{0}|\mathbf{X}))\Big],\end{split}

since
I(A=1)I(A=0)=0
.
The first term is
o(1)
, since

\displaystyle v_{n}^{-1}{\mathbb{E}}

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{I(A=1)}{\pi_{1,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\Big(1-\frac{I(A=0)}{\pi_{0,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\Big)I(s_{0}<Y\leq u_{1})(\phi_{0,\infty}(u_{0}|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{0,\infty}(s_{0}|\mathbf{X}))\Big|

\displaystyle\leq v_{n}^{-1}K(K+1)\sqrt{{\mathbb{E}}I(s_{0}<Y^{1}\leq s_{0}+v_{n}t_{1})}\sqrt{{\mathbb{E}}R^{2}(\mathbf{X})}|v_{n}t_{0}|

\displaystyle\leq K(K+1)|t_{0}|\sqrt{{\mathbb{E}}R^{2}(\mathbf{X})}\sqrt{{\mathbb{E}}I(s_{0}<Y^{1}\leq s_{0}+v_{n}t_{1})}\rightarrow 0,

by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and Assumption
(E5)
.
Similar reasoning can be applied to show that the second term converges to
0
.
Lastly, we study the third term. Indeed, we have

\displaystyle v_{n}^{-1}{\mathbb{E}}

\displaystyle\Big[\Big(1-\frac{I(A=1)}{\pi_{1,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\Big)\Big(1-\frac{I(A=0)}{\pi_{0,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\Big)(\phi_{1,\infty}(u_{1}|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{1,\infty}(s_{0}|\mathbf{X}))(\phi_{0,\infty}(u_{0}|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{0,\infty}(s_{0}|\mathbf{X}))\Big]

\displaystyle\leq v_{n}|t_{1}t_{0}|(1+K)^{2}{\mathbb{E}}R^{2}(\mathbf{X})\rightarrow 0.

This verifies the asymptotic independence of
\widehat{G}_{1,n}^{\rm locmod}
and
\widehat{G}_{0,n}^{\rm locmod}
.
In addition, their independent limit processes
Y_{\sigma_{1},k,\sigma_{1}}
and
Y_{\sigma_{0},k,\sigma_{0}}
uniquely characterize
H_{\sigma_{1},k,\sigma_{1}}
and
H_{\sigma_{0},k,\sigma_{0}}
, respectively.
Thus,
V_{1}
and
V_{0}
are independent, and it concludes the statement of the lemma.
∎

We state and prove the following lemma to confirm that the isotonic correction of the one-step estimator has a negligible impact on the limit distribution of the log-concave MLE
\widehat{p}_{a,n}
. Lemma
16
is used in the proof of Lemma
17
, which in turn was used in the proof of Lemma
14
above.

Lemma 16
.

Let
\mathcal{E}_{s_{0},n}:=[s_{0}-Tv_{n},s_{0}+Tv_{n}]\subset\mathcal{I}_{s_{0},\omega}
for sufficiently large
n
, arbitrary
T>0
, and
v_{n}=n^{-1/(2k+1)}
. Then under the same assumptions as in Lemma
11
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{v_{1},v_{2}\in\mathcal{E}_{s_{0},n}}\Big|(\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(v_{2})-\widehat{F}_{a,n}(v_{2}))-(\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(v_{1})-\widehat{F}_{a,n}(v_{1}))\Big|=o_{p}(n^{-(k+1)/(2k+1)}),

for
a\in\{0,1\}
.

Proof.

Let
U_{m}:=[m,\infty)
and
~L_{m}:=(-\infty,m]
for any
m\in\mathcal{S}_{n}
. Then, similar to the proof of Lemma 3 in
Westling et al. (2020)
, we can define
L_{m}^{*}\in\operatorname*{arg\ min}_{L\in\mathcal{L}_{m}}\bar{\widehat{F}}_{a,n}(U_{m}\cap L),~U_{m}^{*}\in\operatorname*{arg\ max}_{U\in\mathcal{U}_{m}}\bar{\widehat{F}}_{a,n}(L_{m}\cap U)
, where
\mathcal{L}_{m}:=\{(-\infty,v]:v\geq m\},~\mathcal{U}_{m}:=\{[v,\infty):v\leq m\}
, and
\bar{\widehat{F}}_{a,n}(A):=|A|^{-1}\sum_{v\in A}\widehat{F}_{a,n}(v)
for
A\subseteq\mathcal{S}_{n}
, and
|A|
is the cardinality of
A
. Analogous to their proof, we have, for
t,s\in\mathcal{S}_{n}\cap I_{s_{0},\omega}
,

\displaystyle\bar{\widehat{F}}_{a,n}(L_{t}^{*}\cap U_{t})-\bar{\widehat{F}}_{a,n}(L_{s}\cap U_{s}^{*})\leq\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(t)-\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s)\leq\bar{\widehat{F}}_{a,n}(L_{t}\cap U_{t}^{*})-\bar{\widehat{F}}_{a,n}(L_{s}^{*}\cap U_{s}),

where the lengths of the intervals
L_{v}^{*}\cap U_{v},~L_{v}\cap U_{v}^{*}
for
v=s,t
are bounded above by
\kappa_{n}
(which is defined in the proof of Lemma
12
and is the supremum length of an interval on which
\widehat{F}_{a,n}
is decreasing rather than increasing). Hence, letting
t_{n}^{*}:={\rm argmax}_{x\in L_{t}\cap U_{t}^{*}\cap\mathcal{S}_{n}}\widehat{F}_{a,n}(x)
and
s_{n}^{*}:={\rm argmin}_{x\in L_{s}^{*}\cap U_{s}\cap\mathcal{S}_{n}}\widehat{F}_{a,n}(x)
(note that the [finite] endpoints defining
U^{*}_{m}
and
L^{*}_{m}
are elements of
\mathcal{S}_{n}
by the definition of
\bar{\widehat{F}}_{a,n}
, so intersecting with
\mathcal{S}_{n}
in the definitions of
t^{*}_{n}
and
s^{*}_{n}
does not change anything), we have

\displaystyle\Big(\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(t)-\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s)\Big)

\displaystyle-\Big(\widehat{F}_{a,n}(t)-\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\bar{\widehat{F}}_{a,n}(L_{t}\cap U_{t}^{*})-\bar{\widehat{F}}_{a,n}(L_{s}^{*}\cap U_{s})-\Big(\widehat{F}_{a,n}(t)-\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s)\Big)

which is bounded above by

\displaystyle\Big(\widehat{F}_{a,n}(t_{n}^{*})-\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s_{n}^{*})\Big)-\Big(\widehat{F}_{a,n}(t)-\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s)\Big)

\displaystyle=\Big\{\Big[\Big(\widehat{F}_{a,n}(t_{n}^{*})-\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s_{n}^{*})\Big)-\Big(F_{a}(t_{n}^{*})-F_{a}(s_{n}^{*})\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\indent-\Big[\Big(\widehat{F}_{a,n}(t)-\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s)\Big)-\Big(F_{a}(t)-F_{a}(s)\Big)\Big]\Big\}

\displaystyle\indent+\Big(F_{a}(t_{n}^{*})-F_{a}(s_{n}^{*})\Big)-\Big(F_{a}(t)-F_{a}(s)\Big).

Similarly, one can derive the analogous lower bound for the term

\displaystyle\Big(\bar{\widehat{F}}_{a,n}(L_{t}^{*}\cap U_{t})-\bar{\widehat{F}}_{a,n}(L_{s}\cap U_{s}^{*})\Big)-\Big(\widehat{F}_{a,n}(t)

\displaystyle-\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s)\Big).

This further yields, when we define
\mathcal{W}:=\mathcal{S}_{n}\cap\mathcal{E}_{s_{0},n}
and
\mathcal{I}_{v}^{+}:=[v,v+\kappa_{n}]
,
\mathcal{I}_{v}^{-}:=[v-\kappa_{n},v]
,
\mathcal{I}_{v}:=[v-\kappa_{n},v+\kappa_{n}]
for any
v\in I_{s_{0},\omega}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{s,t\in\mathcal{W}}\Big|\Big(\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(t)-\widehat{F}_{a,n}(t)\Big)-\Big(\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s)-\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s)\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 2\sup_{s,t\in\mathcal{E}_{s_{0},n}{~\rm and~}t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{t},s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{s}}\Big\{\Big|\Big[\Big(\widehat{F}_{a,n}(t^{\prime})-F_{a}(t^{\prime})\Big)-\Big(\widehat{F}_{a,n}(t)-F_{a}(t)\Big)\Big]\Big|

(102)

\displaystyle\qquad\indent~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~-\Big[\Big(\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s^{\prime})-F_{a}(s^{\prime})\Big)-\Big(\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s)-F_{a}(s)\Big)\Big]\Big|\Big\}

(103)

\displaystyle\qquad\indent+\sup_{s,t\in\mathcal{E}_{s_{0},n}{~\rm and~}t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{t}^{-},s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{s}^{+}}\Big|\Big(F_{a}(t^{\prime})-F_{a}(t)\Big)-\Big(F_{a}(s^{\prime})-F_{a}(s)\Big)\Big|

(104)

\displaystyle\qquad\indent+\sup_{s,t\in\mathcal{E}_{s_{0},n}{~\rm and~}t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{t}^{+},s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{s}^{-}}\Big|\Big(F_{a}(t^{\prime})-F_{a}(t)\Big)-\Big(F_{a}(s^{\prime})-F_{a}(s)\Big)\Big|.

(105)

Furthermore, since
\varphi_{a}
is at least twice continuously differentiable in
\mathcal{I}_{s_{0},\omega}
, this implies that
F_{a}
is increasing and at least three times continuously differentiable in
\mathcal{I}_{s_{0},\omega}
, this yields the terms (
104
), (
105
) above are of
O_{p}(n^{-(k+2)/(2k+1)})
, since
|t^{\prime}-t|,|s^{\prime}-s|\leq\kappa_{n}=O_{p}(n^{-(k+1)/(2k+1)})
and
\sup_{s,t\in\mathcal{E}_{s_{0},n}}|t-s|=O_{p}(n^{-1/(2k+1)})
.

To analyze the term (
102
)–(
103
), we exploit the following decomposition again:

\displaystyle\widehat{F}_{a,n}(x)-F_{a}(x)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{P}}_{n}D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(x)-P_{*}D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(x)

\displaystyle=({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(x)+P_{*}(D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(x)-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(x)),

for any
x\in\mathbb{R}
. And, we have

\displaystyle 2\sup_{s,t\in\mathcal{E}_{s_{0},n}{~\rm and~}t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{t},s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{s}}\Big\{\Big|\Big[\Big(\widehat{F}_{a,n}(t^{\prime})-F_{a}(t^{\prime})\Big)-\Big(\widehat{F}_{a,n}(t)-F_{a}(t)\Big)\Big]\Big|

\displaystyle\indent~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~-\Big[\Big(\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s^{\prime})-F_{a}(s^{\prime})\Big)-\Big(\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s)-F_{a}(s)\Big)\Big]\Big|\Big\}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 4\sup_{t\in\mathcal{E}_{s_{0},n}{~\rm and~}t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{t}}\left|\left[\left(\widehat{F}_{a,n}(t^{\prime})-F_{a}(t^{\prime})\right)-\left(\widehat{F}_{a,n}(t)-F_{a}(t)\right)\right]\right|.

(106)

Then for the second term (applied to the term (
106
)), we use the decomposition used in Lemma
11
which is given by

\displaystyle P_{*}

\displaystyle\Big\{\Big[(D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}})(t^{\prime})-(D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}})(t)\Big]\Big\}

\displaystyle=P_{*}\Big\{\frac{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})-\pi_{\infty}(\mathbf{X})}{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})}\Big[\Big((\widehat{\phi}_{a}-\phi_{a,\infty})(t^{\prime}|\mathbf{X})-(\widehat{\phi}_{a}-\phi_{a,\infty})(t|\mathbf{X})\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\indent+\frac{\pi_{a,*}(\mathbf{X})(\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})-\pi_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X}))}{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})\pi_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\Big[\Big((\phi_{a,\infty}-\phi_{a,*})(t^{\prime}|\mathbf{X})-(\phi_{a,\infty}-\phi_{a,*})(t|\mathbf{X})\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\indent+\frac{\pi_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})-\pi_{a,*}(\mathbf{X})}{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})}\Big[\Big((\widehat{\phi}_{a}-\phi_{a,\infty})(t^{\prime}|\mathbf{X})-(\widehat{\phi}_{a}-\phi_{a,\infty})(t|\mathbf{X})\Big)\Big]\Big\}.

Thus, due to
\kappa_{n}=O_{p}(n^{-(k+1)/(2k+1)})
and the Assumption
(E6)
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathcal{E}_{s_{0},n}{~\rm and~}t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{t}}\left|P_{*}\Big\{\Big[(D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}})(t^{\prime})-(D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}})(t)\Big]\Big\}\right|=o_{p}(n^{-(k+1)/(2k+1)}).

(107)

On the other hand, Lemma
9
with
l=k-1/2,t=1
instead of
l=k
yields, from the same reasoning for (
70
) in the proof of Lemma
11
,

\displaystyle\sup_{[r_{1},r_{2}]\subset I_{s_{0},\omega},r_{1}\leq r_{2}\leq r_{1}+\kappa_{n}}\Big|({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})\Big[\frac{I(A=a)}{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})}I(r_{1}<Y\leq r_{2})\Big|\leq\kappa_{n}^{k+1/2}+B_{3},

(108)

where
B_{3}
is
O_{p}(n^{-(k+1/2)/(2k)})
which is independent of
r_{1},r_{2}
. Considering that
\kappa_{n}=O_{p}(n^{-(k+1)/(2k+1)})
, the right hand side of (
108
) is
o_{p}(n^{-(k+1)/(2k+1)})
. With (
108
) and another direct application of (
71
) on
r_{1},r_{2}
which satisfy
[r_{1},r_{2}]\subset I_{s_{0},\omega},r_{1}\leq r_{2}\leq r_{1}+\kappa_{n}
implies

\displaystyle\sup_{[r_{1},r_{2}]\subset I_{s_{0},\omega},r_{1}\leq r_{2}\leq r_{1}+\kappa_{n}}

\displaystyle\Big|({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})\Big[\frac{I(A=a)}{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})}I(r_{1}<Y\leq r_{2})

\displaystyle+(\widehat{\phi}_{a}(r_{2}|\mathbf{X})-\widehat{\phi}_{a}(r_{1}|\mathbf{X}))\Big(1-\frac{I(A=a)}{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})}\Big)\Big]\Big|=o_{p}(n^{-(k+1)/(2k+1)}).

This further yields,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathcal{E}_{s_{0},n}{~\rm and~}t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{t}}\Big|({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})\Big\{D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(t^{\prime})-D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(t)\Big\}\Big|=o_{p}(n^{-(k+1)/(2k+1)}).

(109)

Combining (
107
) and (
109
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{s,t\in\mathcal{E}_{s_{0},n}}\Big|\Big(\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(t)-\widehat{F}_{a,n}(t)\Big)-\Big(\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s)-\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s)\Big)\Big|=o_{p}(n^{-(k+1)/(2k+1)}).

(110)

For general points
v_{1},v_{2}
which are off-grid, we exploit

\displaystyle\Big|\Big(\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(v_{2})-\widehat{F}_{a,n}(v_{2})\Big)-\Big(\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(v_{1})-\widehat{F}_{a,n}(v_{1})\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\Big(\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(v^{*}_{2})-\widehat{F}_{a,n}(v_{2})\Big)-\Big(\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(v^{*}_{1})-\widehat{F}_{a,n}(v_{1})\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\Big(\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(v^{*}_{2})-\widehat{F}_{a,n}(v^{*}_{2})\Big)-\Big(\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(v^{*}_{1})-\widehat{F}_{a,n}(v^{*}_{1})\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\indent+\Big|\Big(\widehat{F}_{a,n}(v^{*}_{2})-\widehat{F}_{a,n}(v_{2})\Big)-\Big(\widehat{F}_{a,n}(v^{*}_{1})-\widehat{F}_{a,n}(v_{1})\Big)\Big|,

(111)

where
v^{*}_{i}:=\max\{v:v\in\mathcal{S}_{n},v\leq v_{i}\}
, for
i=1,2
. Since
\delta_{n}=O_{p}(n^{-(k+1)/(2k+1)})
, and from similar reasoning to (
103
)-(
105
), (
107
) and (
109
), one can control (
111
) by

\displaystyle\sup_{v_{1},v_{2}\in\mathcal{E}_{s_{0},n}}\Big|\Big(\widehat{F}_{a,n}(v^{*}_{2})-\widehat{F}_{a,n}(v_{2})\Big)-\Big(\widehat{F}_{a,n}(v^{*}_{1})-\widehat{F}_{a,n}(v_{1})\Big)\Big|=o_{p}(n^{-(k+1)/(2k+1)}).

(112)

In addition, by (
110
), we have

\displaystyle\sup_{v_{1},v_{2}\in\mathcal{E}_{s_{0},n}}\Big|\Big(\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(v^{*}_{2})-\widehat{F}_{a,n}(v^{*}_{2})\Big)-\Big(\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(v^{*}_{1})-\widehat{F}_{a,n}(v^{*}_{1})\Big)\Big|=o_{p}(n^{-(k+1)/(2k+1)}).

(113)

Combining (
112
) with (
113
) completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 17
.

Under the assumptions as in Lemma
11
, for
t\in[-K,K]
for any
K>0
, the following holds for
a\in\{0,1\}
.

\displaystyle\int_{s_{0}}^{s_{n}(t)}\Big((\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(v)-\widehat{F}^{c}_{a,n}(s_{0}))-(\widehat{F}_{a,n}(v)-\widehat{F}_{a,n}(s_{0}))\Big)dv=o_{p}(n^{-(k+2)/(2k+1)}),

where
s_{n}(t)=s_{0}+n^{-1/(2k+1)}t
.

Proof.

Lemma
16
directly concludes the proof.
∎

We state another lemma to control the empirical process term involving the integrated difference in localized terms between
D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}
and
D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}
.

Lemma 18
.

Under the same assumptions as in Lemma
11
, for
a\in\{0,1\}
, we have

\displaystyle({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})\Big[\frac{r_{n}}{p_{a}(s_{0})}\int_{s_{0}}^{s_{n}(t)}(D_{a,\hat{\theta}_{a}}(v)-D_{a,\hat{\theta}_{a}}(s_{0}))-(D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(v)-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0}))dv\Big]=o_{p}(1).

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we assume
t>0
.
First, from the definition of
D_{a,\hat{\theta}_{a}}
and
D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}
, we have

\displaystyle(D_{a,\hat{\theta}_{a}}(v)-D_{a,\hat{\theta}_{a}}(s_{0}))

\displaystyle-(D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(v)-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0}))

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi_{a,\infty}(X)-\widehat{\pi}_{a}(X)}{\pi_{a,\infty}(X)\widehat{\pi}_{a}(X)}I(A=a)I(s_{0}<Y\leq v)

\displaystyle\indent+\Big(1-\frac{I(A=a)}{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(X)}\Big)\Big(\widehat{\phi}_{a}(v|X)-\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s_{0}|X)\Big)

\displaystyle\indent-\Big(1-\frac{I(A=a)}{\pi_{a,\infty}(X)}\Big)\Big(\phi_{a,\infty}(v|X)-\phi_{a,\infty}(s_{0}|X)\Big).

In the proof of Lemma
11
(see (
71
)), we showed that there exists
\epsilon>0
such that

\displaystyle({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\Big|\Big(1-\frac{I(A=a)}{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(X)}\Big)\Big(\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s_{0}+v_{n}s|X)

\displaystyle-\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s_{0}|X)\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle=O_{p}(n^{-(k+1+\epsilon)/(2k+1)}).

Similarly to this, with Assumption
(E2)
,
(E5)
, one can show that

\displaystyle({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\Big|\Big(1-\frac{I(A=a)}{\pi_{a,\infty}(X)}\Big)\Big(\phi_{a,\infty}(s_{0}+v_{n}s|X)

\displaystyle-\phi_{a,\infty}(s_{0}|X)\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle=O_{p}(n^{-(k+3/2)/(2k+1)}).

The two preceding convergence rates are
o_{p}(n^{-(k+1)/(2k+1)})
and
\int_{s_{0}}^{s_{n}(t)}o_{p}(n^{-(k+1)/(2k+1)})dv
is
o_{p}(r_{n})
.
Thus, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\Big|\frac{\pi_{a,\infty}(X)-\widehat{\pi}_{a}(X)}{\pi_{a,\infty}(X)\widehat{\pi}_{a}(X)}I(A=a)I(s_{0}<Y\leq s_{0}+v_{n}s)\Big|

is
o_{p}(n^{-(k+1)/(2k+1)}).

To prove this, define a function class
\mathcal{F}_{n,s_{0},t}
by

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{n,s_{0},t}:=\Big\{(s,f_{n,s_{0},s,\pi}(x_{1},\mathbf{x_{2}},x_{3})):\pi\in\mathcal{F}_{\pi},x_{1}\in\{0,1\},\mathbf{x_{2}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d},x_{3}\in\mathbb{R},s\in[0,t]\Big\},

where

\displaystyle f_{n,s_{0},s,\pi}(x_{1},\mathbf{x_{2}},x_{3}):=\sqrt{v_{n}^{-1}}

\displaystyle\frac{I_{a}(x_{1})}{\pi(\mathbf{x_{2}})}I(s_{0}<x_{3}\leq s_{0}+v_{n}s).

Then we show
\rho
-equicontinuity of this class where the semi-metric
\rho
is a product metric of Euclidean distance in
\mathbb{R}
and
L_{2}
norm in
\mathcal{F}_{\pi}
(eg.
\rho
is the sum of two metrics).

We will show the four conditions for concluding
\rho
-equicontinuity of Theorem 2.11.24 of
van der Vaart and Wellner (2023)
(which we have provided as Proposition
2
in the Appendix for completeness). First, due to the Assumption
(E2)
, the class
\mathcal{F}_{n,s_{0},t}
admits an envelope
F_{n,s_{0},t}:=K\sqrt{v_{n}^{-1}}I(A=a)I(s_{0}<Y\leq s_{0}+v_{n}t)
. And, by the log-concavity of the distribution of
Y^{a}
, there exists a constant
C_{5}>0
such that

\displaystyle P_{*}F_{n,s_{0},t}^{2}=K^{2}v_{n}^{-1}{\mathbb{P}}(s_{0}<Y^{a}\leq s_{0}+v_{n}t)\leq K^{2}C_{5}t.

Furthermore, for any
\vartheta>0
, since
|F_{n,s_{0},t}|\lesssim\sqrt{v_{n}^{-1}}
, it is obvious that

\displaystyle P_{*}F_{n,s_{0},t}^{2}I(F_{n,s_{0},t}>\vartheta\sqrt{n})\rightarrow 0.

Next, we prove

\displaystyle\sup_{|t_{1}-t_{2}|\leq\zeta_{n},\|\pi_{1}-\pi_{2}\|\leq\zeta_{n}}P_{*}(f_{n,s_{0},t_{1},\pi_{1}}-f_{n,s_{0},t_{2},\pi_{2}})^{2}\rightarrow 0,

for any
\zeta_{n}\rightarrow 0
. Indeed, since
\zeta_{n}\rightarrow 0
,

\displaystyle P_{*}(f_{n,s_{0},t_{1},\pi_{1}}-f_{n,s_{0},t_{2},\pi_{2}})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq P_{*}(f_{n,s_{0},t_{1},\pi_{1}}-f_{n,s_{0},t_{2},\pi_{1}})^{2}+P_{*}(f_{n,s_{0},t_{2},\pi_{1}}-f_{n,s_{0},t_{2},\pi_{2}})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq K^{2}C_{5}|t_{1}-t_{2}|+K^{4}C_{5}tP_{*}\|\pi_{1}-\pi_{2}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq K^{2}C_{5}\zeta_{n}+K^{4}C_{5}t\zeta_{n}^{2}\rightarrow 0.

We already showed the bracketing entropy condition of the class
\mathcal{F}_{t1}
in the proof of Lemma
11
. Since, for arbitrary probability measure
Q
and
\epsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\sup_{Q}{\rm log}N(\epsilon,\mathcal{F}_{n,s_{0},t},L_{2}(Q))\asymp\sup_{Q}{\rm log}N(\epsilon,\mathcal{F}_{t1},L_{2}(Q))\lesssim\epsilon^{-V}-{\rm log}(\epsilon),

the function class
\mathcal{F}_{n,s_{0},t}
satisfies the uniform entropy integral condition. Combining the preceding results with
L_{2}(P_{*})
convergence of
\widehat{\pi}_{a}
to
\pi_{a,\infty}
which is given in
(E1)
and
v_{n}t\rightarrow 0
, we have
({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})\sup_{(t,f)\in\mathcal{F}_{n,s_{0},t}}f=o_{p}(n^{-1/2})
by
\rho
-equicontinuity. This further implies

({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\left|\frac{\pi_{a,\infty}(X)-\widehat{\pi}_{a}(X)}{\pi_{a,\infty}(X)\widehat{\pi}_{a}(X)}I(A=a)I(s_{0}<Y\leq v_{n}s)\right|=o_{p}(n^{-(k+1)/(2k+1)}).

And, again since
\int_{s_{0}}^{s_{n}(t)}o_{p}(n^{-(k+1)/(2k+1)})dv=o_{p}(r_{n})
, this concludes the lemma.
∎

D.4.2
Proof of the main Theorem

In this section we give the proof of Theorem
2
based on the preceding lemmas in Section
D.4.1
.

Proof.

First, we find the two constants
\gamma_{1a},\gamma_{2a}
for each
a\in\{0,1\}
which satisfy

\displaystyle\gamma_{1a}G_{\alpha_{a},k,\sigma_{a}}(\gamma_{2a}t)=_{d}Y_{k}(t),

where
Y_{k}
is the integrated Gaussian process defined in (
87
). Due to the scaling property of Brownian motion, we have,

\displaystyle\gamma_{1a}\gamma_{2a}^{3/2}=\alpha_{a}^{-1},~~~\gamma_{1a}\gamma_{2a}^{k+2}=\sigma_{a}^{-1},

where
\alpha_{a},\sigma_{a}
are defined in Lemma
14
-(a).
The solution of the above system of equations is

\displaystyle\gamma_{1a}

\displaystyle=\Bigg(\frac{|\varphi^{(k)}_{a}(s_{0})|}{(k+2)!}\Bigg)^{3/(2k+1)}\Bigg(\frac{p_{a}(s_{0})^{2}}{\chi_{\theta_{a}}}\Bigg)^{(k+2)/(2k+1)},

\displaystyle\gamma_{2a}

\displaystyle=\Bigg(\frac{|\varphi^{(k)}_{a}(s_{0})|}{(k+2)!}\Bigg)^{-2/(2k+1)}\Bigg(\frac{p_{a}(s_{0})^{2}}{\chi_{\theta_{a}}}\Bigg)^{-1/(2k+1)}.

Next, since

\displaystyle\gamma_{1a}\gamma_{2a}^{2}\widehat{H}^{\rm locmod,(2)}_{a,n}\rightarrow H_{k}^{(2)}(t),

(114)

\displaystyle\gamma_{1a}\gamma_{2a}^{3}\widehat{H}^{\rm locmod,(3)}_{a,n}\rightarrow H_{k}^{(3)}(t),

(115)

by Lemma
14
and
15
, the preceding displays imply

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}n^{k/(2k+1)}(\widehat{\varphi}_{1,n}(s_{0})-\varphi_{1}(s_{0}))\\
n^{(k-1)/(2k+1)}(\widehat{\varphi}^{\prime}_{1,n}(s_{0})-\varphi^{\prime}_{1}(s_{0}))\\
n^{k/(2k+1)}(\widehat{\varphi}_{0,n}(s_{0})-\varphi_{0}(s_{0}))\\
n^{(k-1)/(2k+1)}(\widehat{\varphi}^{\prime}_{0,n}(s_{0})-\varphi^{\prime}_{0}(s_{0}))\\
\end{pmatrix}\rightarrow_{d}\begin{pmatrix}C_{k}(s_{0},\varphi_{1})H_{1k}^{(2)}(0)\\
D_{k}(s_{0},\varphi_{1})H_{1k}^{(3)}(0)\\
C_{k}(s_{0},\varphi_{0})H_{0k}^{(2)}(0)\\
D_{k}(s_{0},\varphi_{0})H_{0k}^{(3)}(0)\\
\end{pmatrix},

where
H_{1k}
and
H_{0k}
are independent copies of
H_{k}
defined in (
88
);
C_{k}(s_{0},\varphi_{a})=(\gamma_{1a}\gamma_{2a}^{2})^{-1}
and
D_{k}(s_{0},\varphi_{a})=(\gamma_{1a}\gamma_{2a}^{3})^{-1}
,
a\in\{0,1\}
.

Now, plugging in the exact values of
\gamma_{1a},\gamma_{2a}
, we get the exact values of
C_{k}(s_{0},\varphi_{a})
, and
D_{k}(s_{0},\varphi_{a})
which are given by,

\displaystyle C_{k}(s_{0},\varphi_{a})=\Bigg(\frac{|\varphi^{(k)}_{a}(s_{0})|}{(k+2)!}\Bigg)^{1/(2k+1)}\Bigg(\frac{p_{a}(s_{0})^{2}}{\chi_{\theta_{a}}}\Bigg)^{-k/(2k+1)},

\displaystyle D_{k}(s_{0},\varphi_{a})=\Bigg(\frac{|\varphi^{(k)}_{a}(s_{0})|}{(k+2)!}\Bigg)^{3/(2k+1)}\Bigg(\frac{p_{a}(s_{0})^{2}}{\chi_{\theta_{a}}}\Bigg)^{-(k-1)/(2k+1)}.

Next, (
11
) follows directly from the delta method. This completes the proof.
∎

D.5
Proof of Theorem
3

We give the proof of Theorem
3
as follows.

Proof.

For part (a), based on the result from Lemma
14
-(d), the entire proof of both Theorem 2.4 and 3.2 in
Deng et al. (2022)
can be directly applied to the joint process
(V_{1,n},V_{0,n})
, which converges weakly to
(V_{1},V_{0})
(see (
97
)–(
98
) for the definition of the joint processes).
Consequently, this yields

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{n(\tau_{n}^{+}(s_{0};1)-\tau_{n}^{-}(s_{0};1))}(\widehat{\varphi}_{1,n}(s_{0})-\varphi_{1}(s_{0}))\\
\sqrt{n(\tau_{n}^{+}(s_{0};1)-\tau_{n}^{-}(s_{0};1))^{3}}(\widehat{\varphi}^{\prime}_{1,n}(s_{0})-\varphi^{\prime}_{1}(s_{0}))\\
\sqrt{n(\tau_{n}^{+}(s_{0};0)-\tau_{n}^{-}(s_{0};0))}(\widehat{\varphi}_{0,n}(s_{0})-\varphi_{0}(s_{0}))\\
\sqrt{n(\tau_{n}^{+}(s_{0};0)-\tau_{n}^{-}(s_{0};0))^{3}}(\widehat{\varphi}^{\prime}_{0,n}(s_{0})-\varphi^{\prime}_{0}(s_{0}))\\
\end{pmatrix}\rightarrow_{d}\begin{pmatrix}-\alpha_{1}{\mathbb{L}}_{1k}^{(0)}\\
-\alpha_{1}{\mathbb{L}}_{1k}^{(1)}\\
-\alpha_{0}{\mathbb{L}}_{0k}^{(0)}\\
-\alpha_{0}{\mathbb{L}}_{0k}^{(1)}\\
\end{pmatrix},

(116)

where
\alpha_{a}=\frac{1}{p_{a}(s_{0})}\sqrt{{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\frac{\pi_{a,*}(\mathbf{X})}{\pi^{2}_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\eta_{a,*}(s_{0}|\mathbf{X})\Big]}
, for
a\in\{0,1\}
, is defined in Lemma
14
-(a).
Recall
h_{k;-}^{*}
and
h_{k;+}^{*}
are characterized as the absolute values of the location of the first touch points of the pair
(H_{k},Y_{k})
.
Since
V_{1}
and
V_{0}
converge weakly to independent joint processes that are of the form (
98
), it is obvious that
{\mathbb{L}}_{1k}^{(i)}
and
{\mathbb{L}}_{0k}^{(i)}
are independent, for
i=0,1
.
The distributional result for
\widehat{p}_{a,n}
and
\widehat{p}^{\prime}_{a,n}
follow directly by the delta method.

Next, the part (b) can be obtained directly from the part (a), similarly to the proof of Theorem 2.6 in
Deng et al. (2022)
which is directly concluded by Theorem 2.4 therein.
∎

D.6
Proof of Lemma
4

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we show that the following holds for a positive deterministic sequence
h_{n}
.

\displaystyle h^{-1}P_{*}\left\{D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0}+h)-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0})\right\}^{2}\rightarrow\chi_{\theta_{a}},

(117)

\displaystyle h^{-1}\left[P_{*}\left\{D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0}+h)-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0})\right\}^{2}-P_{*}\left\{D_{a,{\widehat{\theta}_{a}}}(s_{0}+h)-D_{a,{\widehat{\theta}_{a}}}(s_{0})\right\}^{2}\right]\rightarrow 0,

(118)

\displaystyle h^{-1}({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})\left\{D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(s_{0}+h)-D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(s_{0})\right\}^{2}\rightarrow_{p}0,

(119)

since combining (
117
)–(
119
) yields the main result.
First, the same procedure to check the limiting behavior of (
92
)–(
95
) can be directly applied to show (
117
). Hence, we omit the proof.
Secondly, for (
118
), since

\displaystyle\left|P_{*}\left\{D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0}+h)-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0})\right\}^{2}-P_{*}\left\{D_{a,{\widehat{\theta}_{a}}}(s_{0}+h)-D_{a,{\widehat{\theta}_{a}}}(s_{0})\right\}^{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq 4(K+1)P_{*}\left|\left(D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0}+h)-D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s_{0})\right)-\left(D_{a,{\widehat{\theta}_{a}}}(s_{0}+h)-D_{a,{\widehat{\theta}_{a}}}(s_{0})\right)\right|,

(120)

a similar derivation step to check (
107
) (see the decomposition in the preceding paragraph therein) can be applied to (
118
). In (
120
), we used the fact that
|D_{a,{\widehat{\theta}_{a}}}|,|D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}|\leq K+1
by conditions
(E2)
and
(E3)
.
Lastly, we now check (
119
). As in (
69
), we have

\displaystyle({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})\left\{D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(s_{2})-D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}(s_{1})\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle=({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})\left[\frac{I(A=a)}{\widehat{\pi}^{2}_{a}(\mathbf{X})}I(s_{1}<Y\leq s_{2})\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+2I(s_{1}<Y\leq s_{2})(\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s_{2}|\mathbf{X})-\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s_{1}|\mathbf{X}))\Big(1-\frac{I(A=a)}{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})}\Big)\frac{I(A=a)}{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.+(\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s_{2}|\mathbf{X})-\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s_{1}|\mathbf{X}))^{2}\Big(1-\frac{I(A=a)}{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})}\Big)^{2}\right].

By an analogous derivation step used in Lemma
11
to show (
70
), one can easily verify that

\displaystyle\sup_{[s_{1},s_{2}]\subset I_{s_{0},\omega},s_{1}\leq s_{2}\leq s_{1}+R}\Big|({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})\Big[\frac{I(A=a)}{\widehat{\pi}^{2}_{a}(\mathbf{X})}I(s_{1}<Y\leq s_{2})\Big|\leq\epsilon|s_{2}-s_{1}|^{k+1}+B^{\prime}_{1},

(121)

where a random variable
B^{\prime}_{1}
has order of
O_{p}(n^{-(k+1)/(2k+1)})
and is independent of
s_{1},s_{2}
.
And, following a similar step to prove (
71
), one can further check that

\displaystyle\sup_{[s_{1},s_{2}]\subset I_{s_{0},\omega},s_{1}\leq s_{2}\leq s_{1}+R}\Big|(\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s_{2}|\mathbf{X})-\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s_{1}|\mathbf{X}))^{2}\Big(1-\frac{I(A=a)}{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})}\Big)^{2}\Big|\leq\epsilon|s_{2}-s_{1}|^{k+t}+B^{\prime}_{2},

(122)

knowing that this class of functions allows an envelope
(1+K)^{2}R^{2}_{1}(\mathbf{X})\omega^{2\alpha}
where
\alpha\in(1/2,1]
by condition
(E2)
and
(E7)
,
where a random variable
B_{2}^{\prime}
has order of
O_{p}(n^{-(k+t)/(2k+1)})
with some
t>3/2
, and is independent of
s_{1},s_{2}
.
Thus, the following holds

\displaystyle({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})\left[\frac{1}{h}\left|\frac{I(A=a)}{\widehat{\pi}^{2}_{a}(\mathbf{X})}I(s_{0}<Y\leq s_{0}+h)\right|\right]\rightarrow_{p}0,

\displaystyle({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})\left[\frac{1}{h}\left|(\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s_{0}+h|\mathbf{X})-\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s_{0}|\mathbf{X}))^{2}\Big(1-\frac{I(A=a)}{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})}\Big)^{2}\right|\right]\rightarrow_{p}0,

as long as
h^{-1}=O(\sqrt{n})
regardless of an even integer
k
.
Thus, the proof now reduces to show that

\displaystyle({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})\left[\frac{1}{h}\left|I(s_{1}<Y\leq s_{2})(\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s_{2}|\mathbf{X})-\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s_{1}|\mathbf{X}))\Big(1-\frac{I(A=a)}{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})}\Big)\frac{I(A=a)}{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})}\right|\right]\rightarrow_{p}0.

We follow the same derivation steps used in Lemma
11
. Recall that
\mathcal{F}_{1}=\{I_{(s_{1},s_{2}](\cdot)}:[s_{1},s_{2}]\subseteq I_{s_{0},\omega},s_{1}\leq s_{2}\leq s_{1}+R\}
satisfied

\displaystyle\sup_{Q}N(\epsilon,\mathcal{F}_{1},L_{2}(Q))\lesssim-\log(\varepsilon),

and
\mathcal{F}_{2}:=\Big\{\frac{I_{\{a\}}(x_{1})}{\pi_{a}(\mathbf{x_{2}})}:\pi_{a}\in\mathcal{F}_{\pi},x_{1}\in\{0,1\},\mathbf{x_{2}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\Big\}
satisfied

\displaystyle\sup_{Q}N(\epsilon,\mathcal{F}_{2},L_{2}(Q))\lesssim\varepsilon^{-V},

and
\mathcal{F}_{3}:=\{\phi_{a}(s_{2}|\cdot)-\phi_{a}(s_{1}|\cdot):[s_{1},s_{2}]\subseteq[s_{0}-\delta,s_{0}+\delta],s_{1}\leq s_{2}\leq s_{1}+R,\phi_{a}\in\mathcal{F}_{\phi}\}
satisfied

\displaystyle\sup_{Q}{\rm log}N(\epsilon,\mathcal{F}_{3},L_{2}(Q))\lesssim\epsilon^{-V},

for any probability measure
Q
with
V\in[0,2)
.
Thus, the whole function class
\mathcal{F}_{0}:=\mathcal{F}_{1}\cdot\mathcal{F}_{2}\cdot\mathcal{F}_{3}\cdot(1-\mathcal{F}_{2})
has finite uniform entropy integral, by Assumptions
(EC3)
and Lemma 5.1 from
van der Vaart and van der Laan (2006)
,

\displaystyle\sup_{Q}{\rm log}N(\epsilon,\mathcal{F}_{0},L_{2}(Q))\lesssim\epsilon^{-V}-{\rm log}(\epsilon)

up to a constant, with
V\in[0,2)
. Furthermore, the class allows an envelope
\omega^{\alpha}K(K+1)I(s_{0}-\omega<Y\leq s_{0}+\omega)I(A=a)R_{1}(\mathbf{X})
which satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big(\omega^{\alpha}K(K+1)I(s_{0}-\omega<Y\leq s_{0}+\omega)I(A=a)R_{1}(\mathbf{X})\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle=\omega^{2\alpha}K^{2}(K+1)^{2}P_{*}(s_{0}-\omega<Y^{a}\leq s_{0}+\omega)\mathbb{E}R^{2}_{1}(\mathbf{X})

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\omega^{2\alpha+1}K^{2}\mathbb{E}R^{2}_{1}(\mathbf{X})

for some constant
C_{0}>0
.
Hence, by Lemma
9
, for each
\epsilon>0
, there exist a random variable
B_{0}
which has order of
O_{p}(n^{-(k+t)/(2k+1)})
with some
t>3/2
and is independent of
s_{1},s_{2}
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{[s_{1},s_{2}]\subset I_{s_{0},\omega},s_{1}\leq s_{2}\leq s_{1}+R}

\displaystyle\left|I(s_{1}<Y\leq s_{2})(\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s_{2}|\mathbf{X})-\widehat{\phi}_{a}(s_{1}|\mathbf{X}))\Big(1-\frac{I(A=a)}{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})}\Big)\frac{I(A=a)}{\widehat{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{X})}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon|s_{2}-s_{1}|^{k+t}+B_{0}.

This implies that (
119
) when
h^{-1}=O(\sqrt{n})
, and it completes the proof.
∎

D.7
Proof of Theorem
4

Proof.

We denote the empirical process over the
i
-th fold (or subgroup)
\mathcal{V}_{n,i}
by
\mathbb{G}^{i}_{n}=\sqrt{N}({\mathbb{P}}^{i}_{n}-P_{*})
where
{\mathbb{P}}^{i}_{n}
is the empirical measure on the same subgroup.
As discussed in the proof of Theorem
1
, we have to check the following:

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\widehat{F}^{K_{0}}_{a,n}(s)-F_{a}(s)\right|=o_{p}(1),

(123)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}|s|dF^{K_{0}}_{a,n}(s)-\int_{\mathbb{R}}|s|dF_{a}(s)\right|=o_{p}(1).

(124)

Now, we prove (
123
), which is equivalent to show that

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in(-\infty,0]}\left|\widehat{F}^{K_{0}}_{a,n}(s)-F_{a}(s)\right|=o_{p}(1),

(125)

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[0,\infty)}\left|(1-\widehat{F}^{K_{0}}_{a,n}(s))-(1-F_{a}(s))\right|=o_{p}(1).

(126)

We start with showing (
125
).
First, by Assumption
(E4)
, we have

\begin{split}&\widehat{F}^{K_{0}}_{a,n}(s)-F_{a}(s)\\
&=\frac{1}{K_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{K_{0}}\left[{\mathbb{P}}^{i}_{n}\left\{D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a,-i}}(s)\right\}\right]-P_{*}D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(s)\\
&=\frac{1}{K_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{K_{0}}\left[{\mathbb{P}}^{i}_{n}\left\{D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a,-i}}(s)\right\}\right]-{\mathbb{P}}_{n}D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(s)+({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(s),\\
\end{split}

(127)

where
{\mathbb{P}}_{n}^{i}D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a,-i}}
is the estimated centered efficient influence function evaluated with validation sample in which the nuisance estimators are constructed upon only the observations from the training set
\mathcal{T}_{n,i}
.
Since
\sum_{i=1}^{K_{0}}{\mathbb{P}}^{i}_{n}D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(s)=\sum_{i=1}^{K_{0}}{\mathbb{P}}_{n}D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(s)
, the preceding display equals

\displaystyle\frac{1}{K_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{K_{0}}\left[({\mathbb{P}}^{i}_{n}-P_{*})\left\{D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a,-i}}(s)-D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(s)\right\}\right]+\frac{1}{K_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{K_{0}}\left[P_{*}\left\{D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a,-i}}(s)-D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(s)\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(s)

\displaystyle=:R_{1n}(s)+R_{2n}(s)+({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(s).

(128)

We show the three terms in (
128
) are negligible.
We start with the last summand,
({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(s)
.
First, by a similar reasoning used in the proof of Lemma
19
, we note that one can show that

\displaystyle N_{[]}(\epsilon,\mathcal{F}_{\phi_{\infty}},L_{2}(P_{*}))<\infty,

(129)

for any
\epsilon>0
, where
\mathcal{F}_{\phi_{\infty}}:=\{\mathbf{X}\mapsto{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X}):s\in(-\infty,0]\}
.
This implies that
\mathcal{F}_{\phi_{\infty}}
is a Glivenko-Cantelli class (see Theorem 2.4.1 of
van der Vaart and Wellner (2023)
). Thus, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in(-\infty,0]}\Big|({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s)\Big|\rightarrow 0~~a.s.

Moreover, a similar procedure used to prove that (
46
)–(
48
) are
o_{p}(1)
in the proof of Theorem
1
can be applied to show that
\sup_{s\in(-\infty,0]}\left|R_{2n}\right|
is
o_{p}(1)
, since
K=O_{p}(1)
.
Next, it suffices to study the term
R_{1n}(s)
in (
128
).
By the tower property of expectation,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n,-i}}\left|\mathbb{G}^{i}_{n}f\right|\right]={\mathbb{E}}\left\{{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n,-i}}\left|\mathbb{G}^{i}_{n}f\right|\Big|\mathcal{T}_{n,i}\right]\right\},

where
\mathcal{F}_{n,-i}:=\left\{D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a,-i}}(s)-D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(s):s\in(-\infty,0]\right\}
. We further note that

\begin{split}&\left|D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a,-i}}(s)-D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(s)\right|\\
&=\left|I(A=a)\left(\frac{1}{\widehat{\pi}_{a,-i}(\mathbf{X})}-\frac{1}{\pi_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\right)\left[I(Y\leq s)-\phi_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right]\right|\\
&\indent+\left|\left(1-\frac{I(A=a)}{\widehat{\pi}_{a,-i}(\mathbf{X})}\right)\left(\widehat{\phi}_{a,-i}(s|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right)\right|,\end{split}

(130)

which is bounded by

\displaystyle H_{n,i}(\mathbf{z})=K^{2}\left|\widehat{\pi}_{a,-i}(\mathbf{x})-\pi_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{x})\right|+(K+1)\sup_{s\in(-\infty,0]}\left|\widehat{\phi}_{a,-i}(s|\mathbf{x})-\phi_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{x})\right|.

(131)

By Theorem 2.14.16 in
van der Vaart and Wellner (2023)
(see Proposition
4
in our Appendix), for sufficiently large
n
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n,-i}}\left|\mathbb{G}^{i}_{n}f\right|\Big|\mathcal{T}_{n,i}\right]\leq C_{*}\|H_{n,i}\|_{P_{*},2}J_{[]}(1,\mathcal{F}_{n,-i},L_{2}(P_{*})),

for a universal constant
C_{*}>0
, where
J_{[]}(1,\mathcal{F}_{n,-i},L_{2}(P_{*}))
is the bracketing integral of the function class
\mathcal{F}_{n,-i}
(see Proposition
4
for its definition).
We have uniformly bounded
J_{[]}(1,\mathcal{F}_{n,-i},L_{2}(P_{*}))
for all
n
and
i
, since
\{Y\mapsto I(Y\leq s):|s|\leq M\}
is a VS subgraph class, and Lemma
19
can be applied to a function class
{\mathcal{F}}_{\widehat{\phi}_{a,-i}}:=\{\mathbf{X}\mapsto\widehat{\phi}_{a,-i}(s|\mathbf{X})ds:s\in(-\infty,0]\}
by Assumption
(E3′)
. Hence, it suffices to show that
\max_{1\leq i\leq K_{0}}{\mathbb{E}}\|H_{n,i}\|\rightarrow 0
. Indeed, by Assumption
(E1′)
,

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq i\leq K_{0}}\|H_{n,i}\|_{P_{*},2}

\displaystyle\leq K^{2}\max_{1\leq i\leq K_{0}}\left\|\widehat{\pi}_{a,-i}(\mathbf{X})-{\pi}_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})\right\|_{P_{*},2}

\displaystyle\qquad+(K+1)\max_{1\leq i\leq K_{0}}\left\|\sup_{s\in(-\infty,0]}\left(\widehat{\phi}_{a,-i}(s|\mathbf{X})-{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right)\right\|_{P_{*},2}\rightarrow 0,

since the convergence of the second term on the right hand side of the preceding display can be easily obtained by the similar steps used in Theorem
1
.
Similar reasoning can be applied to show (
126
), since we have

\begin{split}&(1-\widehat{F}^{K_{0}}_{a,n}(s))-(1-F_{a}(s))\\
&=\frac{1}{K_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{K_{0}}\left[{\mathbb{P}}^{i}_{n}\left\{1-D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a,-i}}(s)\right\}\right]-P_{*}\left(1-D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(s)\right)\\
&=\frac{1}{K_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{K_{0}}\left[{\mathbb{P}}^{i}_{n}\left\{1-D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a,-i}}(s)\right\}\right]-{\mathbb{P}}_{n}\left(1-D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(s)\right)+({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})\left(1-D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(s)\right),\\
\end{split}

and, further since, analogously to Lemma
19
, we obtain

\displaystyle N_{[]}(\epsilon,\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{\phi_{\infty}},L_{2}(P_{*}))<\infty,

(132)

for any
\epsilon>0
, where
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{\phi_{\infty}}:=\{\mathbf{X}\mapsto(1-{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})):s\in[0,\infty)\}
.
Hence, we omit the detailed derivation.

Now, we prove (
124
).
Since one can write
\widehat{F}^{K_{0}}_{a,n}(s)=\widehat{F}^{K_{0}}_{a,n,1}(s)-\widehat{F}^{K_{0}}_{a,n,2}(s)
for

\displaystyle\widehat{F}^{K_{0}}_{a,n,1}(s)=\frac{1}{K_{0}}\sum_{k=1}^{K_{0}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{n,k}}\left\{\frac{I(A_{i}=a)}{\widehat{\pi}_{a,-k}(\mathbf{X}_{i})}I(Y_{i}\leq s)+\widehat{\phi}_{a,-k}(s|\mathbf{X}_{i})\right\},

\displaystyle\widehat{F}^{K_{0}}_{a,n,2}(s)=\frac{1}{K_{0}}\sum_{k=1}^{K_{0}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{n,k}}\frac{I(A_{i}=a)}{\widehat{\pi}_{a,-k}(\mathbf{X}_{i})}\widehat{\phi}_{a,-k}(s|\mathbf{X}_{i}),

where
\widehat{F}^{K_{0}}_{a,n,1}(s)
and
\widehat{F}^{K_{0}}_{a,n,2}(s)
are uniformly bounded monotone functions (by Assumption
(E2′)
and
(E3′)
),
we can still proceed with the derivation used in the proof of Theorem
1
.
It suffices to check

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{0}\left|\widehat{F}^{K_{0}}_{a,n}(s)\right|ds<\infty\quad\mathbf{X}-a.e.,

(133)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\leq 0}\left|\int_{-\infty}^{t}\left[\widehat{F}^{K_{0}}(s)-F_{a}(s)\right]ds\right|=o_{p}(1),

(134)

since one can derive the following analogously:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\left|\widehat{1}-F^{K_{0}}_{a,n}(s)\right|ds<\infty\quad\mathbf{X}-a.e.,

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t}\left|\int_{t}^{\infty}\left[\left\{1-\widehat{F}^{K_{0}}(s)\right\}-\left\{1-F_{a}(s)\right\}\right]ds\right|=o_{p}(1).

We first check (
133
).
By Assumption
(E2′)
and
(E3′)
, we have

\begin{split}{\mathbb{E}}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\left|\widehat{F}^{K_{0}}_{a,n}(s)\right|ds&={\mathbb{E}}\left[\int_{-\infty}^{0}\left|\frac{1}{K_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{K_{0}}{\mathbb{P}}_{n}^{i}D_{a},\hat{\theta}_{a,-i}(s)\right|ds\right]\\
&\leq{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\frac{1}{K_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{K_{0}}{\mathbb{P}}_{n}^{i}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\left|D_{a},\hat{\theta}_{a,-i}(s)\right|ds\right\}\\
&\leq{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\frac{1}{K_{0}}\sum_{k=1}^{K_{0}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{n,k}}\frac{I(A_{i}=a)}{\widehat{\pi}_{a,-k}(\mathbf{X}_{i})}I(Y_{i}\leq 0)(-Y_{i})\right\}\\
&\quad+{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\frac{1}{K_{0}}\sum_{k=1}^{K_{0}}\frac{1}{N}\left|\left[1-\frac{I(A_{i}=a)}{\widehat{\pi}_{a,-k}(\mathbf{X}_{i})}\right]\int_{-\infty}^{0}\widehat{\phi}_{a,-k}(s|\mathbf{X}_{i})ds\right|\right\}\\
&\leq K{\mathbb{E}}|Y^{a}|+(1+K){\mathbb{E}}h(\mathbf{X})<\infty.\end{split}

Thus, this implies (
133
).
Now we check (
134
).
As in (
128
), we have

\begin{split}&\int_{-\infty}^{t}\widehat{F}^{K_{0}}_{a,n}(s)-F_{a}(s)ds\\
&=\int_{-\infty}^{t}\frac{1}{K_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{K_{0}}\left[({\mathbb{P}}^{i}_{n}-P_{*})\left\{D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a,-i}}(s)-D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(s)\right\}\right]ds\\
&\indent+\int_{-\infty}^{t}\frac{1}{K_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{K_{0}}\left[P_{*}\left\{D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a,-i}}(s)-D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(s)\right\}\right]ds\\
&\indent+\int_{-\infty}^{t}({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(s)ds\\
&=:\tilde{R}_{1n}(t)+\tilde{R}_{2n}(t)+\int_{-\infty}^{t}({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(s)ds.\end{split}

A similar technique used to prove that (
58
)–(
60
) (see the proof of Theorem
1
) can be applied to show that
\sup_{t\leq 0}|\tilde{R}_{2n}(t)|=o_{p}(1)
.
For
\tilde{R}_{1n}
, one can apply Lemma
19
to a class of functions
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\phi_{\infty}}:=\{\mathbf{X}\mapsto\int_{-\infty}^{t}{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})ds:t\in(-\infty,0]\}
,
since
\int_{-\infty}^{0}{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})ds\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}|s|{\phi}_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\in L_{2}(P_{*})
(see the proof of Theorem
1
).
This implies

\displaystyle N_{[]}(\epsilon,\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\phi_{\infty}},L_{2}(P_{*}))<\infty,

(135)

for any
\epsilon>0
, which in turn indicates
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\phi_{\infty}}
is a Glivenko-Cantelli class by Theorem 2.4.1 of
van der Vaart and Wellner (2023)
.
Thus, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in(-\infty,0]}\Big|\int_{-\infty}^{t}({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})D_{a,{\theta_{a,\infty}}}(s)ds\Big|\rightarrow 0~~a.s..

Lastly, we study the term
\tilde{R}_{1n}
.
By the tower property of expectation, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sup_{f\in\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{n,-i}}\left|\mathbb{G}^{i}_{n}f\right|\right]={\mathbb{E}}\left\{{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sup_{f\in\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{n,-i}}\left|\mathbb{G}^{i}_{n}f\right|\Big|\mathcal{T}_{n,i}\right]\right\},

where
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{n,-i}:=\left\{\int_{-\infty}^{t}\left(D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a,-i}}(s)-D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(s)\right)ds:t\in(-\infty,0]\right\}
.
We further note that

\begin{split}&\left|\int_{-\infty}^{t}\left(D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a,-i}}(s)-D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(s)\right)ds\right|\\
&=\left|I(A=a)\left(\frac{1}{\widehat{\pi}_{a,-i}(\mathbf{X})}-\frac{1}{\pi_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\right)\left[I(Y\leq t)(t-Y)-\int_{-\infty}^{t}\phi_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})ds\right]\right|\\
&\indent+\left|\left(1-\frac{I(A=a)}{\widehat{\pi}_{a,-i}(\mathbf{X})}\right)\int_{-\infty}^{t}\left(\widehat{\phi}_{a,-i}(s|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{X})\right)ds\right|,\end{split}

(136)

which admits an envelope function of

\begin{split}\tilde{H}_{n,i}(\mathbf{z})&=K^{2}\left|\widehat{\pi}_{a,-i}(\mathbf{x})-\pi_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{x})\right|(I(A=a)|Y|+h(\mathbf{X}))\\
&\indent+(K+1)\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\left|\widehat{\phi}_{a,-i}(s|\mathbf{x})-\phi_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{x})\right|ds,\end{split}

(137)

by Assumption
(E2′)
and
(E3′)
.
By Theorem 2.14.16 in
van der Vaart and Wellner (2023)
(see Proposition
4
in our Appendix), for sufficiently large
n
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sup_{f\in\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{n,-i}}\left|\mathbb{G}^{i}_{n}f\right|\Big|\mathcal{T}_{n,i}\right]\leq C^{\prime}_{*}\|\tilde{H}_{n,i}\|_{P_{*},2}J_{[]}(1,\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{n,-i},L_{2}(P_{*})),

for a universal constant
C^{\prime}_{*}>0
, where
J_{[]}(1,\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{n,-i},L_{2}(P_{*}))
is the bracketing integral of the function class
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{n,-i}
(see Proposition
4
for its definition).
We have uniformly bounded
J_{[]}(1,\mathcal{F}_{n,-i},L_{2}(P_{*}))
for all sufficiently large
n
and
i
, since
\{Y\mapsto I(Y\leq t)(t-Y):|t|\leq M\}
has a polynomial covering number (discussed in the proof of Theorem
1
), and Lemma
19
can be applied to a class of functions
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\widehat{\phi}_{a,-i}}:=\{\mathbf{X}\mapsto\int_{-\infty}^{t}\widehat{\phi}_{a,-i}(s|\mathbf{X})ds:t\in(-\infty,0]\}
by Assumption
(E3′)
.
Hence, it suffices to show that
\max_{1\leq i\leq K_{0}}{\mathbb{E}}\|\tilde{H}_{n,i}\|\rightarrow 0
.
By Assumption
(E1′)
, we have

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq i\leq K_{0}}\|\tilde{H}_{n,i}\|_{P_{*},2}

\displaystyle\leq 2K^{2}\max_{1\leq i\leq K_{0}}\left\|\widehat{\pi}_{a,-i}(\mathbf{X})-{\pi}_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})\right\|_{P_{*},2}(\|Y^{a}\|_{P_{*},2}+\|h\|_{P_{*},2})

\displaystyle\qquad+(K+1)\max_{1\leq i\leq K_{0}}\left\|\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\left|\widehat{\phi}_{a,-i}(s|\mathbf{x})-\phi_{a,\infty}(s|\mathbf{x})\right|ds\right\|_{P_{*},2}\rightarrow 0.

This completes the proof.
∎

This is an extension of the Lemma 4 of
Westling et al. (2024)
.

Lemma 19
.

Assume that a fixed function
f
satisfies
0\leq f_{s}(x)\leq f_{t}(x)
for all
s\leq t
and
x\in\mathcal{X}
, and
F:=f_{0}
satisfies
F\in L_{2}(P)
.
Then
N_{[]}(\epsilon\|F\|_{P,2},\mathcal{F},L_{2}(P))\leq 4/\epsilon^{2}
for all
\epsilon\in(0,1]
, where
\mathcal{F}:=\{x\mapsto f_{t}(x):t\in(-\infty,0]\}
.

Proof.

When
f_{0}=0
, then there is nothing to show.
Hence, without loss of generality, we assume
\|F\|_{P,2}>0
.
We denote
f_{-\infty}=0
, and
h(s)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}f_{s}(x)^{2}dP(x)
which is a monotone function from
(-\infty,0]
to
[0,1]
.
And, we have
P(f_{t}-f_{s})^{2}\leq Pf_{t}^{2}-Pf_{s}^{2}=h(t)-h(s)
, for all
-\infty<s\leq t\leq 0
.
For a given
\epsilon\in(0,1]
, we define
\eta=\epsilon\|F\|_{P,2}
.
We follow the same procedure used in the proof of Lemma 4 in
Westling et al. (2024)
to construct a set of
\eta
-brackets (in
L_{2}(P)
) which cover
\mathcal{F}
.
We denote
t_{0}=-\infty
and
\ell_{1}=f_{-\infty}=0
.
Then we define
t_{1}:=\sup\{t\leq 0:h(t)-h(-\infty)\leq\eta^{2}\}
, where
h(-\infty)=0
.
It implies
h(t_{1}-)\leq\eta^{2}
and
h(t_{1}+)\geq\eta^{2}
.
If
h(t_{1})\leq\eta^{2}
, then we define
I_{1}=(-\infty,t_{1}]
and
u_{1}:=f_{t_{1}}
.
On the other hand, if
h(t_{1})>\eta^{2}
, we define
I_{1}=(-\infty,t_{1})
and
u_{1}:x\mapsto sup_{t<t_{1}}f_{t}(x)
. Then, the bracket
[\ell_{1},u_{1}]
covers
\{f_{t}:t\in I_{1}\}
and satisfies
P(u_{1}-\ell_{1})^{2}\leq\eta^{2}
.
The rest of the proof follows exactly same steps in the proof of aforementioned Lemma 4 of
Westling et al. (2024)
, except that we define
r_{1}:=h(t_{1})
when
I_{1}=(-\infty,t_{1})
, and we define
r_{1}:=h(t_{1}+)
when
I_{1}=(-\infty,t_{1}]
.
We refer the readers to the proof therein for detailed derivation.
∎

D.8
Proof of Theorem
5

Proof.

The only differences compared with the proof of Theorem
2
arise from Lemma
11
,
16
and the limiting behavior of
\widehat{G}^{\rm locmod}_{a,n}
that is defined in Lemma
14
.
We start by checking the conclusion of Lemma
11
still holds, namely,

\displaystyle\tau^{+}_{n}-\tau^{-}_{n}=O_{p}(n^{-1/(2k+1)}),

where
\tau^{+}_{n},\tau^{-}_{n}
are estimator knots as in Lemma
11
.
We first show

\displaystyle\indent\left|\frac{\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-}}{4}\Big[(\widehat{F}^{K_{0}}_{a,n}-F_{a})(\tau_{n}^{+})-(\widehat{F}^{K_{0}}_{a,n}-F_{a})(\tau_{n}^{-})\Big]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon(\tau^{+}_{n}-\tau^{-}_{n})^{k+2}+(\tau^{+}_{n}-\tau^{-}_{n})O_{p}(n^{-(k+1)/(2k+1)})+(\tau^{+}_{n}-\tau^{-}_{n})^{2}o_{p}(n^{-k/(2k+1)}),

for sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
.

Since we verified the following decomposition,

\begin{split}&\widehat{F}^{K_{0}}_{a,n}(s)-F_{a}(s)\\
&=\frac{1}{K_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{K_{0}}\left[({\mathbb{P}}^{i}_{n}-P_{*})\left\{D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a,-i}}(s)-D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(s)\right\}\right]+\frac{1}{K_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{K_{0}}\left[P_{*}\left\{D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a,-i}}(s)-D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(s)\right\}\right]\\
&\qquad+({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(s)\end{split}

(138)

in the proof of Theorem
1
, this yields

\displaystyle\left(\widehat{F}^{K_{0}}_{a,n}(\tau_{n}^{+})-F_{a}(\tau_{n}^{+})\right)-\left(\widehat{F}^{K_{0}}_{a,n}(\tau_{n}^{-})-F_{a}(\tau_{n}^{-})\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{K_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{K_{0}}\left[({\mathbb{P}}^{i}_{n}-P_{*})\left\{\left(D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a,-i}}(\tau_{n}^{+})-D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(\tau_{n}^{+})\right)-\left(D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a,-i}}(\tau_{n}^{-})-D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(\tau_{n}^{-})\right)\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\indent+\frac{1}{K_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{K_{0}}\left[P_{*}\left\{\left(D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a,-i}}(\tau_{n}^{+})-D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(\tau_{n}^{+})\right)-\left(D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a,-i}}(\tau_{n}^{-})-D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(\tau_{n}^{-})\right)\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\indent+({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})\left[D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(\tau_{n}^{+})-D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(\tau_{n}^{-})\right]

\displaystyle=:R_{5n}+R_{6n}+R_{7n},

where
D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a,-i}}
is defined in the proof of Theorem
4
.
The terms
R_{6n}
and
R_{7n}
do not involve empirical processes indexed by the nuisance functions, and so their analysis, which we present briefly next, is similar to the analogous analysis done previously in the proof of Theorem
2
.

By similar reasoning to
({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a}}
in (
70
) and (
71
) with Assumption
(E8)
, we have

\begin{split}(\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})\left|R_{7n}\right|&=(\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})\left|({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})\left[\frac{I(A=a)}{\pi_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}I(\tau_{n}^{-}<Y\leq\tau_{n}^{+})\right.\right.\\
&\indent+\left.\left.(\phi_{a,\infty}(\tau_{n}^{+}|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{a,\infty}(\tau_{n}^{-}|\mathbf{X}))\Big(1-\frac{I(A=a)}{\pi_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\Big)\right]\right|\\
&\leq\epsilon(\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})^{k+2}+(\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})O_{p}\Big(n^{-(k+1)/(2k+1)}\Big),\end{split}

(139)

for sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
.
By relying on similar steps to check terms (
74
)–(
76
) in the proof of Lemma
11
, one can easily prove

\displaystyle(\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})|R_{6n}|\leq(\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})^{2}o_{p}(n^{-k/(2k+1)}),

since
K_{0}=O_{p}(1)
.

Now we consider
R_{5n}
.
By applying the following decomposition,

\begin{split}&\Big((D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a,-i}}(s_{2})-D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a,-i}}(s_{1}))-(D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(s_{2})-D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(s_{1}))\Big)\\
&=\Big(\frac{1}{\widehat{\pi}_{a,-i}(\mathbf{X})}-\frac{1}{\pi_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})}\Big)I(A=a)\Big(I(s_{1}<Y\leq s_{2})-(\phi_{a,\infty}(s_{2}|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{a,\infty}(s_{1}|\mathbf{X}))\Big)\\
&\indent+\Big(1-\frac{I(A=a)}{\widehat{\pi}_{a,-i}(\mathbf{X})}\Big)\Big((\widehat{\phi}_{a,-i}(s_{2}|\mathbf{X})-\widehat{\phi}_{a,-i}(s_{1}|\mathbf{X})-(\phi_{a,\infty}(s_{2}|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{a,\infty}(s_{1}|\mathbf{X}))\Big),\end{split}

(140)

for
s_{0}\in[s_{1},s_{2}]
, and further
by Lemma C.3 in
Kim et al. (2018)
(see Lemma
5
) and Assumption
(I4)
,
(E2)
,
(E0′)
,
(E6′)
(recall that the
L_{2}(P_{*})
norm is denoted by
\|\cdot\|
), we have

\begin{split}&\sqrt{n}P_{*}\left|(\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})^{-1}R_{5n}\right|\\
&\asymp\sqrt{N}P_{*}\left|(\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})^{-1}R_{5n}\right|\\
&\leq\max_{i}P_{*}\left|(\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})^{-1}{\mathbb{G}}^{i}_{n}\Big((D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a,-i}}(\tau_{n}^{+})-D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a,-i}}(\tau_{n}^{-}))-(D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(\tau_{n}^{+})-D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(\tau_{n}^{-}))\Big)\right|\\
&\leq\max_{i}\Big\{K^{2}\|\widehat{\pi}_{a,-i}(\mathbf{X})-\pi_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})\|\\
&\qquad\qquad\times(\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})^{-1}\left(F_{a}(\tau_{n}^{+})-F_{a}(\tau_{n}^{-})+\|\phi_{a,\infty}(\tau_{n}^{+}|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{a,\infty}(\tau_{n}^{-}|\mathbf{X})\|\right)\\
&\indent+(K+1)\|(\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})^{-1}(\widehat{\phi}_{a,-i}(\tau_{n}^{+}|\mathbf{X})-\widehat{\phi}_{a,-i}(\tau_{n}^{-}|\mathbf{X})-(\phi_{a,\infty}(\tau_{n}^{+}|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{a,\infty}(\tau_{n}^{-}|\mathbf{X}))\|\Big\}\\
&\indent\rightarrow 0,\end{split}

by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, Assumptions
(E5)
,
(R1)
,
(E6′)
,
(\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})=o_{p}(1)
and
K_{0}=O_{p}(1)
. This implies that

\displaystyle(\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})|R_{5n}|=(\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})^{2}o_{p}(n^{-1/2}),

which is further
(\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-})^{2}O_{p}(n^{-k/(2k+1)})
.
Furthermore, since the conclusion for the other term

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\bar{\tau}}^{\tau_{n}^{+}}\left[(\widehat{F}^{K_{0}}_{a,n}-F_{a})(y)-(\widehat{F}^{K_{0}}_{a,n}-F_{a})(2\bar{\tau}-y)\right]dy\right|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\bar{\tau}}^{\tau_{n}^{+}}\left|(\widehat{F}^{K_{0}}_{a,n}-F_{a})(y)-(\widehat{F}^{K_{0}}_{a,n}-F_{a})(2\bar{\tau}-y)\right|dy

\displaystyle\asymp\left|\left(\tau_{n}^{+}-\tau_{n}^{-}\right)\left[(\widehat{F}^{K_{0}}_{a,n}-F_{a})(\tau_{n}^{+})-(\widehat{F}^{K_{0}}_{a,n}-F_{a})(\tau_{n}^{-})\right]\right|

follows by the same reasoning to (
73
), (
78
), and the preceding derivation above,
thus we have shown that the conclusion of Lemma
11
still holds (i.e.,
\tau^{+}_{n}-\tau^{-}_{n}=O_{p}(n^{-1/(2k+1)})
).

Next, we verify that the conclusion of Lemma
16
holds (in the sample splitting setting). It suffices to check that the term (
102
)–(
103
) (for the sample splitting estimator
\widehat{F}_{a,n}
) is
o_{p}(n^{-(k+1)/(2k+1)})
(the terms (
104
)–(
105
)
are unchanged). Indeed, by exploiting the decomposition (
138
) again, we have

\displaystyle\Big[\Big(\widehat{F}^{K_{0}}_{a,n}(t^{\prime})-F_{a}(t^{\prime})\Big)-\Big(\widehat{F}^{K_{0}}_{a,n}(t)-F_{a}(t)\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{K_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{K_{0}}\left[({\mathbb{P}}^{i}_{n}-P_{*})\left((D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a,-i}}-D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}})(t^{\prime})-(D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a,-i}}-D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}})(t)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{K_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{K_{0}}\left[P_{*}\left((D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a,-i}}-D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}})(t^{\prime})-(D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a,-i}}-D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}})(t)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\indent+({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})\left[D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(t^{\prime})-D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle:=R_{8n}+R_{9n}+R_{10n}.

Handling the terms
R_{9n}
and
R_{10n}
is done quite similarly as in Lemma
16
;
R_{8n}
requires some modifications. To check
R_{10n}
, following the steps used to prove (
109
) in the proof of Lemma
16
, it can be easily shown that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathcal{W}{~\rm and~}t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{t}^{K_{0}}}\Big|

\displaystyle({\mathbb{P}}_{n}-P_{*})\Big(D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(t^{\prime})-D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(t)\Big)\Big|=o_{p}(n^{-(k+1)/(2k+1)}),

where
\mathcal{W}:=\mathcal{S}_{n}\cap\mathcal{E}_{s_{0},n}
,
\mathcal{I}^{K_{0}}_{v}:=[v-\kappa^{K_{0}}_{n},v+\kappa^{K_{0}}_{n}]
in which
\kappa^{K_{0}}_{n}
is defined as

\displaystyle\kappa^{K_{0}}_{n}:=\sup\{|t-s|:t,s\in[L_{n},U_{n}],s\leq t,\widehat{F}^{K_{0}}_{a,n}(t)\leq\widehat{F}^{K_{0}}_{a,n}(s)\},

for any
v\in I_{s_{0},\omega}
, and
I_{s_{0},\omega}
,
\mathcal{E}_{s_{0},n}
are defined in Assumption
(R1)
, Lemma
16
, respectively.
For
R_{9n}
, one can check the following,

\begin{split}&\sup_{t\in\mathcal{W}{~\rm and~}t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{t}^{K_{0}}}\Bigg|\frac{1}{K_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{K_{0}}\left[P_{*}\left\{(D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a,-i}}-D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}})(t^{\prime})-(D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a,-i}}-D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}})(t)\right\}\right]\Bigg|\\
&\qquad=o_{p}(n^{-(k+1)/(2k+1)}),\end{split}

(141)

by similar reasoning to (
107
) in the proof of Lemma
16
, since
K_{0}=O_{p}(1)
.

We now check
R_{8n}
. Indeed, from the proof of Lemma
16
(see (
109
)) it suffices to show

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathcal{W}{~\rm and~}t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{t}^{K_{0}}}

\displaystyle\Bigg|\frac{1}{K_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{K_{0}}\left[({\mathbb{P}}^{i}_{n}-P_{*})\left\{(D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a,-i}}-D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}})(t^{\prime})-(D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a,-i}}-D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}})(t)\right\}\right]\Bigg|

\displaystyle=o_{p}(n^{-(k+1)/(2k+1)}).

Multiplying by
\sqrt{n}
, this is equivalent to showing (since
K_{0}=O_{p}(1)
) that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathcal{W}{~\rm and~}t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{t}^{K_{0}}}

\displaystyle\Bigg|\frac{1}{K_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{K_{0}}\left[{\mathbb{G}}_{n}^{i}\left\{(D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a,-i}}-D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}})(t^{\prime})-(D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a,-i}}-D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}})(t)\right\}\right]\Bigg|

\displaystyle=o_{p}(n^{-1/2(2k+1)}).

Indeed, by the tower property of expectation,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}^{1}_{n,-i}}\left|\mathbb{G}^{i}_{n}f\right|\right]={\mathbb{E}}\left\{{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}^{1}_{n,-i}}\left|\mathbb{G}^{i}_{n}f\right|\Big|\mathcal{T}_{n,i}\right]\right\},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{1}_{n,-i}:=\Bigg\{\Big(D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a,-i}}(t^{\prime})-D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(t^{\prime})

\displaystyle-(D_{a,\widehat{\theta}_{a,-i}}(t)-D_{a,\theta_{a,\infty}}(t)\Big)

\displaystyle:t\in[s_{0}-Tv_{n},s_{0}+Tv_{n}],\,|t^{\prime}-t|\leq\kappa_{n}\Bigg\},

which admits an envelope function

\displaystyle H^{1}_{n,i}(\mathbf{z})

(142)

\displaystyle=2K^{2}\sup_{(t_{1},t_{2})\in\mathcal{W}^{1}}\Bigg\{\left|\widehat{\pi}_{a,-i}(\mathbf{x})-\pi_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{x})\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\indent\times I(A=a)\left|I(t_{1}<Y\leq t_{2})-(\phi_{a,\infty}(t_{2}|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{a,\infty}(t_{1}|\mathbf{X}))\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\left|\left(\widehat{\phi}_{a,-i}(t_{2}|\mathbf{X})-\widehat{\phi}_{a,-i}(t_{2}|\mathbf{X})\right)-\left(\phi_{a,\infty}(t_{1}|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{a,\infty}(t_{1}|\mathbf{X})\right)\right|\Bigg\},

where
\mathcal{W}^{1}:\{(x,y):|y-s_{0}|\leq Tv_{n},\,|y-x|\leq\kappa_{n}^{K_{0}}\}
.
By Theorem 2.14.1 in
van der Vaart and Wellner (2023)
(see Proposition
3
), for sufficiently large
n
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[n^{1/2(2k+1)}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}^{1}_{n,-i}}\left|\mathbb{G}^{i}_{n}f\right|\Big|\mathcal{T}_{n,i}\right]\leq C_{*}n^{1/2(2k+1)}\|H^{1}_{n,i}\|_{P_{*},2}J(1,\mathcal{F}^{1}_{n,-i}).

Similarly to
J(1,\mathcal{F}_{n-i})
in the proof of Theorem
4
, since here
\{Y\mapsto I(t_{1}<Y\leq t_{2}):(t_{1},t_{2})\in\mathcal{W}^{1}\}
is VC, we have uniformly bounded
J(1,\mathcal{F}^{1}_{n,-i})
for all
n
and
-i
. Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle n^{1/2(2k+1)}\max_{i}\|H^{1}_{n,i}\|_{P_{*},2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\max_{i}\Bigg\{\|\widehat{\pi}_{a,-i}(\mathbf{X})-{\pi}_{a,\infty}(\mathbf{X})\|_{P_{*},2}

\displaystyle\qquad\times n^{1/2(2k+1)}\left\|\sup_{(t_{1},t_{2})\in\mathcal{W}^{1}}\Big[(\phi_{a,*}(t_{2}|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{a,*}(t_{1}|\mathbf{X}))-(\phi_{a,\infty}(t_{2}|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{a,\infty}(t_{1}|\mathbf{X}))\Big]\right\|_{P_{*},2}

\displaystyle\indent+n^{1/2(2k+1)}\left\|\sup_{(t_{1},t_{2})\in\mathcal{W}^{1}}\Big[(\widehat{\phi}_{a,-i}(t_{2}|\mathbf{X})-\widehat{\phi}_{a,-i}(t_{2}|\mathbf{X}))-(\phi_{a,\infty}(t_{1}|\mathbf{X})-\phi_{a,\infty}(t_{1}|\mathbf{X}))\Big]\right\|_{P_{*},2}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\qquad\rightarrow 0,

since Assumption
(E6′)
holds, and one can check
\kappa_{n}^{K_{0}}=O_{p}(n^{-(k+1)/(2k+1)})
following the same reasoning in the proof of Lemma
12
. Hence we have shown that the conclusion of Lemma
16
continues to hold in the sample splitting setting.

Lastly, we check that the term
\widehat{G}^{\rm locmod}_{a,n}
has the same asymptotics as in the non sample splitting case. Since it was already shown that

\displaystyle\widehat{G}^{\rm locmod}_{a,n}(t)
